topic: pyforge-steward (Steward) CLI — hexagonal wrap-never-reimplement platform/ops station: keys, deploy, provision, budget
updated: 2026-10-21T12:06

- (event) Surface reconcile 2026-09-03 (fleet hygiene): post-#1038 `pixi lock` and already-landed Epics 42–43 moved governed paths without this memlog moving. Accept the current tree as the post-land surface; no new product work. Scoped-stamped: `--write-baseline --spec pyforge-steward/spec-pyforge-steward`.


- (direction) Distillation, not re-planning: dream+brief+prd+epics is an already-approved, fully-decomposed chain (18 FRs across 4 duties, 9 ADs, 4 epics); capability IDs map to PRD §4 feature groups (4.1 keys, 4.2 deploy, 4.3 provision, 4.4 budget), constraints carry architecture ADs 1-9 + key NFRs, non-goals from PRD §5, success signal from PRD §7. Architecture spine adopted as companion (diagram+layer table+stack+structural seed). Notably clean chain: all 3 of PRD's own §9 open questions were resolved down at the architecture stage (Consistency Conventions table) -- captured as constraints, not open questions.
- (capability) CAP-1 (Keys, PRD §4.1/FR-1-7): intent - operator can issue, scope, rotate, audit, inventory, and record revocation of credentials through one CLI (steward keys), with the historical JFROG_API_KEY cross-host leak pattern closed as a named automated regression test. success - a credential declared with an explicit host allowlist is never attached to a request outside it; encrypt/decrypt round-trips a fixture exactly via age; rotate re-encrypts every secret under a newly generated identity and the old identity fails to decrypt afterward, with no calendar/cron auto-rotation path; audit --drift reports the historical unconditional-injection pattern against a fixture and reports clean against today's fixed _http.py; list enumerates identities with scope/last-rotated metadata and never prints a raw secret value under any flag; revoke marks an identity retired and prints manual remediation guidance with zero third-party API calls; a dedicated conformance test fails loudly if the host-gating logic is ever removed or bypassed.
- (capability) CAP-2 (Deploy, PRD §4.2/FR-8-11): intent - operator can build and publish the Pages dashboard through one reconciled command (steward deploy dashboard) instead of hand-running dashboard-gen + git push. success - running twice with no source change between runs results in zero commits on the second run; a real change to the generated output results in exactly one new commit containing exactly the changed files, pushed to the existing Pages-serving branch; --dry-run performs the build+diff and prints it without committing or pushing (git state unchanged); status reports the last successful deploy's commit SHA and timestamp read from Git history alone, with no separate state store.
- (capability) CAP-3 (Provision, PRD §4.3/FR-12-15): intent - operator or an unattended bmad-loop session can materialize any named pixi environment or a bmad-loop runner+environment together, list what exists, and verify the environment.yaml sync gate, without recalling raw pixi/bmad-loop-worktree syntax. success - a valid environment name materializes via 'pixi install -e'; an invalid name reports a clear error listing valid names rather than surfacing pixi's raw error; --runner bmad-loop --env <name> wraps scripts/bmad-loop-worktree to materialize a worktree plus its named environment in one call, surfacing underlying failures clearly rather than leaving orphaned state unreported; --list enumerates every pixi.toml [environments] entry with its composing features (plus --json); --verify wraps the existing environment.yaml<->pixi.toml sync-gate check and reports drift with a non-zero exit when out of sync.
- (capability) CAP-4 (Budget, PRD §4.4/FR-16-18): intent - operator can declare and query a machine-readable resource ceiling and get an honest signal about spend rather than a fabricated number. success - 'budget set --cap <amount><currency>/<period>' records a stable, documented schema and rejects a malformed cap value without writing a corrupt entry; 'budget show' prints the declared ceiling(s) in human and --json form, and reports clearly (not a crash or misleading zero) when none is declared; 'budget check' returns one of three distinct exit codes -- not-configured / under-budget / over-budget -- never collapsing 'no data' into a pass, with no cloud-cost-SDK or Kubecost/OpenCost/Infracost client import anywhere in the codebase.
- (constraint) AD-1 (wrap, never reimplement): every duty adapter's implementation is a subprocess call or a thin import into an existing tool (_http.py, age, pixi, git, gh, scripts/bmad-loop-worktree). A duty module containing its own copy of logic that already exists elsewhere in this repo is a review-blocking finding, not a style nit.
- (constraint) AD-2 (keys' single chokepoint): steward.keys's host-scoping resolver imports and delegates to _http.py's existing auth_headers_for(url, skip_auth=...) pattern for anything HTTP-shaped; no duty module constructs its own request carrying ambient auth headers. Host-allowlist configuration reads _http.py's existing resolve_*_urls/*_BASE_URL table directly -- no duplicate Steward-owned URL config (resolves PRD open question on host-allowlist config source).
- (constraint) AD-3 (age-encrypted at rest, no standing service): age/age-keygen are required external pixi run-dependencies, never vendored or reimplemented. steward keys encrypt/decrypt/rotate shell out to the age CLI; no Vault/Infisical-class secrets-manager server enters the dependency graph.
- (constraint) AD-4 (deploy is CLI-invoked reconciliation only): steward deploy dashboard computes a diff (freshly-built docs/dashboard/ output vs. the currently committed tree) before any git mutation; a no-diff run performs zero commits. No daemon, scheduler, or new GitHub Actions workflow ships in v1 -- the operator or an existing workflow invokes the CLI directly against the existing Pages-serving branch.
- (constraint) AD-5 (provision wraps, never forks, Marshal-owned machinery): steward provision subcommands shell out to 'pixi install -e <name>' and scripts/bmad-loop-worktree verbatim. Steward's own code reads pixi.toml's [environments] table for inventory but never writes to it and never re-implements pixi's dependency-resolution logic.
- (constraint) AD-6 (budget v1 is declared-not-enforced): steward budget check returns one of three distinct exit codes -- not-configured, under-budget, over-budget -- never collapsing 'no data' into a pass. No cost-integration import (cloud SDK, Kubecost/OpenCost client) exists in the codebase until a future story adds a real metered spend source.
- (constraint) AD-7 (one shared Duty protocol; missing duty degrades, never crashes): every duty module exposes a Duty-protocol-conforming object (name, run(ns) -> DutyResult); cli.py dispatches through the protocol only. A duty not yet implemented for a given subcommand returns a DutyResult carrying an explicit 'not implemented' status rather than raising.
- (constraint) AD-8 (exit-code sole ownership): main() is the only place that returns a process exit code -- it catches KeyboardInterrupt (fixed SIGINT exit), SystemExit raised inside a duty (projected as an internal error, never trusted verbatim), and any other Exception (fixed internal-error exit, never the bare interpreter default 1). A duty module never calls sys.exit directly.
- (constraint) AD-9 (enterprise routing inherited, never duplicated): any new outbound HTTP endpoint Steward introduces is added as one new row to _http.py's existing resolve_*_urls convention (env-var override, no committed URL/credential) -- never a parallel Steward-specific config mechanism. Steward's air-gap posture is the same posture every other pyforge tool has.
- (constraint) Credential provenance convention: steward keys list entries carry a provenance field, 'issued' (an age identity Steward itself minted) or 'observed' (a pre-existing repo credential Steward's audit discovered but did not create, e.g. GITHUB_TOKEN, JFROG_API_KEY). Both are listed for drift-audit visibility; only 'issued' entries are rotatable via steward keys rotate in v1 -- an observed entry's rotation is external.
- (constraint) Credential values are never printed: steward keys list/audit output never contains a raw secret value under any flag combination -- enforced by a dedicated meta-tier invariant test, mirroring pyforge-warden's own invariant-test convention.
- (constraint) Provision's pixi invocations are explicit, user-triggered CLI actions only, never an import-time or build-time side effect -- matches pyforge-warden's 'pixi is a build/dev-env floor, never a runtime dependency' precedent.
- (constraint) Config file location: repo-root .steward/ dotdir, tracked (not gitignored, not under _bmad-output/) -- budget.yaml (FR-16 ceiling), keys-inventory.yaml (FR-5 credential metadata, never a secret value), and *.age encrypted payloads all live here, surviving a bmad-switch to a different active BMAD project (resolves PRD open questions on budget-config location and inventory-scope: a durable operational fact does not belong under a per-project planning-artifacts tree).
- (decision) Non-goal: no standing secrets-manager service (Vault/Infisical/OpenBao-class server) -- keys is a CLI + encrypted files only.
- (decision) Non-goal: no Backstage-class software catalog or scaffolder platform -- provision is a CLI face over pixi, not a new IDP.
- (decision) Non-goal: no ArgoCD/Flux-class GitOps control plane -- deploy's reconciliation is CLI-invoked, not a standing controller.
- (decision) Non-goal: no Kubecost/OpenCost/Infracost-class cost-allocation integration in v1 -- no live cloud spend exists to allocate against yet.
- (decision) Non-goal: no third-party credential-revocation API integration (JFrog, GitHub, Anthropic) -- keys revoke records intent and guides manual remediation, it does not call provider APIs.
- (decision) Non-goal: no presenton-pixi-image on OpenShift or air-gap bundle install support in v1 -- named in the Dream as Steward's eventual territory, explicitly deferred; deploy's v1 scope is the Pages dashboard only.
- (decision) Non-goal: no multi-operator / team features -- Steward is single-operator dogfooded tooling, not a product for external users.
- (decision) Non-goal: no calendar-based automatic key rotation scheduler -- rotation in v1 is on-demand/risk-triggered, per current credential-lifecycle best practice.
- (decision) Success signal (PRD §7): SM-1 the JFROG_API_KEY cross-host leak pattern has a passing, named regression test in Steward's conformance suite. SM-2 steward deploy dashboard replaces 100% of manual dashboard-gen + push invocations going forward. SM-3 steward provision --env <name> succeeds for every entry in pixi.toml's [environments] table without the operator consulting raw pixi syntax. SM-4 steward budget show is queried at least once after any Dream/spec update that touches the ceiling doctrine, replacing a Dream-file grep. Counter-metrics (do not optimize): SM-C1 number of duties/features shipped is not optimized -- budget growing Kubecost-scale before real spend exists is over-building against this Spec's own non-goals. SM-C2 key rotation frequency is not optimized as a vanity metric -- more rotations is not better.
- (assumption) Steward has exactly one user (this repo's maintainer, acting through every other pyforge persona and every bmad-loop session); no team/multi-tenant surface -- not independently re-validated with the user this session.
- (assumption) Third-party credential-revocation API integration is out of scope for v1 because Steward has no existing credential to call such APIs with today -- inferred from the repo's current tooling, not confirmed by the user.
- (assumption) The '1500usd/month' figure in FR-16's example is illustrative, carried verbatim from the Dream's own CLI cadence example -- the actual ceiling value is a config input, not a Spec claim about real spend.
- (assumption) age-based at-rest encryption (constraint AD-3) is the PRD's own lowest-confidence decision (medium confidence, flagged for architecture confirmation) -- assumed durable only while Steward's secret inventory stays a handful of API keys; revisit if it grows materially.
- (assumption) Direct-push deploy with no new GitHub Actions workflow (constraint AD-4) is a medium-confidence PRD decision -- assumed sufficient until scheduled/push-button deploy automation becomes a real want, at which point it should be revisited, not silently reversed.
- (event) No spec-level open_questions[] carried: all 3 of PRD §9's own open questions (credential-inventory scope, host-allowlist config source, budget-config location) were explicitly resolved by the architecture's Consistency Conventions table and are folded into Constraints above (provenance convention; AD-2's _http.py-table reuse; the .steward/ location rule). Epics.md's own 2 leftover items (exact YAML schema field names; static-pattern-match vs AST-based drift detection) are HOW-level implementation choices explicitly framed by epics itself as story-level decisions, not spec-worthy WHAT-level gaps -- omitted per Spec Law rule 2. D2 (age encryption) and D6 (direct-push deploy) are the PRD's own flagged medium-confidence decisions -- carried as Assumptions with their revisit trigger stated, not as open questions, since a decision was actually made.

- (note) Surface declared 2026-07-25: src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/** — the package this Spec will build (not yet created), matching the marshal/mason convention.
- (event) Story 1.1 SHIPPED 2026-07-25: src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/ — installable CLI, argparse dispatcher with one subparser per duty (keys/deploy/provision/budget), the Duty Protocol + DutyResult dataclass (AD-7) with a NullDuty null-engine, and main() as sole owner of the exit code (AD-8). Task names mirror pyforge-warden's verbatim per the AC. 19 tests across the three tiers (unit/conformance/meta).
- (constraint) Exit codes are PROJECTED, never inherited (AD-8): 0 ok / 1 a duty ran and reported ok=False / 2 usage / 70 internal error / 130 interrupted. A crash must never surface as 1, because an undocumented 1 is indistinguishable from a duty that legitimately failed — the exact false signal a gate must not emit. Pinned by a test asserting rc != EXIT_FAILED on a crash, and by a meta-test that AST-parses every non-cli module for sys.exit() calls (the first version substring-matched and false-positived on a docstring).

- (change by claude) Story 14.8 review-fix pass, 2026-09-06 (same branch): `upgrade.py` — CAP-8's skill scan now excludes `__pycache__`/`*.pyc`/`*.pyo` bytecode noise (`_is_pycache_noise`), confirmed live to have caused 2 false-positive findings; `_skill_customization_findings` gains the same `exclude: frozenset[str]` parameter `_scripts_customization_findings` already had (the "no file covered by both mechanisms" boundary now holds for skills too); `LocalCustomizationFinding.reason` no longer embeds the resolved absolute `installed_package_root`/packaged `Path` (machine-independent report text); `build_preflight_report`'s scan-skipped note now also fires when an explicit `--installed-package-root` resolves to a non-directory (previously only noted when unset with no cache match); `reconcile_clobbered_custom_surfaces` tracks delta-replay conflicts locally and ANDs them into `all_clear` (a marker-based delta-replay conflict — e.g. on `resolve_config.py` — now correctly fails `ReconcileReport.all_clear` / `UpgradeDuty.ok`, closing a gap where CAP-8's general reapply path gated on conflict but this CAP-3 amendment did not), and notes when a delta-replay is skipped because a matching packaged file is missing on one side; `reapply_local_customizations` gains a `skipped_installer_removed_file` action so a clean three-way merge can never resurrect a file the core installer intentionally deleted. `failure-modes.md` trap 16's self-contradictory "found all 9... not genuine edits" wording corrected. - (change by claude) Story 14.8 follow-up review pass, 2026-09-06 (same branch; bmad-build-auto's single allowed follow-up, spec's followup_review_recommended flag now false): a four-layer review (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Intent Alignment) re-examined the CAP-8 three-way-merge/reapply machinery in upgrade.py, checking each finding against the prior pass's 24-row triage log first (6 findings carried forward on refutations already on record, none re-patched). 27 new findings; 8 patched: _three_way_merge's git merge-file now passes -L labels (repo customization / old upstream (installed) / new upstream (target)) instead of leaking ephemeral temp-dir paths into a .customization-conflict sibling; reapply_local_customizations clears a stale .customization-conflict sibling from an earlier failed apply before deciding this run's outcome, so a file that merges cleanly now never keeps looking conflicted; default_installed_package_root wraps its body in try/except (OSError, RuntimeError) so Path.home() failing can no longer violate its own never-raises contract; a new _looks_like_bmad_method_package check (build_preflight_report / format_preflight) means an --installed-package-root that is a real, existing, but wrong-shaped directory (missing src/bmm-skills, src/core-skills, and src/scripts) is now noted as scan-skipped instead of silently reading as a verified-clean scan -- the exact trap-16 false-confidence CAP-8 exists to prevent. Tests: test_upgrade_preflight.py +1 (wrong-shape root); test_upgrade_apply.py +3 (skipped_no_package_match's actually-reachable new-side-missing branch; a nested 2-level skill-file path through the real three-way-merge, matching the motivating incident's file shape; stale-conflict-sibling cleanup) plus meaningful-label assertions added to the existing 3-file/2-clean/1-conflict test; test_upgrade_reconcile.py +1 (the CAP-3 delta-replay-conflict all_clear gate proven through the real apply_bmad_core_upgrade path, not only a direct reconcile_clobbered_custom_surfaces call -- _fake_installer gains a clobber_body param). 124/124 upgrade-suite tests (was 119); mypy stays at the pre-existing 17; ruff I001/F clean. 2 deferred (already-accepted 14.6/14.7 pattern: real-installer round-trip untested; CAP-3 delta-replay's non-gating skipped_installer_removed_file precedent unchanged), 17 rejected (see spec's own Review Triage Log for the full breakdown, 6 of them carried). followup_review_recommended forced false at HALT per the skill's own single-follow-up contract. A pre-existing uncommitted cli.py help-text touch (clarifying --package-root is also required for reapply) found in the working tree at session start was reverted rather than paid for with two foreign-spec reconciles (spec-scratch-worktree-lifecycle / spec-bmad-suite-install-class-wiring both name cli.py verbatim) -- same tradeoff the Story 14.6 entry above already made for an identical situation. - (change by claude) Surface reconcile 2026-09-14 (steward Story 53.6, spec-intelligence-hub CAP-2 -- full entry lives in that Spec's memlog): `frames.py` re-keyed the in-repo Frame preflight from `name` onto `identifier`. Frame Spec v0.3 §4.2.1 makes `identifier` the mandatory identity element and SHOULDs a `qualified-ref` (`publisher "/" frame-name`, §5.3), while the element profile marks `title` -- which the Markdown key `name` aliases -- as MUST NOT be slug-constrained. Keying preflight off `name` was therefore keying it off the label rather than the identity, and off a string that was simultaneously the Frame key and the Python distribution name (`pyforge-steward`). Now `COMPANY_IDENTIFIER = "pyforge/company"` + `PUBLISHER = "pyforge"`; REQUIRED_FIELDS gained `identifier` and swapped the unregistered `owner` for the registered repeatable `maintainer`; new `scalar-repeatable` finding enforces §6.2.1's "A writer MUST emit a sequence" for `maintainer`/`inherits`; `unexpected-name` became `unexpected-identifier`; `path-ref` parent resolution unchanged. The `name`/`inherits` KEYS are deliberately kept -- §6.2.1 requires those aliases of a Markdown writer, and `title:`/`composition:` belong to the YAML/JSON encodings only. Tests 13 -> 21. Full steward suite green; all 8 stations green. RECONCILES: src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/frames.py, src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_frame_preflight.py. Scoped-stamped.

- (direction) 2026-09-16 operator: Frames adoption is on the critical path to the intelligence hub, the unifying strategy and launching pyforge-foundry; the two PRs we grounded on (openteams-ai/frame-spec#28, #29) have had edits — analyze and replan. We continue to operate as if the latest heads become v0.3. NO commits or comments to openteams-ai repositories.
- (capability) CAP-6 — Frame draft re-grounding: the estate claims conformance the way the draft now asks. intent: (a) the nine Frames drop the invented version token — bare 'type: frame' (draft §6.2.1 09-14: a document written to the draft omits the token until a release assigns one); (b) docs/foundry/frames/README.md carries an 'Upstream pin' block naming the #28 and #29 head SHAs we conform to, re-pinned only with a memlog line; (c) an opt-in steward task 'frame-upstream-check' fetches openteams-ai/frame-spec at the pinned #29 SHA into a temp dir and runs its validate_frame.py over docs/foundry/frames/ (and --check-profile over our profile) — never vendored, never a gate, never a write upstream; (d) PyForge publishes its conformance profile docs/foundry/frames/conformance-profile.yaml (§7 MUST; #29 YAML shape; specification draft-mcandrew-frame-spec-00): reads markdown, writes none, resolves no composition, treats visibility as declared intent, and — §9 — declares that no trust configuration exists yet so every reachable source is trusted, which is exactly the state the future loader must change. success: upstream validate_frame.py at the pinned SHA reports 9/9 OK and --check-profile accepts the profile; in-repo frame-preflight stays green; the deferred-work accepted-risk entry on #28 carries today's date and the two SHAs.
- (constraint) CAP-6: §9 Security Considerations (record source beside content; MUST NOT load from an untrusted source; trust-every-source declared, never inferred; composed set made visible before loading; identifier is asserted, not authenticated) are acceptance criteria for any story that loads a Frame into an agent's context (marshal-token-economy [context.wire]); no such loader exists yet, and none may land without them. #29's spec/fixtures/composition are the conformance fixtures for a composing reader.
- (decision) 2026-09-16 status shipped -> ready: a station Spec is perpetually ready (spec-one-chain-per-station CAP-6, CHAIN-STANDARD § 4); 'shipped' was the 08-15 bookkeeping flip for CAP-1..4 and is now false with CAP-5/CAP-6 open. CAP-1..4 keep their verified lines. SPEC.md re-derived from this log (CAP-5, CAP-6, two constraints appended; nothing above them edited).
- (decision) 2026-09-16 CAP-5 corrected (operator question: does the minimal env drive marshal token efficiency by default?): the task-import method gave a functional floor only; the token-economy kit leaves no trace in task commands because marshal reaches it by shutil.which / skill-file detection and degrades silently when absent (MRS-DISP-033 WARN, the 2026-09-01 spend-scare shape). Probed: lean pyforge-marshal env has bmad-loop 0.11.1 + tmux but NO headroom; caveman is absent from every env (it is a per-harness skill installed via npx skills add, so the env can only guarantee nodejs). CAP-5 now: pyforge-guild includes the python feature (nodejs, gh, bmad-method) and declares headroom-ai explicitly; success adds 'marshal seed check reports the headroom kit item present and a dispatch dry-run in pyforge-guild raises no MRS-DISP-033'; Epic 46 (marshal-token-economy CAP-19..24, all backlog) names pyforge-guild as the environment its stories are verified in -- the env is the precondition, 46.x is the mechanism.
- (change) 2026-09-16 Story 63.1 landed: feature.pyforge-guild (deps) + feature.guild-tasks (the 51 moved Guild tasks, NO deps) + environment pyforge-guild = python + pyforge-guild + guild-tasks, no-default-feature; local-recipes includes guild-tasks only. Two facts found at the first solve reshaped CAP-5's mechanism, not its intent: (1) a deps-carrying feature cannot be included by local-recipes because station packages range-pin engines to the verified minor (pyforge-warden osv-scanner >=2.4,<2.5; pyforge-marshal tomlkit >=0.13,<0.13.3) while the factory floats (>=2.5.1; >=0.15.1) -- so tasks and deps split into two features, one task name still in exactly one feature; (2) pyforge-warden is NOT in pyforge-guild: doctor does not require it at runtime (run-deps jsonschema/pyyaml/mcp/pyforge-core) and degrades the seam to cannot-evaluate, which is how detectors-ci has always run in local-recipes; the warden seam stays -e pyforge-doctor / -e pyforge-warden. tomli dropped (3.14 stdlib tomllib; llms-full-check flagged it undocumented). The nine adopted bmad-suite members added (~13 MB) because marshal seed check reports each as not installed. Measured: 859 MB on disk (local-recipes 10 GB); detectors-ci 32/32 green in pyforge-guild; headroom, node/npx, gh, git, uv, tmux, bmad-loop 0.11.1, marshal, steward all on PATH; local-recipes still resolves every moved task. marshal seed check residual FAILs are non-environmental: bmad-method-wds-expansion is referenced by the seed manifest but carried by no env (pre-existing; adoption-register item), .marshal/seed-state.yml never adopted (pre-existing), and the three context layers are declared OFF in policy -- Epic 46 (marshal-token-economy CAP-19..24) is the mechanism that flips them; this env is the floor it lands on. default-env: directive in pixi.toml now names pyforge-guild (scripts/load-env.sh reads it). pixi_env_matrix updated on the unifying-strategy Dream.
- (decision) 2026-09-16 (operator) pixi `default` environment = an ALIAS of pyforge-guild (identical feature list). Measured first: moving the 51 tasks into guild-tasks made a bare `pixi run <guild task>` AMBIGUOUS (pixi: provided by local-recipes, pyforge-guild) and bare `pixi shell` pointed at a non-existent default env; verified in a scratch workspace that pixi resolves an ambiguous task to the env NAMED default. So the Guild env is the default by the tool, not by a doc: bare `pixi run fleet-picture` runs in default, `pixi shell-hook` activates envs/default. Cost accepted: a second ~860 MB directory when both names are used. The rename alternative (env named default, no pyforge-guild) was declined -- the name is what every surface and CAP-5 carry.
- (decision) 2026-09-16 (operator) Tier-3 feed in a fresh clone: SEED BY COPYING THE TWIN. Verified in a fresh shallow clone: implementation-artifacts/ does not exist (it is a symlink to the primary checkout only in local worktrees), `sprint-ledger-sync --repair-feed` SKIPS a missing feed ('no Tier-3 feed at ...', NOTHING promoted), so a cloud agent could not land a ledger flip. The twin is written in the same shape as the feed (promote_sprint_status.py docstring), and `mkdir -p implementation-artifacts && cp planning-artifacts/sprint-status-ledger.yaml implementation-artifacts/sprint-status.yaml` then `sprint-ledger-sync` reports `unchanged` with a clean tree. This is the one sanctioned remedy; Story 63.4's session check prints it when the feed is absent, and every fold brief carries it as step 0. No code change to promote_sprint_status.py (marshal's) -- a procedure, not a mode.
- (change) 2026-09-16 Story 63.2 landed and Stories 63.3/63.4 minted (operator: 'you decide -- ready for the cloud/Cursor handoff'). 63.2: 21 guild-task invocations flipped -e local-recipes -> -e pyforge-guild in the LIVING guidance only (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, _bmad-output/PROJECTS.md, docs/dreams/README.md, docs/reference/README.md, docs/reference/library-llms-full.md); archived Dreams and legacy docs/specs keep their text as record. Mechanism surfaces, not prose: .cursor/environment.json force-tracked (Cursor Cloud Agents install pyforge-guild; .cursor/ is gitignored so -f), .claude/hooks/session-start.sh installs pyforge-guild instead of pyforge-doctor alone (Claude Code web), .github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml NEW (the Copilot coding agent sandbox had no pixi at all -- every documented command was dead), tests/test_load_env.sh read the live pixi.toml and went 7/11 red on the directive flip -> 11/11. pr-preflight's test-ci leg pinned to local-recipes (the CFE suite is a factory task; the first pr-preflight from the Guild env failed 'could not find the task test-ci'); pr-preflight from pyforge-guild then exit 0 (detectors-ci 32/32, CFE 9130 passed, 8 station suites + coverage gates). Enforcement survey recorded as 63.3 (one closed session_denials list in guild-roster.json + one hook script for Claude PreToolUse Bash/Edit/Write deny and Cursor beforeShellExecution deny; Gemini/Copilot CLI/Devin named instruction-only -- no verified deny surface) and 63.4 (pyforge steward session check: pixi/env/bmad-method/token kit/gh auth+rate limit/codegraph/Tier-3 feed/scribe reachability as findings, called identically from the four entry points). CI is already lean (pyforge-ci, detectors, per-station envs) -- not a gap; a later story may fold pyforge-ci and detectors into pyforge-guild (three lean envs doing the Guild's job is a one-name-one-job smell). 64.1/64.2 rows promoted to done in the same sync (merged in PR #1387 but left backlog -- the 'respawns on every drain' pitfall).
- (change) 2026-09-16 PR #1388 first CI red: scripts-suite (tests/scripts in -e pyforge-ci, detectors.yml) -- tests/scripts/test_deck_facts.py pinned bmad-groundtruth to cfg['feature']['local-recipes']['tasks'] (KeyError after the 63.1 move). Fixed to resolve the task through whichever ONE feature declares it (asserts exactly one). Same sweep: pyforge-steward tests/unit/test_guards.py anchored on '[feature.local-recipes.tasks.detectors-ci]', find() returned -1 and its 'guards not in ci_block' assertion had gone vacuously true -- re-anchored on guild-tasks and asserts the anchor is found. The two dashboard not-in assertions (test_fresh_clone_class_path, test_invariants) guard RETIRED tasks and stay valid. Lesson applied: pr-preflight gains a fifth pinned leg {pyforge-doctor-scripts-test, pyforge-ci} -- that lane had no local equivalent, the gap pr-preflight exists to close. Local: scripts-suite 489 passed, steward guards 13 passed.

- (capability) CAP-1 — from spec-pyforge-steward: intent - An operator can issue, scope, rotate, audit, inventory, and record revocation of credentials through one CLI (`steward keys`), with the historical `JFROG_API_KEY` cross-host leak pattern closed as a named automated regression test. success - A credential declared with an explicit host allowlist is never attached to a request outside it; `encrypt`/`decrypt` round-trips a fixture exactly via `age`; `rotate` re-encrypts every secret under a newly generated identity and the old identity fails to decrypt afterward, with no calendar/cron auto-rotation path; `audit --drift` reports the historical unconditional-injection pattern against a fixture and reports clean against today's fixed `_http.py`; `list` enumerates identities with scope/last-rotated metadata and never prints a raw secret value under any flag; `revoke` marks an identity retired and prints manual remediation guidance with zero third-party API calls; a dedicated conformance test fails loudly if the host-gating logic is ever removed or bypassed. ← spec-pyforge-steward CAP-1 (shipped 2026-09-17)
- (capability) CAP-2 — from spec-pyforge-steward: intent - An operator can build and publish the Pages dashboard through one reconciled command (`steward deploy dashboard`) instead of hand-running `dashboard-gen` + `git push`. success - Running twice with no source change between runs results in zero commits on the second run; a real change to the generated output results in exactly one new commit containing exactly the changed files, pushed to the existing Pages-serving branch; `--dry-run` performs the build+diff and prints it without committing or pushing; `status` reports the last successful deploy's commit SHA and timestamp read from Git history alone, with no separate state store. ← spec-pyforge-steward CAP-2 (shipped 2026-09-17)
- (capability) CAP-3 — from spec-pyforge-steward: intent - An operator or an unattended bmad-loop session can materialize any named pixi environment or a bmad-loop runner+environment together, list what exists, and verify the `environment.yaml` sync gate, without recalling raw pixi/`bmad-loop-worktree` syntax. success - A valid environment name materializes via `pixi install -e`; an invalid name reports a clear error listing valid names rather than surfacing pixi's raw error; `--runner bmad-loop --env <name>` wraps `scripts/bmad-loop-worktree` to materialize a worktree plus its named environment in one call, surfacing underlying failures clearly rather than leaving orphaned state unreported; `--list` enumerates every `pixi.toml` `[environments]` entry with its composing features (plus `--json`); `--verify` wraps the existing sync-gate check and reports drift with a non-zero exit when out of sync. ← spec-pyforge-steward CAP-3 (shipped 2026-09-17)
- (capability) CAP-4 — from spec-pyforge-steward: intent - An operator can declare and query a machine-readable resource ceiling and get an honest signal about spend rather than a fabricated number. success - `budget set --cap <amount><currency>/<period>` records a stable, documented schema and rejects a malformed cap value without writing a corrupt entry; `budget show` prints the declared ceiling(s) in human and `--json` form, and reports clearly (not a crash or misleading zero) when none is declared; `budget check` returns one of three distinct exit codes — not-configured / under-budget / over-budget — never collapsing "no data" into a pass, with no cloud-cost-SDK or Kubecost/OpenCost/Infracost client import anywhere in the codebase. ← spec-pyforge-steward CAP-4 (shipped 2026-09-17)

- (decision by claude) One-chain fold 2026-09-19: PR #1507 (a parallel session) minted a standalone Dream + Spec (`spec-sprint-ledger-query-module`, `fold-exemption: cross-station-seam`) for a steward-owned feature — the exemption token is reserved for the kernel/testing-kit seams every station imports (one-chain-per-station), so the pair is folded here: the Dream becomes a dated seed on docs/dreams/pyforge-steward.md (the standalone file archived in place), the Spec's capabilities become CAP-146..149 below and its passport capability binds to the existing CAP-140 (Story 61.2 remains the story of record — #1507's `WorkPassport` Django model is a partial, parallel realization outside 61.2's declared surface: no `vendor_id`, not the existing Postgres join store, 61.1's corridor not landed). Story 63.5 re-binds to CAP-146..149 + CAP-140.
- (capability by claude) CAP-146 — one query engine over every station's tracked ledger ← spec-sprint-ledger-query-module CAP-1 (folded 2026-09-19). Intent: an operator, agent or dashboard filters and inspects story/epic telemetry across all eight stations through one engine (`pyforge.steward.sprint_ledger_query.SprintLedgerQueryEngine`) reading each station's TRACKED sprint-status-ledger.yaml and epics.md — never the Tier-3 feed, never docs/dashboard/data.js — with presets and station/epic/status/text filters. Success: per-station done/backlog/blocked counts equal fleet-picture's for the same tree (agreement test against scripts/fleet_scan.py::parse_sprint_status); every preset/filter has a fixture test.
- (capability by claude) CAP-147 — pluggable, hookable, feature-flagged ← spec-sprint-ledger-query-module CAP-2 (folded 2026-09-19). Intent: formatters, sources and lifecycle hooks are registered, not hard-coded, and every optional integration sits behind a flag resolved from flags.json / environment / CLI override (no SDK dependency in v1), so the core query path has no runtime dependency on any exporter's target. Success: a flag left off refuses the gated export (ok=false), never silently runs it; a registered plugin/hook is exercised by test without editing the engine; the module's only third-party import is yaml.
- (capability by claude) CAP-148 — every consumer gets its own shape from one engine ← spec-sprint-ledger-query-module CAP-4 (folded 2026-09-19). Intent: one query result renders as markdown/summary/table, schema-validated json (the schema ships with the package), Herald's facts-ledger shape, an atlas-dataset payload (steward exports, Atlas renders — canopy:AD-13 / canopy:AD-23), jira-csv, github-json and get_runnable_backlog() for Marshal — with strict stdout/stderr separation and no default write into another station's tree. Success: each formatter round-trips a fixture; the json payload validates; get_runnable_backlog() agrees with marshal's Deps grammar (S-x.y, none, station:S-x.y); diagnostics never reach stdout.
- (capability by claude) CAP-149 — three front doors, one engine ← spec-sprint-ledger-query-module CAP-5 (folded 2026-09-19). Intent: `steward ledger-query` (a counted duty), the pixi tasks sprint-ledger-query / sprint-ledger-postgres-sync, and the bmad-sprint-ledger-query skill wrap the engine rather than re-implement it; the HTMX backlog view reads through it. Success: the duty-count invariants include it; --format choices come from the registry; the skill's documented invocations run verbatim.
- (constraint by claude) Folded from spec-sprint-ledger-query-module (2026-09-19): steward never imports vizro (canopy:AD-13 / canopy:AD-23); exporters never write into another station's tree by default (flag + explicit --output only); the minted UUID is identity and tracker keys are aliases (CAP-140's rule); reads come from TRACKED ledgers only and fleet_scan.parse_sprint_status stays the reader of record; stdout carries payload only.

- (note by claude) Derivation record of the folded spec-sprint-ledger-query-module (folder removed 2026-09-19 — chain-sprawl reads no status, so a post-ruling absorbed folder or archived Dream is a finding forever; the detector's own remedy is 'a dated section of a station Dream and a CAP on that station's Spec', which now exist, and the 20-line record lives here). Origin: the parallel session that opened PR #1507 minted a 35-line Intent/Boundaries/Interface sketch plus a two-line 'Decision Log' (2026-09-19 — Initial Specification: architecture approved: pluggable formatters, source plugins, OpenFeature SDK feature flags, Work Passports UUID minting, Django Admin/HTMX views, multi-system exporters) and Story 63.5 citing CAP-1..4 nothing defined. Re-derived through bmad-spec the same day as CAP-1..5 (→ CAP-146, 147, [CAP-140], 148, 149), constraints (never imports vizro; no default cross-station writes; UUID is identity; TRACKED reads only; no hard-coded public URLs; stdout payload only), non-goals (no second ledger parser — fleet_scan.parse_sprint_status stays the reader of record; no Vizro rendering in steward; no live Jira/GitHub API calls in v1). Assumption 'openfeature-sdk import is guarded' resolved by reading the code: no openfeature import at all, flags are file/env/CLI. Q1 (do exporters' default target paths inside other stations' trees need those Specs to declare them?) resolved at remediation: there are no default target paths any more — only an explicit --output writes. - (capability by claude) CAP-150 — the ledger query answers done / running / next in one call. Intent: every story the engine returns carries a next field — done (status done), running (a live dispatch or loop run is on it), ready (backlog with every declared dependency done — get_runnable_backlog()'s predicate, the Deps grammar restated from marshal's spec_deps), waits on S-x.y[, …] (backlog with an unmet dependency, naming them), blocked (status blocked), or ? (running fact unavailable); --ready and --running filter to those values; the summary carries per-station ready and running counts beside done/backlog/blocked; table/markdown/json carry the column. The running fact is read from marshal's own CLI (marshal watch --fleet --format json through the pyforge.core process primitive, one call per query) — steward never parses marshal's journal and never imports pyforge.marshal; when marshal is absent or the call fails, every next that would read running reads ? and one WARN names the cause (fail-open, CAP-15's posture); the fact is the current checkout's (marshal's Tier-3 is per clone — documented in --help). Success: sprint-ledger-query -- --unimplemented --format table reproduces the 08:00Z hand-composed view (doctor 29.1 running, 24.1/30.2 ready, 24.2/24.3/30.3 waiting; marshal 46.7 running, 46.1/46.2/46.6/46.9/46.10/47.1 ready; steward 61.4 running, 61.5/59.3–59.7/60.2–60.4/62.2/62.3/63.3 ready) from a fixture of those ledgers, epics and a recorded watch payload; --ready equals get_runnable_backlog() exactly; --running equals the watch payload's running rows; unreachable marshal → ? + WARN, exit unchanged; the bmad-sprint-ledger-query skill documents the column and the two filters; pyforge-steward-test green; duty-count invariants unchanged (no new duty — flags on the existing one).

- (capability by claude) CAP-151 — pyforge-foundry-full: the fleet's whole dependency closure is one locked artifact. Intent: pixi.toml declares pyforge-foundry-full = the union of every PyForge feature (python, pyforge-guild, guild-tasks, pyforge-core, pyforge-testing-kit, the eight stations), no-default-feature — never the runtime (that is pyforge-guild), never the recipe factory (local-recipes), never installed by default; it exists so every station-only extra resolves together or the lock refuses, and a station's own env carries what its code wields (bmad-eval-quality in pyforge-steward). Success: pixi lock solves pyforge-foundry-full on linux-64, osx-arm64-min and win-64; the lock carries it; llms-full-check stays clean; pyforge-station-tests green on the lock; the first solve's findings (mason python-build window, warden py-rattler floor) are fixed at their owners with the reason recorded; DW-OPS-2026-09-20-1 resolved.
- (capability by claude) CAP-152 — no station code assumes the local-recipes environment at runtime. Intent: only pyforge-guild exists at runtime; every task a station shells to (bmad-loop list/status, deck-export, deck-facts, deck-trio, platform-ci-local, wasm-build, the bmad-builder / TEA skill share dirs steward provisions from) is reachable from pyforge-guild — registered in guild-tasks with its deps in the pyforge-guild feature — and a meta-test in the steward suite reds any -e local-recipes shell-out or .pixi/envs/local-recipes path in any station's src/ (tests may stage fixtures under that path only when the code under test is the provisioner's own source lookup, which then reads the Guild env's share dir instead). Success: the meta-test lists zero offenders once marshal 46.12 and herald 25.1 land (steward's own provision.py / upgrade.py / suite.py fixed in this story); pixi run -e pyforge-guild <task> works for every shelled task; pyforge-guild's size stays under the Dream's minimal-default intent (report the delta); pyforge-station-tests green.

- (capability by claude) CAP-153 — lint and types gate the ten pyforge-* packages, locally and on the runners alike. Intent: every src/shared/packages/pyforge-* pyproject.toml carries [tool.ruff] and [tool.mypy] with py314 targets (mypy strict for pyforge-core); repo-level `ruff`, `ruff-format` and `mypy` tasks in [feature.guild-tasks.tasks] run them over all ten packages from pyforge-guild; a target-version registry check (like pixi-version-check) reds a package whose target drifts from the interpreter; one CI lane runs exactly those tasks and pr-preflight gains the same leg, so local and runner cannot diverge. Success: `pixi run -e pyforge-guild ruff`, `ruff-format`, `mypy` exit 0 on main; the CI lane and the pr-preflight leg invoke the same tasks; a deliberately planted violation in any package reds both; src/platform's own platform-ci lane unchanged. (Dream 2026-09-20 evening.)

- (direction) 2026-09-27 update from docs/dreams/pyforge-steward.md Realization-log entry '2026-09-27 (later) -- Proposed: housekeeping that leaks, and a gate that re-checks what main already checked': (1) workspace clean tars .pixi/envs into every archive (~13 GB per preflighted worktree; .steward/workspace-archive held 75 GB in 62 archives); (2) a fleet clean --merged-only raises on a record whose branch is gone, stops the sweep, and its finally-save drops the failing record (it was popped before the check); (3) the pre-push hook runs the full pr-preflight on a push whose every commit is already on origin/main (marshal refresh: 8 loop/<slug> fast-forwards, ~80 min); (4) .steward/preflight-skips.log records the checked-out branch and HEAD, not the pushed refs. Operator direction 2026-09-27: fix all four now.
- (capability) CAP-155 -- workspace clean archives the work, never the environments, and one undecidable record never stops the sweep. Intent: _archive_worktree leaves reinstallable pixi environment dirs (.pixi/envs, .pixi/solve-group-envs) out of the tar while every other file -- the tracked .pixi/config.toml included -- archives as before; in clean_workspaces a record whose merge check or archive raises is reported as skipped with the reason and kept in bookkeeping, and the sweep continues to the next record. Success: a worktree carrying a populated .pixi/envs archives without it and still carries .pixi/config.toml; a fleet clean --merged-only over [record with missing branch, merged record] archives the merged one, reports the other skipped with its git error, and keeps it in bookkeeping; no record is ever lost from bookkeeping by an exception; pyforge-steward-test green.
- (capability) CAP-156 -- the pre-push gate preflights only what is new, and its journal names what was pushed. Intent: scripts/pre_push_preflight.sh skips the preflight, journaled, when every pushed ref's commits are already reachable from origin/main (git rev-list <local sha> --not origin/main is empty for every non-delete ref) -- a loop-home fast-forward to main carries nothing main has not verified; any new commit, an unreadable origin/main, or refs it cannot parse still run the full preflight. Every .steward/preflight-skips.log line (dispatch/*, nothing-new, manual opt-out) records the pushed remote ref(s) and local sha(s), falling back to the checked-out branch and HEAD only when the hook received no ref information. Success: pushing loop/<slug> at origin/main skips with an 'already on origin/main' line naming refs/heads/loop/<slug>; a push with one new commit runs the preflight; the manual opt-out's line names the pushed ref, not the checked-out branch; the existing dispatch/* and delete skips are unchanged; tests/scripts/test_lint_types_gate.py green.

- (decision) CAP-155/156 amended 2026-09-27 (Stories 68.1/68.2 review 1: FAIL, 1 high + 2 medium). HIGH: under pre-commit (the installed pre-push path) the hook receives only the FIRST ref of a multi-ref push (pre-commit's _pre_push_ns returns on the first line), so a hook-side 'nothing new' skip let 'git push origin loop/x zfeature' send zfeature's new commit unchecked -- it breaks CAP-154. The sound fallback (no local branch/tag/HEAD commit outside origin/main or the remote) never holds here: local tags alone carry ~111k such commits. Operator decision: the hook keeps NO nothing-new skip; the tool that proves a push is nothing-new sets the journaled opt-out with its proof -- marshal refresh, pyforge-marshal:CAP-267 / Story 57.1. CAP-156 is therefore: every skip line (delete, dispatch/*, opt-out) names the pushed ref(s) and sha(s); the delete skip is journaled too. MEDIUM: an exception other than WorkspaceError (Ctrl-C at the confirm prompt) still dropped the in-flight record -- it is now saved back by the finally; a failed tar left a partial archive, re-created every sweep -- it is removed, and tarfile errors are wrapped. LOW folded into CAP-155: .pixi/bld (1-2 GB path-dependency build cache) is reinstallable too; a sweep that reported an error row exits non-zero.

- (decision) CAP-157 minted 2026-09-27 (evening) from the station Dream's entry of the same date (operator's choice after the archive audit: 'workspace clean proves the worktree against main first, and writes only a note when nothing is unique'). Before archiving, _archive_worktree asks git for a proof: 'git status --porcelain' empty (tracked changes and untracked non-ignored files alike) and the worktree HEAD an ancestor of the record's source (both resolved to commits). Proven -> a <slug>-<stamp>.landed.txt note (slug, branch, path, HEAD sha, the source and its sha, and every git-ignored path not kept, from 'git status --ignored') instead of a tarball; anything unproven -- an unlanded commit, an uncommitted edit, an untracked file, a source git cannot resolve -- archives as before (CAP-155). Archive-not-delete stands for all unlanded work. Found 2026-09-27: after Story 68.1, a deep audit of the 25 archives left (3.1 GB) found none holding work missing from main (19 byte-identical to a main commit; the rest earlier drafts main carries in later form); two held stray copies of local secrets. The operator deleted all 25 and asked for this. A new epic (69): Epic 68 is done.
- (decision) CAP-157 amended 2026-09-27 (evening, Story 69.1 review 1: FAIL -> fixed). The proof that lets clean keep a note instead of a tarball now requires ALL of: (1) status --porcelain empty with -c status.showUntrackedFiles=normal and --ignore-submodules=none pinned in _worktree_dirty (config hid an untracked file; a submodule ignore=all hid a submodule commit -- the pin also fixes workspace status and the repo-set dirty gate, which shared the blind spot); (2) no index entry marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged (ls-files -v; both hide an edited file from status); (3) HEAD AND the recorded branch (when it exists) ancestors of the source -- cleanup deletes the branch, not HEAD, so a detached HEAD proved nothing about a branch commit; (4) the source resolved unambiguously (a local branch or tag named origin/main only warns); (5) the git-ignored listing succeeds (a failed one wrote '(0)'), and no ignored path under _bmad-output/ is a real file or directory rather than a backlink symlink (a scratch worktree's own Tier-3 drafts exist nowhere else). The note lists at most 200 ignored paths (core.quotePath=false) and counts the rest. And, in every clean: the recorded branch is deleted only when it is on the source -- an unmerged branch's commits exist nowhere else (a tarball holds files, never history), so it is kept; this closes a history loss that predated 69.1.
- (decision) CAP-157 amended 2026-09-27 (evening, Story 69.1 review 2: FAIL on one medium -> fixed). (1) The git-ignored listing is read as NUL-separated bytes (status --ignored --porcelain -z) and decoded for display with backslashreplace; any undecodable output is no proof, never a crash (a non-UTF-8 ignored name raised UnicodeDecodeError and aborted the whole sweep -- breaking CAP-155's one-bad-record rule; a C-quoted path also slipped past the Tier-3 guard). (2) The source must resolve to a remote-tracking ref: 'rev-parse --symbolic-full-name' names the ref git's lookup chose, which is locale- and config-independent (core.warnAmbiguousRefs=false silenced the ambiguity warning the first fix relied on). (3) A gitlink (a submodule's own ignored files would go unnamed) and any per-worktree ref (refs/worktree/, refs/bisect/) are no proof. (4) The pre-push skip journal (.steward/preflight-skips.log, git-ignored, per worktree) is copied into the note. (5) A kept unmerged branch is reported: the archived row carries branch_kept and the clean output says '(branch <b> kept: not on its source)'; a later start of that slug needs it deleted by hand. Branch deletion itself compares against the source as git resolves it (a shadow keeps the commit on the shadow ref).

- (decision) CAP-158 minted 2026-09-27 (night) from the station Dream's entry of the same date (operator: fix the residual short-origin/main reads; closes DW-steward-platform-diff-guard-short-origin-main-2026-09-27 and steward's half of DW-warden-tea-advisory-short-base-2026-09-27). workspace.py hands git its recorded source and branch by short name in start (worktree add), status (_ahead_behind, _branch_merged_into) and clean (--merged-only's merged check, _source_commit, the branch-drop _commit_of). Probed on the old code: a local branch or tag named origin/main at an unmerged workspace's tip made clean --merged-only archive the worktree and delete its branch (its commits then reachable only through the stray ref), status report it merged, and start refuse; a tag named like the branch stood in for it in the merged check. Every git read of a source origin/<b> now names refs/remotes/origin/<b> and every branch read refs/heads/<branch>; recorded sources stay as written. The platform's copy of the kit's diff guard (src/platform/tests/test_warden_portal_audit_start_get.py) and the tea-test-review pixi task name refs/remotes/origin/main. Epic 70 / Story 70.1.
- (decision) CAP-158 amended before landing (Story 70.1 review 1: FAIL -> fixed). (1) It also covers the one documented caller of the tea-test-review task, marshal's review lens (_bmad/custom/bmad-review.toml, spec-bmad-suite-lifecycle CAP-4's marshal half, marshal Story 31.3): TEA keeps the last --base it is given, so the lens's own --base origin/main overrode the task's new default; the lens now defaults to refs/remotes/origin/main and writes a stated origin/<b> as refs/remotes/origin/<b>. (2) _branch_ref always returns refs/heads/<branch>: a record's branch is the slug start created, and a slug beginning refs/ (refs/heads/main) passed through read the real main, so an unmerged workspace looked merged. (3) CAP-157's success clause 'a local ref shadowing the source (also with core.warnAmbiguousRefs=false)' among the cases that still archive is superseded: the proof now reads refs/remotes/<source> itself, so a landed worktree past a shadow leaves a note and an unlanded one still archives (its two tests are retargeted to say so). Tests added for the confirmed clean's branch-drop proof and the landed proof past a shadow; every full-ref read in workspace.py is killed by a test when reverted alone.

- (direction) 2026-09-27 update from docs/dreams/pyforge-steward.md Realization-log entry '2026-09-27 -- Proposed: the preflight answers in under a minute': pr-preflight is the one command that makes a green local run mean a green CI run, and since Story 66.2 the pre-push hook runs it on every push (scripts/pre_push_preflight.sh:80). Measured 2026-09-27 (16-core laptop, marshal-only branch at c18d048b78): 28 leaf lanes, 622.8 s serial. Verified against the code the same day: (1) serial -- pr-preflight is a depends-on aggregate (pixi.toml:1491-1501) whose legs lint-types (:1310-1312), pyforge-station-tests (:1458-1476, 'depends-on runs the nine in order') and pyforge-station-coverage-gates (:1478-1489) nest further aggregates; flattened it is exactly 28 leaves; scripts/detectors.py:450 runs every selected detector one after another; no suite task passes -n, and pytest-xdist is declared only in [feature.local-recipes.dependencies] (pixi.toml:1960, :2227). (2) more than CI -- .github/workflows/pyforge-station-tests.yml:26-44 path-filters the workflow and its changes job (:109-131) selects a station's suite only on that station's paths (core on any station; all nine on pyforge-core / pyforge-testing-kit / pixi.toml / pixi.lock / the workflow file); coverage-gates.yml:31-37 and :87-104 likewise, but its trigger paths leave pixi.toml out entirely (its changes job lists pixi.toml as shared surface, yet the workflow never starts on a pixi.toml-only diff); cfe-regression-net.yml:48-54 fires test-ci only on the CFE paths, pixi.toml, pixi.lock and itself; docsite-check.yml:32-37 fires site-check only on docsite/, docs/dashboard/, presentations/, pixi.toml and itself; lint-types.yml:16-26 on src/shared/packages/** and its own scripts; detectors.yml:54-55 has no paths filter, so detectors-ci and pyforge-doctor-scripts-test (detectors.yml:89) run for every diff. (3) one suite twice -- pyforge-marshal-coverage-gate (pixi.toml:740-743) runs scripts/coverage_gates_ci.py, whose _run_pytest_cov (scripts/coverage_gates_ci.py:147-188) re-runs tests/unit + tests/meta -m 'not slow' under --cov with the station src and scripts/ on PYTHONPATH, after pyforge-marshal-test (pixi.toml:732-734) ran the whole tree. (4) facts the Dream did not state: docs-map-render-test and docs-gen-test (pixi.toml:1217-1219, :1247-1249) have no CI lane of their own (tests/scripts under pyforge-ci only importorskips them), so no rule can skip them and they always run; detectors.yml's sweep is advisory on the runner (exit 0 except the blocking cfe_rebuild_guard_check and ledger-regression steps, detectors.yml:170-229) while detectors-ci reds locally on any finding -- the preflight stays deliberately stricter there (AGENTS.md's merge gate), so 'red locally iff CI would red' binds lane selection and each lane's own verdict, never a loosening of a lane to CI's advisory posture; CI's atlas-test runs kedro-test, which depends-on pyforge-atlas-test (pyforge-station-tests.yml:195, pixi.toml:2655-2657), and the coverage matrix runs the driver by command line (coverage-gates.yml:197), so a lane's CI counterpart is found through depends-on closures and command lines, not task names alone. Two seams bind the change: pyforge-core's meta-test reads pr-preflight's depends-on (src/shared/packages/pyforge-core/tests/meta/test_conformance_lane_wired.py:173) and tests/scripts/test_lint_types_gate.py:110 pins the hook's command line. Operator direction 2026-09-27: spec it now and decompose it into dispatchable stories; SPEC.md rendered by an anchor-asserting script (operator-approved).
- (capability) CAP-159 -- the preflight answers in under a minute. Intent: pixi run -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight -- and so CAP-154's pre-push hook -- runs the lanes CI's own rules select for the branch's diff and no others, runs them concurrently, and journals every run's per-lane wall time and exit code. The lane list stays declared once in pixi.toml (the depends-on aggregate, read, never copied); which lanes a diff selects is read at run time from .github/workflows/*.yml (each workflow's on.pull_request.paths and its changes job), never from a second hand-kept list; the diff base is refs/remotes/origin/main (CAP-158), and an unreadable base, a rule the reader cannot evaluate, or a lane with no CI counterpart runs the lane. Concurrent lanes share no mutable state: environments install one at a time before any lane starts (.pixi/bld), each lane gets its own temp dir, pytest basetemp and cache dir, and coverage data file, and lanes sharing a CI service container (scribe's Postgres on 5433) never overlap. A station's suite lane skips the tests its coverage gate's own run already covers, so every test runs once and the floor stays the driver's verdict; the station suites that bound a single-station run declare pytest-xdist in their own tasks, so CI runs them the same way; the detector registry runs its detectors concurrently. Verdicts stay each lane's exit code, never a pipe; PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1 stays the one journaled opt-out; the not-covered list (container, atlas's Chromium/DuckDB/WASM, herald's browser check, scribe's Postgres) does not grow. Success: on a single-station branch (a diff inside one src/shared/packages/pyforge-<station>/ tree, no shared surface) pr-preflight returns its verdict in under 60 s of wall clock on the 16-core reference laptop, and a budget check reading the journal (exit 0 / 1 / 2, never part of pr-preflight's verdict) reds such a run over 60 s naming its slowest lanes; for fixture diffs (one station, pixi.toml, a CFE path, a docsite path, a Dream-only edit, no origin/main) the preflight selects exactly the lanes the workflows' own rules select, plus every lane with no CI counterpart; the lanes the gate's run and the reduced suite run collect are disjoint and together equal the station task's own collection; the detector registry reports the same statuses, findings and exit code at --jobs 1 and --jobs 8; a shared-surface run still runs every lane CI's rules select and its journaled wall time is bounded by its slowest lane; a red lane still refuses the push; pyforge-steward-test green.
- (event) Self-validate 2026-09-27 (CAP-159): Pass 1 coherence -- intent and success present; ID sequential after CAP-158; success concrete and machine-checkable (fixture diffs with named expected lane sets and a parity oracle against the workflows' own rules; a collect-only disjointness check for the coverage reuse; a jobs-1 vs jobs-8 determinism check for the registry; a journal-reading budget check with exit 0 / 1 / 2); consistent with CAP-154 (the hook's command line, its red-push refusal and its opt-out unchanged), CAP-153 (the lint-types lane runs verbatim), CAP-156 (the skip journal unchanged) and CAP-158 (the diff base is the full ref); no Dream Constraint weakened -- a lane is skipped only by CI's own rule, an undecidable lane runs, verdicts stay exit codes, the budget check never changes pr-preflight's verdict. Pass 2 preservation -- every Dream-entry measurement maps: everything serial -> Stories 71.3 (lanes), 71.5 (the registry, the scripts suite) and 71.6 (the large suites); more than CI -> 71.2; one suite twice -> 71.4; per-lane timings journaled -> 71.1; the budget as a checked number -> 71.7. Two sketch items not taken, recorded: making the two slow detectors incremental (concurrency inside the registry reaches the budget without touching their internals; open again if the journal shows detectors-ci on the critical path), and xdist for test-ci (the CFE suite, 111.5 s) -- cfe-regression-net fires only on CFE paths and pixi.toml / pixi.lock, so test-ci never runs on a single-station branch; its speed is mason's work under the conda-forge-expert rules, and a CFE branch is bounded by its slowest lane. One parity gap the reuse opens is closed in 71.4: the gate's run puts scripts/ on PYTHONPATH, so a station whose unit or meta tests import a scripts/ module is not reduced. FR-32 registered in the PRD (Currency reconciliation -- 2026-09-27); the spine's currency note lands it on AD-1, AD-5 and AD-8, no AD amended.

- (direction) 2026-09-28 (later) update from docs/dreams/pyforge-steward.md Realization-log entry '2026-09-28 (later) -- Proposed: steward session check reads the seed check it asks'. Every marshal factory dispatch warns MRS-DISP-049 (cli/dispatch.py _surface_session_precondition_findings) because 'pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild steward session check --json' exits 1 with token-kit and codegraph-index ok:false, detail '... marshal seed check --json returned unparseable output: "failing": true; }; }', remedy marshal seed kit. Diagnosed 2026-09-28 in a fresh worktree: 'marshal seed check --json' writes ONE valid JSON document to stdout (8,302 bytes; stderr empty -- no pixi banner, nothing merged) and exits 1 on its own HARD findings. Since marshal Story 12.5 (2026-08-23, FR-123 / NFR-12; cli/seed.py _json_ok_envelope / _json_error_envelope) every marshal seed verb wraps its payload in a schema-stable {verb, ok, result|error} envelope, so the kit sits at result.kit. session.py _seed_kit_findings (Story 63.4, 2026-09-24) reads payload['kit'] at the top level; the KeyError falls into the same except as JSONDecodeError (except json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, TypeError), is reported as 'unparseable output', and the detail quotes the last three stdout lines joined by '; ' -- the closing lines of a valid document. Not the cause: stderr (empty), the pixi banner (absent from stdout), the non-zero exit (the reader never looks at returncode), a line-joined rendering (that is only how the detail prints). tests/unit/test_session.py builds a flat {'kit': [...]} payload the CLI never emitted, so the suite stayed green while the check never read a real kit. The seed check's own HARD findings (manifest paths whose slug placeholder is never rendered) are marshal's, minted in parallel on spec-pyforge-marshal the same day -- Kinship only, not this Spec's.
- (capability) CAP-162 -- steward session check reads marshal seed check's envelope, whatever its exit code (extends CAP-5; Story 63.4's token-kit and codegraph-index findings). Intent: session.py _seed_kit_findings reads the kit from the seed check's schema-stable envelope -- result.kit under {verb, ok, result} (marshal FR-123 / NFR-12) -- whatever the process exit code: exit 1 is the seed check's own verdict on its HARD findings, not a failed read. token-kit and codegraph-index report the kit entries' statuses by today's per-item rules (any status but ok is non-ok for token-kit; the codegraph-index entry drives codegraph-index). An ok:false envelope makes both findings non-ok with the envelope's error type and message as the detail. Stdout that is not JSON keeps 'unparseable output' (its tail named, stderr when present); a JSON document with no result.kit list reads 'no kit report' naming the top-level keys it found -- never 'unparseable'. The unit fixtures take the envelope shape, one of them recorded verbatim from the live CLI on 2026-09-28. Steward still shells marshal's CLI and never imports marshal. Success: the recorded 2026-09-28 document (exit 1, result.failing true, kit statuses ok / layer-off / instrument-unavailable) yields token-kit ok:false naming its non-ok items and codegraph-index ok:false naming instrument-unavailable, with no detail saying 'unparseable'; the same document with every kit item ok yields both findings ok whether the process exited 0 or 1; an ok:false envelope names the seed error's type and message; 'not json' still reads 'unparseable output'; {verb: check, ok: true, result: {}} reads 'no kit report'; restoring the top-level payload['kit'] read fails the recorded-document test (mutation); pyforge-steward-test green.
- (decision) 2026-09-28 (later) -- pyforge-atlas stays out of the Guild environment, and pixi.toml's Guild section says why beside warden's (operator direction 2026-09-28: measure whether atlas can join [feature.pyforge-guild.dependencies] the way Story 72.1 adds mason; if it cannot, mint nothing for inclusion and document the exclusion like warden's). Measured 2026-09-28 on linux-64: 'pixi run -e pyforge-guild pyforge atlas --help' answers "unknown station 'atlas'; known: doctor, herald, marshal, scribe, steward" (exit 2); in -e pyforge-atlas it answers (kedro's run help, exit 0, also with pyforge.warden and boring_semantic_layer blocked). It SOLVES: pyforge-foundry-full composes the pyforge-guild and pyforge-atlas features and is locked on linux-64 (720 packages), osx-arm64 and win-64. It should NOT join: (1) weight -- the pyforge-atlas package's run-dependency closure (kedro, kedro-dagster, dagster, duckdb, pandas, pyarrow, ibis-framework, pandera, vizro, bokeh, openlineage, opentelemetry, a2a-sdk, ...) is 404 packages, 257 of them absent from the Guild: 179 MB to download, ~841 MB installed (conda-meta sizes, softlinks excluded), onto a Guild of 243 packages / ~1,396 MB (du 1.5 GB after a cold install the same day) -- about +60% on the environment every agent, harness and CI job installs, for one station's analytics stack; (2) pins -- the union moves the Guild's own: libabseil 20260526 -> 20260107 (libarrow, libgrpc, libprotobuf, orc pin it), which takes nodejs 26.10.0 -> 24.21.0; protobuf 7.35.1 -> 6.33.5 (dagster protobuf <7); filelock 4.0.3 -> 3.32.6 (ibis-framework-core <4); (3) it would still be partial -- atlas's pipelines lean on feature-level supply the package does not carry (boring-semantic-layer, the warden [gate] extra), which stays in -e pyforge-atlas. No CAP minted and no story for inclusion. The exclusion is documented in pixi.toml's [feature.pyforge-guild.dependencies] comment beside warden's by Story 72.1's banner rewrite (the same lines; amended 2026-09-28). CAP-161's existing text already says 'pyforge-atlas is not added here'. Kinship: the front door's 'on the roster but not installed here; use -e pyforge-<station>' message, minted in parallel on spec-pyforge-core (marshal project) -- not duplicated here. Two CAP-5 measurements found on the way, carried on Story 72.1 for its author and the operator: the Guild's cold install is already 1.5 GB (du), past CAP-5's 1 GB bound (measured 859 MB on 2026-09-16) before mason joins; and pyforge-mason's package run-dependencies cap pixi >=0.80.0,<0.81 and bring conda-lock (virtualenv <21), so the Guild + Mason union takes the Guild's pixi 0.81.0 -> 0.80.0 and virtualenv 21.12.1 -> 20.39.0 (read from pyforge-foundry-full's lock).

- (decision) (by operator) 2026-09-28: CAP-5's size bound is restated at the measured size plus headroom -- 1 GB -> 2 GB on disk for a cold 'pixi install -e pyforge-guild' -- so the criterion checks growth instead of failing on day one; never silently loosened, and this entry records old and new. OLD: under 1 GB (measured 2026-09-16: 859 MB, detectors-ci 32/32, Story 63.1). MEASURED 2026-09-28: the Guild is already past the old bound before pyforge-mason joins -- a cold install in a fresh worktree at 258883d3e8 (.pixi/envs/pyforge-guild created 06:08 that day) reads 1.5G by 'du -sh', 1,461 MiB by 'du -sm', 1,419,115,141 bytes apparent, 243 packages (conda-meta), matching the 2026-09-28 (later) atlas decision's 1.5 GB / ~1,396 MB. ESTIMATE with pyforge-mason (from pixi.lock, pyforge-guild vs pyforge-mason on linux-64, sized from the extracted rattler package cache): +50 packages, ~146 MB (rattler-build 72.9 MB and libglib 31.4 MB the largest), so ~1.6 GB. NEW: under 2 GB (the operator's figure: Story 72.1's measurement with pyforge-mason plus headroom), dated 2026-09-28. Story 72.1 measures the cold install with pyforge-mason and records the before and after sizes against the new bound; a measurement at or over 2 GB stops that story for the operator -- the bound is never raised inside a story. CAP-161's success and FR-34's consequence ('the Guild still installs under 1 GB from cold') move to the restated bound; Story 63.1's done record keeps its 1 GB text as history.
- (decision) (by operator) 2026-09-28: Story 72.1's pixi-downgrade stop condition is replaced, and conda-lock's virtualenv ceiling is decided on evidence. (1) Pixi: operator ruling 'no station or environment caps pixi' -- spec-pyforge-mason:CAP-30 (mason Story 20.1, key 20-1-mason-s-pixi-run-dependency-is-a-floor-and-a-guard-reds-any-pixi-ceiling, minted in parallel) makes pyforge-mason's run-dependency 'pixi >=0.80.0' (the <0.81 ceiling was the only pixi ceiling in the repo). 72.1 carries a Kinship to it (it lands after 20.1; if 20.1 has not landed and the re-solve still takes pixi to 0.80.x, 72.1 stops and reports -- it never re-pins pixi or edits mason's pixi.toml) and an AC that the Guild's resolved pixi does not move when pyforge-mason joins (0.81.x at the ruling; pixi-version-check exits 0). No ledger gate is added (no ledger key changes). (2) virtualenv: ACCEPT, recorded. conda-lock 4.0.2's conda-forge build requires 'virtualenv >=20.26.6,<21.0.0' (pixi.lock), so the union takes the Guild's virtualenv 21.12.1 -> 20.39.0, and virtualenv 20.39.0 requires 'filelock >=3.24.2,<4', so filelock 4.0.3 -> 3.x (a consequence the 72.1 amendment had not named). Evidence read 2026-09-28 from pixi.lock and the tree: the Guild's only virtualenv dependent is pre-commit 4.6.2 ('virtualenv >=20.10.0'); .pre-commit-config.yaml has one 'repo: local' with two 'language: script' hooks, so pre-commit creates no virtualenv here at all; no file under src/, scripts/ or .claude/scripts imports virtualenv and pixi.toml pins none; the Guild's filelock dependents are huggingface_hub (>=3.10.0), python-discovery (>=3.15.4; required only by virtualenv 21, so it may leave) and virtualenv; pyforge-foundry-full (the SBOM and default laptop install) already locks pre-commit 4.6.2 with virtualenv 20.39.0 and filelock 3.32.6, and local-recipes and pyforge-mason lock conda-lock with virtualenv 20.39.0. The ceiling is conda-lock's own upstream pin, not an estate pin, so the no-ceiling rule does not reach it; stopping would buy nothing the union has not already proven. Predicted union moves (pyforge-guild vs pyforge-mason locks): pixi (removed by 20.1), virtualenv, filelock; importlib-metadata stays 8.7.1 (opentelemetry-api <8.8.0) and tomlkit stays 0.13.2 (marshal's cap). 72.1's stop condition becomes: any Guild package other than virtualenv (to 20.x) and filelock (to 3.x) changes version, or pixi moves.
- (capability) CAP-5 (size bound restated 2026-09-28, operator ruling) -- `pyforge-guild`: one minimal environment is the default for every agent and harness. Intent: unchanged. Success: `pixi install -e pyforge-guild` from cold lands under **2 GB** on disk — the bound restated 2026-09-28 (operator ruling) at the measured size plus headroom, so the criterion checks growth instead of failing on its first day. It was 1 GB (**measured 2026-09-16: 859 MB, `detectors-ci` 32/32 — Story 63.1**); a cold install measured 1.5 GB on 2026-09-28 before `pyforge-mason` joined (`du -sm` 1,461 MiB, 243 packages), and Story 72.1 records the size with `pyforge-mason` (estimated +50 packages, ~146 MB) against the new bound (`local-recipes`: 10 GB). And `pixi run -e pyforge-guild detectors-ci` is green on `main`; `pixi run -e pyforge-guild marshal seed check` reports the headroom kit item present and a dispatch dry-run raises no `MRS-DISP-033`; `marshal-token-economy` Epic 46 names `pyforge-guild` as the environment its stories are verified in; `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.cursor/rules/*.mdc`, the eight station `SKILL.md` files and the cloud-environment install command name `pyforge-guild` as the session default; scribe recall stays `-e pyforge-scribe`; `environment.yaml` regenerated in the same PR; `pyforge-station-tests` green (shared surface).
- (capability) CAP-161 (amended 2026-09-28, operator rulings) -- the Guild environment answers `pyforge mason`. Intent: `pyforge-guild`, the session default every agent and harness runs (CAP-5), installs `pyforge-mason` beside core, doctor, marshal, steward, herald and scribe, so the front door routes `pyforge mason …` and the `mason` script is on `PATH` — the only grammar `bmad-agent-mason` may use. The front door is correct as built: `pyforge.core.dispatch` maps a station token to the primary console script of a `pyforge-*` distribution installed in the running environment (`dispatch.py:94-105`, with a per-station `distribution()` fallback at `:154-166`), and `[feature.pyforge-guild.dependencies]` (`pixi.toml:841-864`) never installed `pyforge-mason`, so `pixi run -e pyforge-guild pyforge mason --help` answered `unknown station 'mason'; known: doctor, herald, marshal, scribe, steward` (measured 2026-09-28). The feature gains one path dependency — the package's own run-dependencies (its engines and `pyforge-core`) come with it — and its banner comment names what the environment carries. Recipe work still runs in `-e local-recipes`; `mason doctor` reports the recipe verbs' availability in this environment as it finds it (mason's degradation contract). `pyforge-warden` stays out (its `osv-scanner` pin, `pixi.toml:844-849`) and `pyforge-atlas` is not added here. *(Amended 2026-09-28, operator rulings:)* the Guild's pixi does not move when Mason joins: no station or environment caps pixi, and `spec-pyforge-mason:CAP-30` (mason Story 20.1) makes Mason's run-dependency `pixi >=0.80.0`, dropping the `<0.81` ceiling that would have taken the Guild's pixi 0.81.0 → 0.80.0 (a Kinship; this story lands after it). Conda-lock, one of Mason's engines, still moves two Guild pins, and both are accepted: its conda-forge build requires `virtualenv >=20.26.6,<21` (21.12.1 → 20.39.0), and virtualenv 20 requires `filelock >=3.24.2,<4` (4.0.3 → 3.x). Evidence, 2026-09-28: the Guild's only virtualenv consumer is `pre-commit` 4.6.2 (`virtualenv >=20.10.0`), whose two hooks here are `repo: local`, `language: script`, so it builds no virtualenv for them; no repository code imports virtualenv; the Guild's other filelock consumer, `huggingface_hub` (`>=3.10.0`), admits 3.x, and `python-discovery`, which only virtualenv 21 requires, may leave; and `pyforge-foundry-full`, the SBOM and default laptop install, already locks `pre-commit` 4.6.2 with virtualenv 20.39.0 and filelock 3.32.6, as `local-recipes` and `pyforge-mason` lock conda-lock with virtualenv 20.39.0. The ceiling is conda-lock's own, upstream; this estate adds none. The size bound is CAP-5's, restated the same day at 2 GB. Success: `pixi run -e pyforge-guild pyforge mason --help` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild pyforge mason doctor --format json` exit 0; a steward meta-test reds a `[feature.pyforge-guild.dependencies]` without `pyforge-mason`; a cold `pixi install -e pyforge-guild` with `pyforge-mason` stays under CAP-5's bound as restated 2026-09-28 (2 GB), measured and recorded in the story beside the before-mason size (1.5 GB, 243 packages); the Guild's resolved pixi does not move (the version the rest of the workspace resolves, 0.81.x at the ruling) and `pixi-version-check` exits 0; the Guild's virtualenv reads 20.x and filelock 3.x, recorded in the story, and no other Guild package changes version; `environment.yaml` regenerated in the same change; `pyforge-station-tests` and `detectors-ci` green; `pyforge-steward-test` green.
