- (direction by claude) 2026-09-28 (night) update from docs/dreams/pyforge-steward.md Realization-log entry '2026-09-28 (night) -- Proposed: the object-storage seam gets its first consumer, Herald's deck exports' (Kinship: docs/dreams/pyforge-herald.md, the same-night entry; intake archive/docs/intake/airgapped_pptx_architecture_specification.md). Measured 2026-09-28 at cfae04e14b: src/platform/config/object_storage.py (Story 50.3, CAP-97) resolves one boto3 S3 client from OBJECT_STORAGE_ENDPOINT_URL / OBJECT_STORAGE_ACCESS_KEY / OBJECT_STORAGE_SECRET_KEY and says 'No existing feature is wired to consume this seam yet'; the chart wires those three as optional secretKeyRefs (templates/_helpers.tpl) and names no bucket or prefix, and no bucket or prefix setting exists in config/settings/base.py; every django-<station> portal reaches the host only through django_pyforge.* (access, portals, supervisor, flags), never through the host's config package; with networkPolicy enabled the web and worker egress policies list exactly their in-cluster peers, so a consumed endpoint outside the cluster has no egress allow; local development is CAP-96's platform-object-storage feature (silo on 127.0.0.1:9000), and CAP-97's round-trip test starts its own ephemeral silo. Operator rulings 2026-09-28: Herald's exports are the first consumer; bytes never go in PostgreSQL (the database belongs to the enterprise DB team). Herald's Stories 29.1/29.2 (minted in parallel on herald's chain) are blocked on this CAP's first story.
- (capability by claude) CAP-163 -- the object-storage seam gets its first consumer: a station streams bytes in and out by sha256 key (extends CAP-94..97; Herald's deck exports first). Intent: a streaming, sha256-addressed put/get contract over the platform seam, in the chrome every portal already imports -- django_pyforge.object_store -- so a django-<station> package, or the station API it serves under /stations/<name>/api/v1/, reaches the store while src/platform/ imports no pyforge.* and the chrome imports nothing from the host's config package. put spools the caller's stream to a bounded temporary file while hashing it, then uploads it under <prefix>/sha256/<hex> -- content-addressed, so identical bytes land once and an existing key is not re-uploaded -- and returns the key, sha256, size and content type; get streams the object back in chunks for a StreamingHttpResponse; nothing loads a whole object into memory or a model field. The client is CAP-97's config.object_storage.object_storage_client(), named by a setting (OBJECT_STORAGE_CLIENT_FACTORY) and resolved with import_string -- the chrome never builds a second client (AD-1). The bucket and prefix are settings (OBJECT_STORAGE_BUCKET, OBJECT_STORAGE_PREFIX) that the Helm chart names per environment, beside the three existing secret references (canopy:AD-19); with networkPolicy enabled, the web and worker workloads gain an egress allow to the consumed endpoint and nothing else. No presigned URLs in v1: the portal streams every byte behind OIDC and the station role, so the store needs no browser-reachable endpoint. The store is consumed and never self-hosted in production (CAP-94's dated 2026-09-10 exception; the chart renders no object-store workload); local development keeps CAP-96's platform-object-storage feature and its silo server. Metadata stays out of the seam: a consumer's rows (Herald's topic, kind, date, size, content type, source commit) live in its own table in public under a namespaced Liquibase changeset (canopy:AD-9), on the consumer's chain. PostgreSQL stays at 17 (fnd:CAP-12); no new process. The capability ships behind the flag pyforge.steward.object_store_consumer (spec-feature-flag-governance CAP-1), default off until the tree carries per-environment values: OFF, put and get refuse with a named ObjectStoreDisabled and a consumer keeps its git-only behaviour. Success: against a real silo (CAP-97's ephemeral-instance pattern), a put of a stream larger than the spool threshold returns the sha256 the test computed, lands at <prefix>/sha256/<hex> and never holds the whole payload in memory; a second put of the same bytes uploads nothing; get streams the same bytes back; a key that is not sha256/<64 lowercase hex> is refused before any client call; an unset bucket or prefix raises ImproperlyConfigured naming the setting; with the flag OFF both refuse; a meta-test reds a src/platform import of pyforge.* and a django_pyforge import of config.*; the rendered chart names the bucket and prefix per release, carries only secret references for credentials, renders no object-store workload, and with networkPolicy enabled allows egress to the configured endpoint from web and worker only; pyforge-steward-test green.
- (direction by claude) 2026-09-28 (night) update from docs/dreams/pyforge-steward.md Realization-log entry '2026-09-28 (night) -- Proposed: Warden's fleet scan reaches GitHub Enterprise with host-scoped credentials the key inventory holds' (Kinship: docs/dreams/pyforge-warden.md, the same-night entry; intake archive/docs/intake/system_architecture_specification.md). Measured 2026-09-28 at cfae04e14b: keys.py's HostScopedCredential / resolve_headers (FR-7, Story 1.2) decides only host membership and delegates every header to .claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/scripts/_http.py's auth_headers_for, which attaches GITHUB_TOKEN / GH_TOKEN only when the host contains github.com; a GitHub Enterprise Server host named by GITHUB_API_BASE_URL (resolve_github_api_urls, the existing row) falls to the JFrog branch or netrc, so no GitHub token ever reaches it (the docstring records this as tracked, not fixed). .steward/keys-inventory.yaml (FR-5) holds three observed rows (crc-pull-secret, crc-kubeadmin, github-pat-read-project) and no issued GitHub identity; KeyIdentityEntry has name, scope, provenance, status, last_rotated, identity_path, secrets; steward keys has encrypt, decrypt, rotate, list, audit and revoke, and no verb hands a secret to a child process. Operator rulings 2026-09-28: the scan covers the enterprise fleet on GHE; a fix PR on a fleet repo opens only after the operator approves that proposal. Warden Story 16.1 (minted in parallel on warden's chain) is blocked on this CAP's story.
- (decision by claude) 2026-09-28 (CAP-164 mint): the enterprise host stays out of the CFE surface. Two routes were weighed. (a) Teach _http.auth_headers_for to send GITHUB_TOKEN to the GITHUB_API_BASE_URL host: it changes what every CFE caller sends to a GHES host, needs the conda-forge-expert skill and a CFE retro with a CHANGELOG semver bump, and still sends one ambient token rather than a scoped identity. (b) Keep host membership in keys.py, which already 'decides only host membership': an inventory-issued, host-scoped identity is attached by keys itself, only for the enterprise host read from _http.py's existing GITHUB_API_BASE_URL row (AD-9: no Steward-owned URL config), and only through the same HostScopedCredential gate; every ambient credential still goes through auth_headers_for. Chosen: (b). AD-2's rule text ('delegates to auth_headers_for for anything HTTP-shaped') is amended in the spine to name the exception; keys.py stays the one chokepoint. A token reaches a consumer only in a child process's environment (steward keys exec), never on argv, stdout or a log, because warden may not import pyforge.steward and a printed token breaks the 'credential values are never printed' constraint.
- (capability by claude) CAP-164 -- steward keys learns the GitHub Enterprise host: a read identity for the fleet scan, and a PR-draft identity only for an operator-approved proposal (extends FR-5 and FR-7; Warden's fleet scan first). Intent: the host-scoped resolver (FR-7, keys.py) knows the enterprise host, read from _http.py's existing GITHUB_API_BASE_URL row (resolve_github_api_urls; AD-9, no Steward-owned URL config); keys.py still decides only host membership, and .claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/scripts/_http.py does not change. Because auth_headers_for never sends a GitHub token to a GHES host, an inventory-issued enterprise identity is attached by keys itself, only when a URL's host is the enterprise host, through the same HostScopedCredential gate; every ambient credential still goes through auth_headers_for (AD-2, amended 2026-09-28). The inventory (FR-5) gains two issued rows, each with its own age-encrypted payload and each rotated by steward keys rotate --scope: ghe-fleet-read (clone and contents read, for the fleet scan) and ghe-fleet-pr-draft (pull-request drafts, used only to open a proposal the operator approved). A consumer receives a token only through steward keys exec --scope <scope> [--approval <ref>] -- <argv>: the child's environment carries GH_HOST and GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN for the enterprise host, the ambient GITHUB_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN, GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN and GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN are removed from it first, and the token never appears on argv, stdout, stderr or a log. The draft scope refuses without an approval reference and journals the reference, scope, UTC time and the child's argv[0] (never the token) to .steward/keys-exec.log; the read scope never yields the draft token, and one payload serving both scopes is a keys audit finding. Deployed pods carry secret references only (canopy:AD-19); the chart wiring lands with the story that deploys the fleet scan. Nothing inbound: no webhook and no listener -- the scan polls. Secrets never ride CLI flags. Posture as docs/dreams/work-passports-dated-extracts.md states it: Security can show no PAT that opens their org or writes ours on their behalf. No calendar rotation and no provider API call (the Non-goals hold). The capability ships behind the flag pyforge.steward.ghe_fleet_credentials (spec-feature-flag-governance CAP-1), default off until the tree carries per-environment values: OFF, keys exec stays listed in --help as disabled and refuses with exit 2 and a flag-off message, and the resolver resolves no enterprise host (github.com behaviour unchanged). Success: with GITHUB_API_BASE_URL naming a fixture enterprise host, a ghe-fleet-read credential resolves a Bearer header for that host and nothing for github.com, another host, or a look-alike (a suffix or prefix of the host); with it unset, nothing resolves for any enterprise host; keys exec --scope ghe-fleet-read runs a child whose environment carries the read token and GH_HOST and none of the four ambient token variables, and a planted token appears in no argv, stdout, stderr or journal; keys exec --scope ghe-fleet-pr-draft without --approval exits 2 and runs nothing, and with one runs and journals the reference without the token; keys list and keys audit show both rows and never a value; audit reports a payload shared by both scopes; keys rotate --scope re-encrypts each; with the flag OFF exec exits 2 and the resolver resolves nothing for the enterprise host; the JFROG_API_KEY cross-host regression stays green; pyforge-steward-test green.
- (note by claude) 2026-09-28 (night): the Guild Spec docs/governance/spec-feature-flag-governance/SPEC.md (ready, rule date 2026-09-28) gives CAP-5 -- 'the tree can say what the rule needs' -- to steward ('Who does the work'). Decomposed as steward Epic 76, Stories 76.1-76.3, citing spec-feature-flag-governance CAP-5 directly: no steward CAP and no station FR is minted, following doctor Epic 24 (spec-coverage-gate-independence CAP-1..3 decomposed in doctor's epics.md with no station CAP and no PRD FR, 57b9921ea3). The three stories are flag-exempt: flag-infrastructure. Shape decided at the mint: (1) per-environment values are ONE value-only overlay document, src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json, keyed dev / staging / production, each mapping a key the tree defines to one of that flag's variants; the tree stays the only definition of every flag, the rendered per-environment tree is what the ConfigMap mounts and every evaluator reads, and state DISABLED in the tree wins in every environment (doctor's kill switch). This takes up the spine's Deferred row 'FILE flag env promotion overlays' and amends canopy:AD-11 in the spine. (2) Each flag carries owner, story, created, on_everywhere and cleanup_by in flagd flag metadata; on_everywhere is set exactly when the flag reads ON in every rendered environment, and cleanup_by is on_everywhere plus 90 days (Q4). (3) The six flags behind .steward/flags.json and the FLAGS_<NAME> layer (five in sprint_ledger_query.py, Story 65.1; enable_glass_export in glass.py, Story 61.3) become tree keys and evaluate through django_pyforge.flags (OpenFeature in-process FILE), reached by a function-local import with a named refusal, as doctor's flags kill-switch does; the environment-variable layer retires (a Guild non-goal), and --flag name=value stays a per-invocation override that persists nothing. Ordering: 76.2 and 76.3 wait on 76.1 (Deps S-76.x, station-local). The Guild Spec and its memlog are not steward's to edit; the 76.x ledger keys are reported for the Guild record.

- (note by claude) 2026-09-28 (night), amends the Epic 76 note above on one point, the CLI reader: a steward CLI cannot reach django_pyforge at runtime (django-pyforge has no pixi manifest, so no environment installs it; steward's tests reach it by sys.path only) and no station CLI environment carries the OpenFeature packages. The CLI-side evaluation therefore lives in pyforge.core.flags -- the pyforge.core.cutover_root precedent (Story 44.12, a steward-authored CLI reader in pyforge-core; spec-pyforge-core co-governs the path and is reconciled at landing, not blocked) -- which owns the environment (PYFORGE_ENVIRONMENT), the overlay composition and its validation, the rendered tree and a boolean evaluation through OpenFeature's in-process FILE provider, imported function-locally and refusing by name when absent; django_pyforge.flags configures the host's provider from the same rendered tree. - (decision by coordinator) 2026-09-28 (night), coordinator ruling on the CLI flag reader: steward Story 75.1 is THE home of pyforge.core.flags.read_boolean, and its shape is the fleet-wide contract every other station's flagged CLI story reuses (never re-implements); 75.1 dispatches first. The contract, now written into spec-75-1 and epics.md: read_boolean(key, default=False, *, flags_path=None) -> bool; tree resolution is pyforge.core.cutover_root.resolve_flags_path, reused (explicit path, PYFORGE_FLAGS_PATH, then the walk-up to src/platform/config/flags.json); state DISABLED reads False whatever default says (the kill switch wins); a present bool defaultVariant reads its value; a missing or unreadable tree, a missing key or a non-bool value reads default with one named WARN on stderr -- a new capability passes default=False, only a retrofit kill switch (Guild CAP-7) may pass True; Q3 helpers FlagOff (key and 'flag <key> is off'), require (raises FlagOff when off) and disabled_help (appends ' [disabled: flag <key> is off]' so the verb stays listed); the helpers choose no exit code -- each station's main() maps FlagOff to its own usage code (steward: 2, AD-8), so no frozen exit domain moves. Stories 76.1 (per-environment) and 76.3 (OpenFeature) extend it without changing the signature, the resolution order or the OFF/absent semantics. Numbering and Deps unchanged: 75.1 none, 76.1 S-75.1.

- (direction by claude) 2026-09-28 (night), grounding for Story 76.4 (grep of the tree at 0c8c07e6fc). Consumers of pyforge.three_surfaces: (1) src/platform/config/flags.json, the entry itself (state ENABLED, variants on/off, defaultVariant on). (2) src/shared/packages/django-pyforge/src/django_pyforge/flags.py: FLAG_KEY = 'pyforge.three_surfaces' is the default key of evaluate_boolean, evaluate_from_source, the MCP get_flag tool and python -m django_pyforge.flags, AND the key wait_until_ready probes with get_boolean_details inside every configure_file_provider call. apps.py's ready() runs configure_from_env at host startup, so removing the entry without re-keying readiness makes every host start raise 'FILE provider not ready after 8s' (FLAG_NOT_FOUND on each probe). openfeature-sdk 0.10.0 (the platform-dev pin) exposes client.get_provider_status() -> ProviderStatus.READY, a readiness signal that names no flag. (3) src/platform/tests/test_openfeature_file_flags.py imports FLAG_KEY to build its temporary flagd trees; those tests exercise the FILE machinery and stay, re-keyed to a test-local fixture key. Not consumers, left as they are: pyforge-doctor's 'flags kill-switch --flag' help example (__main__.py) and tests/unit/test_flag_kill_switch.py use the name as an arbitrary key in a temporary tree and read no real tree (doctor's chain; reported to the operator, not edited by steward); doctor spec-34-2's acceptance criterion names the key on the live tree; the done story specs spec-26-4 and spec-48-5, the Guild Dream's dated grounding and a project memory entry keep the name as record. (2) DW-FU-26-1 (duplicate DW-FU-18-2), CAP-86 and spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy CAP-12 (Story 49.6): SOCIALACCOUNT_STORE_TOKENS is never set, so allauth stores no token, idp_userinfo._access_token_for always returns None and current_claims falls back to login-time claims on every request; the only test mocks _access_token_for. - (decision by operator) 2026-10-04 (later): operator governance rulings on the preserved-work refs standard (accepting the architecture review's recommended defaults, preserved-work-refs-2026-10-04-review.md): (A) a new session_denials entry denies any branch or tag deletion whose tip is not an ancestor of refs/remotes/origin/main and that no preserve/ or archive/ tag contains, naming marshal preserve tag and the sweeper's --retire as the sanctioned forms (review B3); (B) the protected-ref denial widens from branch deletion to tag deletion, update-ref -d, push --mirror, a push --prune or fetch --prune-tags covering a listed prefix, rm of a ~/.bmad-loops/ home and gh pr merge --delete-branch on an explicit loop/ head (review M6). Story 85.1 lands as ruled earlier the same day, changing only the roster key's shape (protected_refs: full refnames, kind, scope; the roster only adds to each consumer's code floor; the recover/ and rescue/ branch entries drop). The ruleset parity check lives in steward (Q17); the pre-push hook skips a push it can prove carries only preserve/ or archive/ tags (Q13); applying any ruleset is the operator's settings change.
- (capability by claude) 2026-10-04 (later): mint CAP-165 (extends CAP-5, CAP-156, CAP-157; co-governs spec-pyforge-marshal:CAP-287) -- the protected refs are declared once, the live rulesets are proven to match, and no session deletes what would orphan commits. docs/governance/guild-roster.json declares protected_refs (full-refname prefixes with kind operational-branch | preserve-tag | archive-tag | legacy, scope origin | local | both, and the server rules each needs); every consumer unions them with its own code floor. A declared ruleset document under docs/governance/rulesets/ is rendered from the roster (tag rulesets for preserve/**, archive/** and rescue/** with deletion, update and force-push rules and no bypass; non_fast_forward on loop/**; restricted creation of refs/heads/preserve/** and refs/heads/archive/**), and a runtime detector compares it with the live rulesets through an authenticated gh api, failing closed; applying is the operator's. The hook denies every deletion form for a listed ref and any unlisted deletion that would orphan commits (rulings A and B), as defence in depth: the server ruleset is the guarantee, a git config such as fetch.pruneTags bypasses any command matcher, and harnesses with no hook deny surface are guarded by the rulesets alone. The pre-push gate skips and journals a push it can prove is tag-only under preserve/ or archive/. workspace clean parks unlanded commits as a preserve/<slug>/<N.M>/workspace-<sha8> tag through pyforge.core.preserve_refs before removal (a local tag suffices; the push goes through the content gate, debt otherwise), and its tarball keeps only gitignored bytes.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-04 (later): CAP-156 amended -- the hook adds one skip of its own: a push it can prove carries only tags under refs/tags/preserve/ or refs/tags/archive/ (every ref visible on stdin, or, in the pre-commit form that sees only the first ref, a proof the pushing tool supplies; pyforge.core.preserve_refs pushes one refspec per tag and supplies it), journaled; a mixed push still runs the preflight. CAP-157 amended (review minor 8: CAP-157, not CAP-155, owns branch deletion in clean) -- before an unlanded worktree is removed its commits are parked as a preserve/ tag (workspace producer), so a kept branch is no longer their only reachability; the tarball keeps gitignored bytes, and untracked files go into the snapshot, which the content gate scans before any push. PRD FR-38 registered; spine re-stamped (AD-1, AD-8, canopy:AD-11; no AD amended).

Measured on 488837c504: tests/scripts/test_session_denial_forms.py _script_accepts_argv (:109) runs [sys.executable, script, *argv] with cwd=REPO_ROOT (:112-:117) and counts the argv accepted unless the output says 'unrecognized arguments' or 'invalid choice:' (:119); the spec-surface-bare-write-baseline reason (guild-roster.json:374) names 'python scripts/spec_surface_check.py --write-baseline --spec <project>/<spec>', which _PLACEHOLDERS (:30) fills as pyforge-steward/spec-pyforge-steward, so test_live_roster_session_denial_forms_resolve (:225), test_broken_retire_form_in_reason_fails (:231) and test_historical_roster_with_retire_and_marshal_preserve_fails (:310) each run a real scoped stamp (spec_surface_check.py parses at :557, stamps at :612) wherever pyforge-doctor-scripts-test or pr-preflight's leg of it (pixi.toml:1592) runs. One run of the first test in a fresh worktree of 488837c504 rewrote scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json from 1,073,738 to 1,075,879 bytes (only this Spec's entry: its memlog hash and 35 file hashes) and left the ignored .lock sidecar; first seen 2026-10-08 during marshal Story 69.1's fix (the file's mtime moved after each of the three tests). Contract: the probe decides argv acceptance without executing the script's body -- a sys.executable -c bootstrap wraps argparse.ArgumentParser.parse_args and parse_known_args (and the intermixed forms) with a depth counter, runs runpy.run_path(script, run_name='__main__') with sys.argv=[script, *argv] and the script's directory on sys.path, and ends with exit 0 when the outermost parse succeeds; rejections still print 'unrecognized arguments' / 'invalid choice:'; a script with no argparse import is unprobeable and never run, one that exits before it parses is unprobeable, never accepted. ACs: the probe of spec_surface_check.py --write-baseline --spec pyforge-steward/spec-pyforge-steward leaves the baseline's bytes and mtime unchanged; a marker-after-parse fixture is accepted with no marker and rejected for an unknown flag; a subparser fixture pins the depth counter; a non-argparse fixture is unprobeable and not run; every existing test still passes. - (event) Story 71.10 minted 2026-10-08 (fix, no CAP, no flag; spec ready-for-dev), key 71-10-a-stopped-lane-is-journaled-cancelled-and-the-lane-that-stopped-the-run-red, in Epic 71 (in-progress), Deps S-71.9, from the station Dream's 2026-10-08 (cancelled lane) entry. Shipped behaviour: CAP-159 (FR-32), Story 71.3's run-and-cancel contract, whose AC reads "Given one fake lane that exits 1 while two others still run When the preflight runs Then it exits 1, the other two are terminated and journaled `cancelled`, and no child process is left running". Measured on ad6f0428ff: src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py _run_lane_in_pool (:300) journals every non-zero exit red (:336-:342), so a lane _RunCoordinator.terminate_children() (:219; SIGTERM :226, SIGKILL :233) killed returns -15 red and joins red_lanes (:338); the failing lane's own terminate_children() call (:341) waits for each killed process, so the killed lane's future finishes first, and the pool loop (:499-:511) takes it, sees stop_on_red, cancels and drops every other future, so lanes without a collected result, the culprit included, are journaled cancelled exit 0 / 0 s (:545-:549); _on_sigint (:467) hits the same red branch. Journal of the herald branch hygiene-herald-shelf-two-headings (two runs at 4bf5b746f8, 14 lanes, --jobs 16): pyforge-herald-coverage-gate red -15 after 14.0 s and 13.1 s, pyforge-herald-test (pytest exit 5, Story 71.9's defect) cancelled 0 / 0.0 s, four running lanes cancelled 0.0 s; stderr named the gate. test_red_lane_cancels_others (tests/unit/test_preflight_concurrency.py:156) uses an injected runner with no process and asserts nothing about the running lane; no test runs a real lane process or SIGINT. Contract: terminate_children() marks a lane's process terminated only when it signals a process that has not exited, and records the trigger (the first lane whose own failure set stop_on_red, or interrupt); a terminated lane is journaled cancelled with observed exit code and seconds and cancelled_by, never red, never in red_lanes; after a stop or interrupt every started future's result is collected, so the culprit is red with its own exit code; a lane that never started stays cancelled exit 0 / 0 s; a lane that exits non-zero on its own before the signal stays red; the verdict and stderr red-lane lines name only lanes journaled red; a Story 71.9 reduced lane stopped mid-segment is cancelled with that segment cancelled. ACs: two real subprocess lanes (exit 1 and sleep 30) -> sleeper cancelled, cancelled_by the failing lane, red_lanes == [failing lane], no process group left; SIGINT with two sleeping lanes -> 130, both cancelled (interrupt); existing 71.1-71.9 preflight tests pass unchanged; mutations (drop the mark; discard running futures) fail. Surface: preflight.py and its unit tests (inside Epic 71's derived epic surface); at landing reconcile this Spec and spec-pyforge-core (preflight.py). SPEC.md untouched.

Shipped behaviour: CAP-159 (FR-32) -- Story 71.3's run-and-cancel ACs ("terminated and journaled `cancelled`, and no child process is left running"; after SIGINT "no lane's process group is still alive"), Story 71.9's reduced lanes, Story 71.10's cancelled journal shape. Found by the 71.10 landing repair; read on d377581be4 in src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py. Race 1: _run_lane_in_pool checks the stop flags last at :474, then reaches Popen in _run_pixi_argv (:294) and only then register_proc (:247, called :302) adds the process under proc_lock (:248-:250); terminate_children() (:257) signals only its snapshot of active_procs (:258-:259); the stop flag is set before it runs (stop_on_red :514 then :515; _on_sigint cancel :658 then :661), so a lane registering after the snapshot starts a process nothing signals, is not in terminated_lane_tasks, is journaled from its own result, and the pool drain (:699-:709) waits for it; same for the collection check (:386). Race 1b: _subprocess_reduced_suite_lane (:309) checks only coord.cancel before each segment (:321), never stop_on_red, so the next segment starts after a stop on red. Race 2: on cancel the loop records the rest not-run and breaks with lane_exit still 0 (:317, :321-:331; the for-else :375-:389 is skipped), the lane was never marked terminated, so _run_lane_in_pool journals it ok (:508-:509) and the interrupted record keeps ok (:714-:721). 71.10's tests (tests/unit/test_preflight_concurrency.py :397-:684) stop only lanes whose processes are already running. Contract: register_proc checks cancel and stop_on_red (unless --keep-going) under proc_lock and terminates a late registration, marking the lane terminated (journaled cancelled, cancelled_by the stop trigger); the segment loop checks cancel and stop_on_red before each segment and the collection check; a lane whose segments did not all run is cancelled with the rest not-run, never ok; test-only hooks before_popen(ctx) and between_segments(ctx, index) beside subprocess_argv_for_lane. ACs: a lane held by before_popen until terminate_children() returned is killed at registration (cancelled, cancelled_by fail, no process left); an interrupt reaches a late start; a stop on red keeps segment 2 from starting (not-run, lane cancelled); SIGINT between segments -> 130, lane cancelled (interrupt); --keep-going unchanged; 71.1-71.10 tests pass unchanged; mutations (drop the registration check; drop the segment stop check; restore lane_exit 0) fail the new tests. Defect, seen 2026-10-09 in .worktrees/dispatch-pyforge-mason-25.2 (153 commits behind main, roster from before 85.8) and reproduced on 20b7e853c3: Claude Code runs .claude/hooks/pre-shell.py from $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR (.claude/settings.json:51, the primary checkout on main), but main() calls load_denial_rules(ctx.repo_root) (:1652) with ctx.repo_root = find_repo_root(cwd) (:130, :271), so MATCHERS come from the script's revision and docs/governance/guild-roster.json from the cwd's; every Bash/Edit/Write call exited 2 (:1669-:1677) with implemented-but-undeclared=['outward-git-push', 'outward-github-write', 'outward-mcp-submission', 'outward-package-submission']. A newer cwd roster fails the same way (declared-but-unimplemented), and a cwd in any other git repository exits 2 with FileNotFoundError (no roster). load_protected_deletion_prefixes (:642; called at :954, :1003) reads protected_refs through the same root and falls back to the floor, so moving only the denial read would let a pre-85.1 worktree pass the parity check and protect only main and loop/. Contract: both roster reads take _hook_install_repo_root() (:1126, Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]); the loaders keep their repo_root parameter; branch/worktree state, primary-checkout detection, uv-run and governed-path checks, is_tracked, get_guild_tasks (pixi.toml) and _commit_msg_hook stay cwd-relative; a drift in the hook's own tree still exits 2; the fail-loud tests and the protected-refs floor test run a copy of the hook inside the fixture; .cursor/hooks.json (relative python3 .claude/hooks/pre-shell.py; the hook never chdirs) needs no change. Surface: .claude/hooks/pre-shell.py and tests/scripts/test_pre_shell_hook.py only (Epic 85's [epic_surfaces] already admits both); no roster entry, reason, matcher, hook registration or AGENTS.md rule changes. SPEC.md untouched.
- (decision by user) 2026-10-10 operator ruling, verbatim: "lets record it here - that there are issues we need to open and resolve upstream - and maintain a list of upstream to-do's but leave it to the operator to decide to file, decide to track (once and issue has been opened), and decide to retire - but make the story self contained to this repo". Options chosen: (1) list home "Registry + drafts": docs/foundry/upstream-todos.yaml (one entry per item: id, source, proposed target tracker, state, draft link, issue URL once filed, decided_by, date) plus docs/foundry/upstream-drafts/<id>.md (paste-ready issue text), steward owns it; (2) seed scope "SBOM rows + session finds": the three upstream rows of docs/foundry/sbom-gaps.md (feature:conda-smithy, feature:crm, feature:python-agent-platform) plus closing staged-recipes PRs #33977/#33978 (superseded; mason Story 25.15's deferred row), the conda-recipe-manager SentinelType key leak (CFE G121) and conda-recipe-manager's parse crash on valid re-indented YAML surfaced by conda-smithy's lint (CFE G93 addendum, CFE v8.99.1, mason Story 25.2's mem0ai finding); new upstream SBOM rows join the list; (3) "Re-scope 67.4, unblock it": key 67-4-upstream-tickets-for-the-gaps-that-need-one kept, rewritten repo-only, ledger blocked -> backlog (the operator approved this flip in the ruling). Lifecycle accepted: proposed (agent, gap found with evidence) -> drafted (agent, issue text written in-repo) -> filed (operator, URL recorded) -> tracking (operator; an agent may refresh an observed field by read-only GET only) -> resolved (operator: fixed upstream and the local follow-up done or storied) | retired (operator, reason recorded). An agent records an operator state only when the operator says so in chat, with decided_by: operator and the date. No agent files, comments on or closes anything outside this repository (the session hook's outward-github-write and outward-git-push denials, Story 85.8, CAP-5's closed list).

- (decision by user) 2026-10-10 operator ruling, verbatim: "Mint both (Recommended)" -- the two fix stories proposed after the steward herald-sidecar chain lost two pr-preflight runs and doctor Story 37.1 lost its dispatch commit: this Spec's Story 88.1 (pr-preflight runs a task's depends-on twice) and spec-pyforge-marshal's Story 88.1 (commit_paths and a tracked path under an ignored directory). Evidence for this Spec, verified on origin/main 5f141b983e: src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py _expand_task (:96-:117) flattens a task's depends-on into leaf lanes (:110-:112) and also keeps the task itself as a lane when it has a cmd (:113-:114); the pool (:700-:745) submits lanes in list order with no ordering between them; the default lane argv (:438) is `pixi run --frozen -e <env> <task>` without --skip-deps (pixi 0.81.0 has it), so pixi re-runs the task's depends-on inside that lane while the same deps run as their own lanes. Three lanes of the live pr-preflight-lanes graph (36 lanes) carry depends-on: pages-check (site) -> pages-build -> docs-site-install, docs-site-sidebar; and docs-site-validate-sidebar -> docs-site-validate-sidebar-order. So in one preflight docs-site-install runs three times, pages-build twice, concurrently, into one docs-site/ and one dist/. The herald-sidecar chain's two pushes (head 71f555eeb5; .steward/preflight-runs.jsonl runs ced93a76-f521-4f09-9a52-d6e28f7da11e and fdba8d13-1ab9-4b35-bf83-ad279cd09ffe) started all four pages lanes at one offset (4.4 s and 1.8 s); pages-check went red (exit 1), its kept lane logs (/tmp/pyforge-preflight-xdz34kng/pages-check.log, /tmp/pyforge-preflight-e18_y4ey/pages-check.log) show pixi running docs-site-install, docs-site-sidebar and pages-build inside the lane and failing with "download size mismatch: pyforge-unifying-strategy_infographic_deck-2026-09-15.pptx" and then "assemble_pages: collision at herald/"; the red stopped the run and cancelled detectors-ci, pyforge-doctor-scripts-test, pyforge-doctor-aggregate-scripts-test and pages-build (cancelled_by pages-check), and both pushes then went out under PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP (.steward/preflight-skips.log 15:25:22Z, 15:31:15Z). `pixi run --frozen -e site pages-check` alone exits 0, which is also what CI runs (docsite-check.yml:57, dashboard.yml:59).
- (event) 2026-10-10: Epic 88 / Story 88.1 minted (fix, M, deps none; key 88-1-the-preflight-runs-each-task-once-after-its-dependencies, backlog; epic-88 backlog, epic-88-retrospective optional; spec spec-88-1-the-preflight-runs-each-task-once-after-its-dependencies.md, ready-for-dev). A fix of shipped behaviour under CAP-159 (FR-32; Stories 71.1, 71.2, 71.3, 71.10, 71.11): no CAP, no flag (spec-feature-flag-governance Q1), no SPEC.md change, no PRD or spine change; a new epic because Epic 71 is done. Shape chosen, per the spine: every task with a cmd stays its own lane and carries its depends-on read from pixi.toml (through cmd-less aggregates, in each dependency's declared environment); a task reached twice in one environment is one lane; a lane starts only after its dependencies finish ok and runs `pixi run --frozen --skip-deps -e <env> <task>`; lanes with no order between them stay concurrent under --jobs. AD-1: the graph is read from pixi.toml, never re-declared, and pixi still runs every task; AD-8: each task keeps its own exit code and journal row, so a failed dependency is named as itself. Rejected: dropping the dependency lanes and letting the dependent lane run them inside one pixi run, because a dependency shared by two selected lanes would still run twice and a dependency failure would be journaled as its dependent. A red dependency is the only lane reported red; its dependents never start (cancelled with cancelled_by the dependency, or not-run with blocked_by under --keep-going). Selection (Story 71.2) unchanged: PAGES_CHECK_LEAVES stays the same four lanes, selected only when docsite-check.yml paths fire, plus an assertion on their dependency edges. - (decision by user) 2026-10-10 operator ruling, verbatim: "yes mint the platform-ci-local lock fix story" -- a fix story for scripts/platform-ci-local.sh after steward 87.3's re-verification waited out the Story 63.7 lock on a dead run. Evidence, verified on origin/main 8ad2d18d69: acquire_run_lock (:57-:81) opens the lock on fd 9 (exec 9>, :63) with flock -w (default 7200 s, :61) and writes pid/checkout/stages/started to <lock>.holder (:74-:79); bash leaves fd 9 inheritable, so pg_ctl start (:122) and redis-server --daemonize yes (:129-:130) inherit it, and both lead a session of their own (live run 2026-10-10 13:24 -05:00, shell pid 2823842: postmaster and redis-server with SID == PID, /proc/<pid>/fd/9 -> /tmp/platform-ci-local.lock), so a process-group signal never reaches them and they keep the flock after the run dies. Dispatch run pyforge-steward-20261010T154144466Z-88c00f58 (87.3): the verify-fix turn was stopped by terminate_process_group at 16:19:55Z (signalled_term true, signalled_kill false, returncode 143), outcome MRS-DISP-059 after 900.18 s; the run inside it (started 11:05:24 -05:00) died without its EXIT trap stopping the services; .lock.holder kept pid=2564298 (checkout .worktrees/dispatch-pyforge-steward-87.3, stages test); fuser listed postgres 2564337-2564343 and redis-server 2564352; the re-verification started about 11:22 -05:00 waited 7200 s and exited 2 ("lock held longer than 7200s"); TERMing the orphans released the lock. Reproduced Docker-free on 8ad2d18d69 with Story 63.7's stub root and a setsid redis-server stand-in: after killpg(SIGKILL) the stand-in still held the lock and the next run (PLATFORM_CI_LOCAL_LOCK_WAIT=3) exited 2 after 3.0 s naming the dead pid; with 9>&- on that one call the next run took the lock in 0.1 s, but the dead run's stand-in kept running beside it. 63.7's test_sigkilled_holder_does_not_block_next_run missed it: its stubs exit at once and none detaches.
- (event) 2026-10-10: Epic 89 / Story 89.1 minted (fix, M, deps none; key 89-1-a-killed-platform-ci-local-run-never-blocks-the-next-one, backlog; epic-89 backlog, epic-89-retrospective optional; spec spec-89-1-a-killed-platform-ci-local-run-never-blocks-the-next-one.md, ready-for-dev). A fix of shipped behaviour under CAP-152 (Stories 63.6, 63.7; 63.7's epics AC promised that a holder that died never blocks a later run): no CAP, no flag (spec-feature-flag-governance Q1), no SPEC.md change, no PRD or spine change; a new epic because Epic 63 is done. Shape chosen, per the spine: only the run's own shell holds the lock (every service, step and helper starts with fd 9 closed; never a flock(1) wrapper, which could free the lock while the run lives); the stop_services call at the top of start_services stays the reclaim of a dead run's services in the shared work dir, now behind a /proc/<pid>/cmdline identity check before any signal (pg_ctl stop for PostgreSQL, the redis.pid kill for Redis), with a log line per stop and a bounded wait; a PostgreSQL or Redis port still held by anything the run cannot prove is refused (exit 2, nothing signalled, port, pid and command line named); a timed-out wait names the live holders and marks a dead .holder pid stale. AD-1: the flock stays the one liveness oracle, and services stop through the tools that own them, never by name or port. Rejected: refusing every leftover (each marshal verify-fix cap would leave the next verification refused until an operator killed pids) and killing whatever holds the ports (Story 63.7 forbids stopping what another run started). Marshal's kill path is the trigger, not the defect; its command, gate and budget do not change. Story 63.7's six tests stay, changed only to free ports and pid-file-writing stubs. Healed in passing: Story 63.7's epics Status line read backlog while its ledger key and spec are done; set to done. Shape chosen, per the spine: one pure helper in preflight_suite_reduction.py strips every --basetemp and cache_dir override (shlex tokens, each spelling pytest 9.1.1 accepts: --basetemp=X, --basetemp X, -o cache_dir=X, -ocache_dir=X, -o=cache_dir=X, --override-ini=cache_dir=X, --override-ini cache_dir=X) from an inherited PYTEST_ADDOPTS and keeps every other option in order; _lane_scratch_env applies it before appending the lane's own two options; collect_pytest_node_ids applies it and adds none; the reduced-lane test helper builds its env with _lane_scratch_env, as the pool does; test_collect_pytest_node_ids_raises_on_failure and the scratch mutation test assert their real failure, never exit 4. AD-1: steward edits only the variable it writes, with pytest's own tokenizer; AD-8: a lane verdict stays its suite's exit code, never a usage error from steward's env. Rejected: dropping PYTEST_ADDOPTS wholesale (loses the invoker's other options in every lane), delenv in each test (hides the defect), a pyforge-core helper for every station (a cross-station seam with its own chain). Child-pytest audit recorded in the spec (ten pyforge-* packages and src/platform): every unsafe test is steward's (9 red in a lane, 2 green for the wrong reason); doctor test_cli_bridge.py, marshal test_dispatch_supervisor_main_coverage_floor.py and platform test_helm_gate.py already drop PYTEST_ADDOPTS; no follow-up rows. - (decision by user) 2026-10-11: operator ruling on the django portal packages' lint gap (steward Story 93.1, herald Story 38.1). - (decision by claude) 2026-10-11, django portal lint gate (Story 93.1) evidence and design. Binding: CAP-153 is the lint-types capability (Story 66.1): one set of ruff / ruff-format / mypy tasks over the ten pyforge-* packages that one CI lane and pr-preflight's leg call verbatim. Running the same tasks, lane and leg over the nine django-* portal packages widens that capability's package scope; it adds no new capability, so no CAP is minted and no SPEC.md text changes here. CAP-153's wording ("the ten pyforge-* packages") stays as minted until a later bmad-spec pass folds a dated clause. Evidence on 2b5e0d649b: scripts/lint_types.py packages() globs pyforge-* only (:36-:37) and its count check expects ten (:56-:58); scripts/platform-ci-local.sh lints src/platform only (:280-:281); .github/workflows/lint-types.yml already triggers on src/shared/packages/** (:17), so a django-* change already runs the lane and pr-preflight's leg; none of the nine django-*/pyproject.toml has a [tool.ruff] table and no ruff.toml, .ruff.toml or [tool.ruff] table sits above them, so the guild ruff (0.16.9) applies its own default selection (413 rules in 38 families, line length 88, target inferred as py312 from requires-python >=3.12; ruff --show-settings and --isolated differ only in the target). Measured that way: ruff check atlas 14, doctor 7, herald 17, marshal 7, mason 10, pyforge 106, scribe 6, steward 8, warden 40 (215); ruff format --check would reformat 26 files (herald 7, marshal 1, pyforge 14, warden 4), so "nothing to reformat" does not hold. Design: (1) Config: each django-* pyproject.toml gains the [tool.ruff] table the ten pyforge-* packages share (line-length 120; extend-exclude */migrations/*.py; select E, F, W, I; ignore E501; per-file-ignores __init__.py F401 and **/tests/** E402) with one change, target-version py312, the floor of the packages' own requires-python: at py314 ruff format rewrites "except (A, B):" to the 3.14-only "except A, B:" (measured in django-doctor pulse.py:58 and django-mason apps.py:37), a SyntaxError on 3.12 and 3.13. A table per package was chosen over one shared file passed with --config: ruff reads a package's own table with no flags, so a hand run, an editor and the gate agree; a shared file resolves extend-exclude, per-file-ignores and isort's src against its own directory; and it would be a new path outside the foundry tree. The nine files share one governor, spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy (scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json), so the edit is one reconcile, not nine stations' surfaces. The platform's broader table (src/platform/pyproject.toml) was measured and rejected for now: 305 findings and 24 files to reformat, against 64 and 19. Under the chosen table, measured with --config overrides from each package directory: ruff check atlas 4, doctor 2, herald 5, marshal 1, mason 5, pyforge 35, scribe 1, steward 1, warden 10 (64: 61 I001 and 3 F401, all safe fixes); ruff format --check atlas 1 file, herald 3, marshal 1, pyforge 10, steward 1, warden 3 (19); doctor, mason and scribe already format-clean. (2) The list: NOT_YET_CLEAN, a constant in scripts/lint_types.py, maps a django-* package to the verbs it is not yet clean for (ruff, ruff-format), seeded from the measurement at implementation time (expected: all nine for ruff, six for ruff-format). Per verb: an unlisted package fails on any finding; a listed package's exit 1 is reported and does not fail; a listed package that is clean fails the verb and names the entry to remove; a listed package whose tool exits other than 0 or 1 still fails; an entry naming a missing package, a pyforge-* package or an unknown verb is could-not-run (exit 2). A steward test pins the seeded map as a ceiling, so an entry can leave but never return. ruff-format --fix skips a listed package, so it never rewrites another station's package. (3) The ten-package count check counts pyforge-* alone; fewer than nine django-* packages is could-not-run. (4) A django-* package with no [tool.ruff] table is a finding, so ruff never falls back to its defaults. (5) mypy stays pyforge-* only: mypy over the django packages is out of scope for this effort (recorded at mint). (6) Wiring unchanged: the lint-types task, the CI lane and pr-preflight's leg call the same tasks; the lines that say "the ten pyforge-* packages" are fixed in the same story: lint_types.py's docstring, the lint-types.yml header comment, pixi.toml's ruff / ruff-format / ruff-format-fix descriptions and their comment block (docs/how-to/pixi-tasks.md regenerated), and AGENTS.md's Lint and types line. Tests live in pyforge-steward's own tests: unit tests drive the list semantics through a stubbed runner (the steward env carries no ruff), and a meta-test reads the nine live tables and the ceiling. No django-* source line changes in this story; each station's own fix story clears its findings, herald first (spec-pyforge-herald:CAP-55, Story 38.1).

- (decision by user) 2026-10-11: operator's ask for steward Story 94.1, verbatim: "Do this task here: Stop test_oidc_identity leaking structlog config". The defect it names was found the same day by warden Story 16.1's fix pass: a new platform test asserting a task_failed event rendered by the production JSON renderer failed only in the full platform-ci-local -- --test run (warden worked around it on its own branch by pinning that test's structlog configuration; the fix here does not depend on that branch).
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-11, structlog leak (Story 94.1) evidence and design. Owner: this Spec, CAP-87 (telemetry, absorbed from spec-platform-fifteen-factors' telemetry capability; Story 16.3 built the platform's structlog configuration, src/platform/config/observability/logging.py configure_structlog :57-:70, run once when the settings load, config/settings/base.py :406). scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json names spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy as the only governor of src/platform/tests/test_oidc_identity.py and src/platform/conftest.py, so the landing reconciles that Spec's memlog. Evidence on e4ab638bb6: test_sync_ignores_unmatched_groups_and_logs (:77-:92) calls structlog.configure(processors=[KeyValueRenderer()], wrapper_class=make_filtering_bound_logger(0), logger_factory=PrintLoggerFactory(), cache_logger_on_first_use=False) at :80-:85, never restores it, and asserts nothing about logs. A scratch probe test (platform-ci-test's python, a scratch PostgreSQL 17 and Redis): run alone it saw the platform configuration (stdlib LoggerFactory, stdlib BoundLogger, cache_logger_on_first_use True, nine processors ending in ProcessorFormatter.wrap_for_formatter) and its event reached caplog as a dict; run after tests/test_oidc_identity.py, or after that one test, it saw PrintLoggerFactory, BoundLoggerFilteringAtNotset, cache_logger_on_first_use False and one KeyValueRenderer, and its event printed k='v' event='probe_event' to stdout with caplog empty (1 failed, 18 passed); run after the file with that test deselected, the platform configuration (18 passed). The test's own configure is order-dependent too: run alone, the mapper's authorization.unknown_group_claim warning printed as key-value; run after a test that had already used the mapper's logger, it went through the platform JSON renderer, because that logger cached the platform configuration on first use. A full-suite survey (a scratch plugin comparing structlog.get_config() before and after every test, putting it back after a change): 1208 passed, 13 skipped, and the OIDC test is the only test that changes structlog's configuration, so no test depends on the leaked configuration. Five tests replace the root logger's handlers by running django.setup() again, which re-applies settings.LOGGING through dictConfig: test_cloudevents_redis_broker.py's test_execute_supervised_run_leaves_no_celery_result_key, test_consume_events_command_runs_one_pass_for_station, test_enumerate_dlq_returns_quarantined_and_empty and test_consume_events_binds_the_broker_through_the_ad10_guard (through _ensure_django :1176-:1179, and through mcp_host.app's _real_station_apps on reload), and test_herald_webhook_sidecar.py's test_mcp_host_sidecar_herald_dependency_missing_not_blamed_on_herald. They leave structlog's configuration alone and re-apply the platform's own LOGGING; what they drop is pytest's session handlers (live logging and --log-file), which no platform lane passes. Recorded here, out of this story's scope. No other in-process structlog.configure, dictConfig, basicConfig or root-logger change exists in the suite (the two in test_herald_portal_without_herald.py run in child processes). Chosen shape: (1) a yield fixture in the test module saves structlog.get_config() with its processors list copied (structlog.testing.capture_logs edits that list in place) and calls structlog.configure(**saved) after the test, pass or fail; the test's own configure call and its assertions stay; (2) an autouse fixture in src/platform/conftest.py, the suite's only conftest (there is no tests/conftest.py), records the configuration once per session before the first test and compares it after each test; on a difference it restores the baseline first, so one leak cannot spread, then fails that test at teardown naming its node id and the keys that differ. Rejected: restoring with configure_structlog() (new processor objects, so not the configuration the session started with, and loggers that cached the old chain keep it); deleting the test's configure call (it changes what the test does, and the ask is to stop the leak); structlog.reset_defaults() (structlog's defaults are not the platform's configuration); guarding the stdlib root logger too (the five django.setup() re-runs would go red, and they re-apply the platform's own LOGGING). Bound: CAP-87, no CAP minted, SPEC.md unchanged; only the two test-harness files change.

Verification Commands carry only steward's verify_commands. - (decision by user) 2026-10-11: operator's ask for steward Story 95.1, verbatim: "can we add a story to update to python 3.14 - configuration targets Python 3.12 on purpose: targeting 3.14 makes ruff". Relayed with its scope: raise the nine django-* requires-python floors and the ruff/mypy targets of the django-* tables and src/platform to 3.14; extend scripts/target_version_check.py to cover them, derived from the pixi pin; apply what ruff then produces, the PEP 758 rewrites included; Deps S-93.1; and first verify that nothing runs this code below 3.14.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-11, Python 3.14 floor (Story 95.1) verification, rationale and design. Owner: this Spec, CAP-153 (a target-version registry check reds a package whose target drifts from the interpreter; Story 93.1 extends the lint gate to the django-* packages under the same CAP). Governors: scripts/target_version_check.py is this Spec's; the nine django-* pyproject.toml files, their src/ and src/platform are spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy's (pap:CAP-5, the platform environment is 3.14-bound). Verified on e4ab638bb6 that nothing runs this code below 3.14: every environment in pixi.lock (36, on every platform each lists) resolves python 3.14.7, including platform-ci-test, platform-dev, python-agent-platform, dbgpt-sidecar, mcp-host and pyforge-container; the three images build from those environments (src/platform/Containerfile python-agent-platform, compose/dbgpt dbgpt-sidecar, compose/mcp-host mcp-host) and start through their shell hooks, and the root Containerfile builds pyforge-container; platform-ci runs on platform-ci-test and platform-deploy on platform-dev; every setup-python in .github/workflows is 3.14 and none runs src/platform or a django-* package (pyforge-pip-install pip-installs pyforge-core and pyforge-steward only); the only pyforge recipe, recipes/pyforge-estate-catalog, is noarch generic catalog data with no portal or platform code; the django-* packages are composed by PYTHONPATH and pytest pythonpath, never pip-installed, so no tool reads their requires-python today. Configuration that still names 3.12 but that no lane runs: src/platform/.python-version (3.12), src/platform/.pre-commit-config.yaml (default_language_version python3.12; the root .pre-commit-config.yaml is the one installed), src/platform/.readthedocs.yml (python 3.12, installing requirements/local.txt, which Story 16.1 retired), and src/platform/ingest/github_projects/README.md (says the dlt env is Python 3.12; python-agent-platform resolves 3.14.7 with dlt 1.30.0). Rationale for the old targets: src/platform/pyproject.toml's ruff py312 and mypy 3.12 came with Story 10.1's render (4febffd7bf, 2026-08-14) and the django-* requires-python >=3.12 with the portal packages (92365511b1 and 2641d098c2, 2026-08-24), when spec-python-agent-platform's pap:AD-8 pinned the platform environment to python 3.12.*: "py3.12 env-scoped now, 3.14 as a release gate ... the pin flips to 3.14 the release after the langflow-base bcrypt ==4.0.1 prerequisite clears". Story 43.6 flipped it (done 2026-09-03; the PRD's 2026-09-09 currency note records the floor 3.12.* to 3.14.*), but the targets were never raised; Story 66.1 left src/platform's lane unchanged and AGENTS.md records "(py312 target)". Story 93.1 sets the django-* tables to py312 because at py314 ruff format writes PEP 758 except A, B:, a SyntaxError on the 3.12 and 3.13 its packages declare. With no runtime below 3.14 and the floors raised together, that reason no longer holds. Measured on e4ab638bb6 with ruff 0.16.9 (read-only; the django-* tables simulated with 93.1's AC2 table through --config): django-* ruff check findings are identical at py312 and py314 (64), and ruff format at py314 differs from py312 by exactly 13 PEP 758 rewrites in 9 files (django-doctor pulse.py:58; django-mason apps.py:37; django-pyforge assertion/jwks.py:102, assertion/middleware.py:53, assertion/views.py:42, celery_probes.py:36 and :46, mcp_dual_era.py:140, :154 and :174, queues.py:159, rate_limit.py:151 and :275); handlers with an as clause stay parenthesised. src/platform at py314: ruff format rewrites 4 sites (tests/test_circuits_trip_on_async_too.py:47, tests/test_object_storage_client.py:52, tests/test_object_store_seam.py:85, tests/test_openfeature_file_flags.py:143); ruff check finds 11, one safe fix (UP035, platformapp/front_door/celery_task_backend.py:14, ParamSpec from typing) and ten TC001/TC002/TC003 imports used only in annotations (config/settings/base.py:8 and :14, platformapp/conftest.py:3, platformapp/users/tests/factories.py:1, test_forms.py:6, test_models.py:1, test_urls.py:4, test_views.py:11 and :15, platformapp/users/views.py:3), whose fix ruff marks unsafe because 3.14 evaluates annotations lazily; mypy platformapp config tests at --python-version 3.14 finds no issue in 185 files, as at 3.12; pytest 9.1.1 collects a fixture whose annotation names a TYPE_CHECKING-only import. Design: the nine requires-python become >=3.14; the nine django-* tables and src/platform's ruff target py314 and its mypy 3.14; target_version_check.py reads three surfaces from the one pin (the ten pyforge-* as today, plus the requires-python floor of every package with a [project] table; the nine django-* ruff target-version and requires-python, no mypy table, since 93.1 keeps mypy on pyforge-*; src/platform's ruff and mypy targets), with fewer than ten or nine packages or no src/platform/pyproject.toml as could-not-run. Code: src/platform takes everything ruff produces at py314 (the 4 rewrites, the UP035 fix, the ten imports moved under TYPE_CHECKING, each reviewed); the django-* packages take only the 13 PEP 758 rewrites, because their other findings belong to each station's own fix story under the 2026-10-11 ruling "Gate all, clean herald (Recommended)", so 93.1's NOT_YET_CLEAN does not change (the rewrites clean no listed package; doctor and mason, not listed for ruff-format, stay format-clean). The 3.12 residue in src/platform (.python-version, .pre-commit-config.yaml, .readthedocs.yml, the ingest README line) and AGENTS.md's "(py312 target)" are corrected in the same change. Rejected: keeping py312 (it holds the code to a runtime nothing uses); running ruff format and ruff check --fix over the whole django-* trees (it would do the stations' fix stories and empty the not-yet-clean list); a src/platform requires-python (it has no [project] table). Bound: CAP-153 (with pap:CAP-5 for the platform environment), no CAP minted, SPEC.md unchanged.

- (decision by user) 2026-10-11: operator's ask for steward Story 96.1, verbatim: "Do this task here: Make platform-ci-local sync a stale env". The defect it names surfaced the same day in steward 91.1's worktree (.worktrees/dispatch-pyforge-steward-91.1): after a merge of main, platform-ci-local -- --test failed 15 tests in src/platform/tests/test_warden_fleet_inventory.py with "No module named 'pyforge.warden'" until a hand-run pixi install --frozen -e platform-ci-test.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-11, platform-ci-local stale env (Story 96.1) evidence and design. Owner: this Spec, CAP-152 (platform-ci-local is a guild task that provisions the platform envs it uses; Stories 63.6, 63.7, 89.1). scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json names this Spec as the only governor of scripts/platform-ci-local.sh and of both platform-ci-local test files. Defect at 485d325d56: ensure_envs (:244-:250) loops over platform-ci-test, platform-dev and pyforge-warden whatever stages run, and installs one only when its bin/python is missing ([ -x "$ROOT/.pixi/envs/$e/bin/python" ] || pixi install --frozen -e "$e", :247); the stages then call those envs' binaries by path (CIT/DEV/WRD, :224-:226), and pixi run -e pyforge-guild syncs only the Guild env, so an env older than pixi.lock is never synced. Evidence verified: the 91.1 worktree created platform-ci-test at 18:53:50 (conda-meta/pixi_env_prefix); merged main at 20:22 (548b976af3), bringing warden 16.1's dae6101829 (pixi.toml gained pyforge-warden as a path dependency of feature platform-ci-test, pixi.lock +76 lines); pyforge-warden-0.1.0-pyh4616a5c_0 entered the env at 20:43:10 (its conda-meta record) by the hand-run pixi install --frozen -e platform-ci-test; the failing run's log is gone (the work dir is overwritten per run, and no dispatch journal holds it). Measured on 485d325d56 (pixi 0.81.0, warm cache): pixi install --frozen -e <env> on a current env 0.20-0.29 s each (platform-ci-test five runs 0.25-0.29 s); nested in pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild with --manifest-path 0.28-0.33 s; with the env's conda-meta/pixi environment_lock_file_hash set stale by hand 0.24 s, and pixi rewrote it to the lock's hash; into an empty prefix platform-ci-test 2.03 s, platform-dev 1.28 s, pyforge-warden 0.37 s. pixi install --help (0.81.0): --frozen installs the lock as it is and never updates it; --locked is the flag that aborts on a lock behind pixi.toml, so a --frozen failure means the lock's content cannot be installed (unknown environment, fetch failure, broken prefix), never lock drift; CI's test job installs platform-ci-test frozen (.github/actions/platform-test-setup). Test hazard: under pixi run, PIXI_PROJECT_MANIFEST names the checkout's pixi.toml and pixi honours it from any cwd (checked from an empty dir), and Story 63.7's tests copy the runner's environment with no pixi stand-in, so an unscoped real pixi install would sync the real checkout's envs from a unit test; with --manifest-path naming the stub root's absent pixi.toml, pixi exits 1 (unable to canonicalize) and touches nothing. Chosen shape: (1) ensure_envs drops the -x guard and runs pixi install --frozen --manifest-path "$ROOT/pixi.toml" -e <env> for each env the selected stages use, once each, in a fixed order (test: platform-ci-test, platform-dev; images: none; container: platform-dev; promotion: platform-ci-test, pyforge-warden); pixi alone judges staleness (AD-1, AD-5); (2) it stays where it is: after acquire_run_lock, before start_services and any stage, fd 9 closed; (3) it reads environment_lock_file_hash from .pixi/envs/<env>/conda-meta/pixi before and after (read only) and prints env <name>: installed|synced|current (checked when no hash is recorded after a successful install), also as an INFO row in results.tsv, with each install's output in $WORK/env-sync-<name>.log; (4) a non-zero install (127 for a missing pixi included) exits 2 (63.7's usage-or-environment code) naming the env and pixi's exit code with the log's last 25 lines, before any later env, service or stage; (5) tests: a recording pixi stand-in first on PATH (the shared _prepare_stub_root gains a minimal one that logs and exits 0, the only change to 63.7's and 89.1's files) and a new test module test_platform_ci_local_env_sync.py; a script-text test refuses pixi update/add/lock/upgrade/remove/reinstall, --locked and any unfrozen install. Rejected: a script-side staleness probe (pixi's hash format is internal; computing it reimplements pixi, and pixi's own check costs 0.2-0.3 s); --locked (lock drift is the lock gates' to catch, CI's test job is frozen); pixi run -e <env> per step (rewrites every step line, pays activation each step); $PIXI_EXE (bypasses PATH, the tests' seam); an opt-out env var (nothing asks for one). Out of scope, recorded: doctor's platform-policy-suite source (pyforge.doctor.sources.platform_policy) also runs platform-ci-test's interpreter by path without syncing it; that is doctor's code. Bound: CAP-152, no CAP minted, SPEC.md unchanged; pixi.toml, pixi.lock and marshal's command unchanged.

Relayed with its scope: the station-tests workflow decides which suites run, its changes job sets steward=true only when src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward or the shared surface moves, and its on.paths omits scripts/lint_types.py and every django-* package but django-pyforge; herald Story 38.1 (merge e0b8ceae0c) moved scripts/lint_types.py and django-herald, CI never ran steward-test, and test_lint_types_django_tables.py went red on main unnoticed (that test is fixed by Story 97.1, out of scope here). The relay asks for each station's filter to cover every outside path its tests read, derived rather than hand-listed where feasible, with scripts/lint_types.py and scripts/target_version_check.py firing steward and each django-<station> package firing its station and steward, and pr-preflight's lane selection kept in step.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-12, station tests run when what they read changes (Story 98.1) evidence and design. Owner: this Spec, CAP-159 (Story 71.2: pr-preflight reads which lanes run from .github/workflows/*.yml and runs each workflow's own changes job, never a second list), with spec-pyforge-core CAP-8 / CAP-10 (the station-tests lane and its full-ref base) and spec-pyforge-testing-charter CAP-3 (testing-kit-test's gate) as Kinship, as 71.2 bound them. Evidence on 5beef7c9eb, the workflow's own changes shell run against one-path fixture diffs (GITHUB_BASE_REF=main, base at refs/remotes/origin/main): scripts/lint_types.py, src/shared/packages/django-herald/src/x.py, scripts/target_version_check.py, .github/workflows/lint-types.yml, scripts/fleet_scan.py and scripts/cfe_rebuild_guard_check.py fire no on.pull_request.paths entry and no job; django-pyforge and src/platform/config/flags.json fire testing_kit only; pyforge-steward/src fires core and steward. Measured map (scope-aware AST resolution of Path(__file__)-anchored reads in each suite's tests, plus a grep for the django portal reads the resolver misses; tracked paths only), outside reads not covered today: atlas: every .github/workflows file, flags.json, django-atlas, pyforge-doctor/src, pyforge-scribe/src. doctor: .claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/scripts, .github/workflows/detectors.yml, docs/governance/guild-roster.json, scripts/{apply_verification_verdicts,coverage_gate,flag_gate_check,fleet_scan,spec_surface_check}.py, src/platform/config, django-pyforge/src, django-doctor. herald: .github/workflows/docsite-check.yml, docs/map.yaml, docs/foundry/capability-ledger.yaml, docsite/ (build.py, README, fonts, content/dossier.yml), django-herald, pyforge-steward/src. marshal: _bmad-output/harness-profiles/cursor.toml, _bmad/custom/*.toml, _bmad/scripts/{bmad_tea_playwright,memlog}.py, scripts/{coverage_gate,fleet_picture,promote_sprint_status,spec_surface_check}.py, src/platform/compose/compose.yml, src/platform/pyproject.toml, django-pyforge/src, django-marshal. mason: scripts/cfe_rebuild_guard_check.py, django-mason, and its task's .claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/tests/meta/test_cli_tool_parity.py. scribe: AGENTS.md, src/platform/**/*.py, src/platform/db/changelog/changes. steward: every .github/workflows file, .claude/hooks/session-start.sh, .cursor/environment.json, scripts/{lint_types,detectors}.py, scripts/platform-ci-local.sh, flags.json, all nine django-*/pyproject.toml, django-pyforge/src, django-steward, pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/dispatch.py. warden: django-warden. core: all of src/ and scripts/ (preserve_refs sole-grammar scan), flags.json. testing-kit: covered. Planning content read live by doctor and marshal (deferred-work ledgers, epics, sprint ledgers, every station's marshal-policy.toml and specs, test-architecture.md, archive/**), by atlas (recipes/) and herald (a steward spec companion) is also outside every filter. scripts/target_version_check.py is read by no suite test (tests/scripts/test_lint_types_gate.py reads it, in detectors.yml's always-on pyforge-doctor-scripts-test). pr-preflight's preflight_ci runs the same changes shell, so it skips what CI skips; its selection test test_testing_kit_branch_selects_only_the_kit_not_core_or_stations asserts a django-pyforge change selects no station suite, which the fix makes false. actionlint is in no pixi env. Chosen shape: (1) the workflow's changes job gives each of the ten suite jobs the tracked inputs its tests read, and on.pull_request.paths / on.push.paths (identical) fire for all of them; the shared surface, herald's presentations/docs-site and atlas's inventory quartet stay; steward's list also carries scripts/target_version_check.py as a declared extra, per the relay. (2) A steward meta-test derives each job's reads from its tests and from the outside test paths its pixi task collects (pixi.toml read, never copied), evaluates the workflow's own changes shell on a fixture diff of each read path, and fails naming the job and the path when the job's output is not true or on.pull_request.paths does not fire; a declared exemption map (job, glob, reason) holds planning content out (_bmad-output/projects/*/planning-artifacts/**, archive/**, docs/dreams/**, recipes/**, .claude/memory/**) so doctor, marshal and atlas do not run on every chain or recipe PR, and it may only shrink (a subset of its seed, Story 97.1's rule). (3) The meta-test's own inputs are every suite's tests, so steward's filter carries src/shared/packages/pyforge-*/tests/**: a station PR that adds a new outside read runs steward-test and is caught there, not later on main. (4) pr-preflight changes no code; its selection tests gain a django-herald-only fixture (selects steward and herald) and a scripts/lint_types.py-only fixture (selects steward), and the testing-kit fixture's assertion follows the new rule. (5) core's gate follows its measured reads (src/ and scripts/), and the workflow comment that calls django-* packages deliberately not core inputs is corrected. Rejected: hand-adding only scripts/lint_types.py and the django-* packages (closes the reported case, leaves the other measured misses and the next one unseen); a scanner run inside the CI changes job (a scanner bug would silently skip suites; committed lists checked by a test fail loudly instead); widening every job to everything (all ten suites on every PR); hosting the derivation in pyforge-core's tests, where core-test already fires on any station change (cheaper, but the file would be spec-pyforge-core's chain, not this station's; recorded as the alternative if steward-test's wider trigger costs too much). Open for the operator before dispatch: the planning-content exemption (the residual risk is a planning edit, e.g. a marshal-policy.toml typo, reddening doctor or marshal on main unseen) and steward-test running on station test edits. Bound: CAP-159, no CAP minted, SPEC.md unchanged; coverage-gates.yml, pyforge-pip-install.yml and the other workflows unchanged.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-12, how the two rulings were applied to Story 98.1 (spec-98-1-station-tests-run-when-what-they-read-changes.md, amended before dispatch). Ruling 1 ("Trigger on planning too"): the planning-content exemption (_bmad-output/projects/*/planning-artifacts/**, archive/**, docs/dreams/**, recipes/**, .claude/memory/**) and its shrink-only AC are dropped, and no exemption list remains: the measurement found no tracked read its test does not depend on, so an empty shrink-only list would only invite one; a read the filter should not carry is removed from the test, and any exemption would need a new ruling. Planning reads trigger like any other: at minimum doctor carries _bmad-output/projects/*/planning-artifacts/**, _bmad-output/projects/*/README.md, archive/**, docs/dreams/** and recipes/*/recipe.yaml; marshal carries _bmad-output/projects/*/planning-artifacts/** and .claude/memory/MEMORY.md; herald carries the steward spec companion spec-foundry-regenerate-not-fold/case-list.md; atlas carries recipes/**. New measurement while applying it: 32 test files hand the bare repo root to the code under test (call arguments resolving to the root, on 5beef7c9eb): doctor 19 (chain, board, one-chain, dreams-hygiene, deferred-work, docs-shelf, general-docs, hygiene, status-body and factory gatherers, e.g. test_live_tree_dream_readme_missing_count over docs/dreams), herald 5, marshal 5 (the test-architecture generator's live-tree smoke reads every station's epics.md and tests), steward 2, atlas 1. The static scan cannot see what that code reads, and those are where most planning reads happen, so the meta-test now derives the set of root-handoff test files and requires a per-job ROOT_HANDOFF_INPUTS declaration (file -> globs + reason) whose keys equal the derived set; declared globs get the same fire check; the declaration only adds. AC2's floor gains planning rows and three handoff files; AC3 covers declared globs and says no read is exempt; AC4 is now the handoff declaration; AC5 gains three mutations (docs/dreams/** dropped from doctor's list, _bmad-output/projects/*/planning-artifacts/** dropped from marshal's, a handoff file removed from its declaration). The CI cost is recorded in the story with the option text quoted: doctor-test and marshal-test run on nearly every chain PR, a recipe PR runs doctor-test and atlas-test. Ruling 2 ("In steward (Recommended)"): the meta-test stays at src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/meta/test_station_tests_trigger_map.py; hosting it in pyforge-core's tests (cheaper, core-test already fires on any station change, but spec-pyforge-core's chain) is recorded as the rejected alternative; steward's list keeps src/shared/packages/pyforge-*/tests/**. - (decision by user) 2026-10-12, operator rulings Q1-Q8, all the plan's recommended answers: Q1 per-surface repository variables with rendered if: fragments, not one JSON CI_PAUSES variable plus a gate job per workflow (a gate job costs runner minutes on every run; metadata belongs in the tracked registry); Q2 a workflow-level switch on every push/PR/schedule job plus a job-level switch only where a cost: high row exists; Q3 pr-preflight honors CI pauses by default, --ignore-ci-pauses runs them; Q4 retire the four staged-recipes-linter workflows, keep test-all and kedro-viz-publish as paused-manually rows with a reason and review_by; Q5 PAUSE_ALL_CI covers the merge gates too, behind a warning and an explicit flag; Q6 a required-status-checks ruleset on main stays a separate decision; Q7 the six existing variable names are grandfathered, not renamed; Q8 review_by 2026-11-01 for both live pauses.
- (capability by claude) 2026-10-12: mint CAP-166 (extends CAP-159, CAP-154; works with CAP-131) -- every CI surface the operator can pause is declared once, switched by one convention, visible in one place and judged. intent: a tracked registry docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml (with a JSON Schema) declares every GitHub Actions surface and local twin -- workflow, job, scheduled run, local stage -- with its switch, switch class (pause | enable), owner, cost class, gate flag, state (live | paused | opt-in-off | paused-manually | retired), paused_since, paused_by, reason, review_by, local twin and canary; the convention is PAUSE_<WORKFLOW> on every push/PR/schedule job, PAUSE_<WORKFLOW>_<JOB> where a cost: high row exists, ENABLE_* for opt-ins, PAUSE_ALL_CI as master, workflow_dispatch always exempt, six existing names grandfathered and mapped; a script renders each job's if: fragment from the registry and a repo-scope detector in detectors-ci judges it (a vars.X no row names, a row whose job lacks its fragment, a naming violation, a comparison other than the literal 'true', a disabled_manually workflow without a paused-manually or retired row); steward ci status reads the registry, gh variable list, the Actions API (workflow state, recent runs) and pending deployments and prints one row per surface with a verdict (live, paused, opt-in-off, paused-manually, retired, drift, stuck, overdue), refusing an unauthenticated read at a 0/60 rate limit; steward ci vars --refresh writes .steward/ci-vars.json; steward ci pause|resume --reason --review-by is a dry run until --yes, then sets the variable, updates the row, journals .steward/ci-pauses.log, captures to team memory, and refuses a gate: true row without --i-am-removing-a-merge-gate; preflight_ci resolves vars.* from the snapshot (stale after 24 h, then the registry's declared state, then today's unknown-runs rule) and journals each lane it skips as paused in CI with switch, since and review_by; pr-preflight banners and honors paused lanes; platform-ci-local and the guild image build banner and run; a weekly canary workflow builds each paused cost: high surface once; a how-to and one AGENTS.md pointer; the linter family retires, test-all and kedro-viz-publish get paused-manually rows, stale ACTIONS_ENABLED citations are struck. success: the detector exits 0 on main after seeding and reds each planted fixture; preflight_ci evaluates every workflow with no unknown site once the snapshot exists; a dispatch with PAUSE_ALL_CI=true still runs; ci status on a recorded gh fixture yields the expected verdict per row including drift, stuck (the 2026-10-06 waiting-deploy replay) and overdue; ci pause without --yes makes no gh call; with --yes the fixture asserts the gh variable set argv, the registry diff, the journal line and the capture text; pr-preflight journals a paused lane as skipped and --ignore-ci-pauses runs it; the canary has one green run; no tracked doc cites ACTIONS_ENABLED as live evidence.

scripts/pin_policy_check.py (repo-scope, detectors-ci and pr-preflight) reds an undeclared hold, a row with no declaration, a stale pointer, and under --diff <base> a lost cap, a flattened channel table, a lowered floor or a moved never-move package, naming package, feature, before and after; scripts/pin_policy_restore.py rewrites each named declaration to its policy clause at the new floor, keeps the comment, re-locks with pixi lock, re-checks, and refuses by name when the lock cannot solve; pyforge steward estate plan|refresh|reconcile|verify|status, behind pyforge.steward.estate_refresh: plan runs pixi upgrade --dry-run --json through the policy, prints the moves, the BMAD table (pinned / installed / latest, doctor's reader, fail-open) and days since the last journal (--due reds past 30 days); refresh refuses on a dirty tree or on main, renders the command, runs pixi upgrade --pinning-strategy latest-up --json, the guard, the restore and pixi lock, journals, and runs pixi update only under --transitive (operator-only, a session_denials row); reconcile regenerates every derived surface by its own generator -- environment.yaml by the exporter, the library catalog's floors by a new deterministic llms_full_check.py --fix-floors, the BMAD-estate catalog by scribe catalog bmad-estate --write, the env matrix by pixi_env_matrix.py --update, the generated docs by their generators, the pixi-version sites by bump-pixi-version when the pixi floor moved, the bmad-suite metapackage by generate-bmad-suite when a bmad-* floor moved -- runs every check by exit code, then appends one memlog event line and one scoped stamp per Spec the surface baseline names for the moved files (read from scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json, never a list); a moved bmad-method or bmad-* floor is handed to upgrade bmad-core pre-flight (CAP-9) and pin-fan-out (CAP-12) always, to the deliberate apply (CAP-10/14/16) and prove-landed (CAP-13) under --apply-bmad, and to suite pipeline-truth (CAP-21) with suite advance (CAP-22) named as the next operator step; an installed core below the pinned floor reds verify; one tracked journal per run under .steward/estate-refresh/<date>.json validating against a shipped frozen schema; one how-to, one AGENTS.md pointer, the release-cadence runbook's step 7 pointing at estate refresh; the first refresh lands through the path, superseding the 2026-10-13 hand batch. success: pin_policy_check.py --diff over the 2026-10-13 batch (a fixture pair) yields exactly nine findings (five lost caps, two flattened tables, two lowered floors) and zero on a floor-only move;
