- (decision by user) 2026-10-14: operator approval for steward Story 101.1, relayed verbatim: "The operator approved this fix: the platform-ci-local test stubs leak processes, and the holder scan is slow." Scope relayed with it: the stub pg_ctl and redis-server stand-ins exec their long-running process, so the pid they record is the one the stop path kills; every test teardown stops what it started; a regression test proves that no process from the test tmp dir survives, scoped by a unique marker and never by a host-wide pgrep; _pids_holding_path uses fuser or lsof when present, keeps a /proc walk as the fallback, forks no readlink per descriptor, and keeps its output contract (one pid per line, sorted, unique); PLATFORM_CI_LOCAL_LOCK_WAIT keeps its meaning. Numbering ruled: Epic 101 / Story 101.1, a fix under the existing platform-ci-local CAP (CAP-152), with no new CAP and no flag. Also ruled: record, with no implementation, an open deferred-work row DW-steward-101-1-1 (owner steward, severity medium): steward ci status --json has no row for the PAUSE_ALL_CI master switch.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence and design for the platform-ci-local stub leak and holder scan (Story 101.1). Owner: this Spec, CAP-152 (Stories 63.6, 63.7, 89.1, 96.1). scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json names this Spec as the only governor of scripts/platform-ci-local.sh and of the three test_platform_ci_local_*.py files. The leak: on 2026-10-14 the host held 6,222 orphaned "sleep 999999" processes, reparented to systemd --user after about 23 hours of test runs; the operator cleared them. _prepare_stub_root (tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_concurrency.py, about :83-:89 and :112-:118) writes $pgdata/postgres and $rdir/redis-server as bash scripts whose body is "sleep 999999". It starts them with setsid and records $!, which is the wrapper bash, in postmaster.pid and redis.pid. stop_services kills that pid, and the sleep child survives. Every run that starts services therefore leaks two. test_platform_ci_local_killed_holder.py adds a third: its lock_holder.sh runs "sleep 999999" under a bash that the test SIGKILLs by pid. Measured at e0ad43f18c: test_lock_wait_timeout_names_holder took 33.33 s and test_lock_wait_names_live_holder_and_stale_record 2.89 s. Both passed and left 3 such sleeps. Only their environment found them; their cmdline is "sleep 999999" and names no tmp path. The holder scan was measured at e0ad43f18c with one process holding a lock. The readlink-per-descriptor /proc walk took 74.6 s at 550 processes (25,611 descriptors) and 110.6 s at 6,549. The waiter report calls the scan twice, so at that load it outruns the tests 120 s subprocess timeout. fuser took 0.40 s and 1.12 s; lsof -t 5.6 s and 6.1 s; a bash -ef /proc walk, which forks nothing per descriptor, 0.48 s and 0.84 s. fuser first landed at 07fa34a20d, in the 99.7 dispatch on 2026-10-14, with no story; this story chains it. Also found: the waiter lists itself and its own pipeline helpers as holders, because fd 9 stays open in the waiter while it scans. A manual run listed 6 such pids. Design: (1) the stubs exec -a "$0 $*" sleep 999999. The recorded pid is then the long-running process, and its cmdline still satisfies _is_our_postgres and _is_our_redis. lock_holder.sh execs its sleep, and the killed-run helper kills the holder process group in teardown. (2) An autouse fixture in the three platform-ci-local test modules fails any test that leaves a process carrying the environment entry PLATFORM_CI_STUB_MARKER=<tmp_path>. It kills only those processes. Every process a test starts carries the marker. A named regression test runs one start/stop cycle and checks the same. (3) _pids_holding_path keeps fuser and otherwise walks /proc/<pid>/fd/* with the bash builtin -ef test (same device and inode), then sort -un. It does not use lsof, which took 6 s at this load, slower than the walk it would replace. Deviation from the brief recorded: "fuser or lsof" is met by fuser. (4) A waiter that times out closes fd 9 before it reports and scans once. Unchanged: PLATFORM_CI_LOCAL_LOCK_WAIT and its 7200 s default, exit codes, the contention report format, and the stage, step and summary output.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, CAP-168 design with evidence. (a) The file move: .github/workflows/dashboard.yml sits on spec-pyforge-herald's surface: today (its SPEC.md) and in scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json under pyforge-herald/spec-pyforge-herald; docsite-check.yml has no governor in the baseline; kedro-viz-publish.yml is pyforge-atlas's; docs/how-to/deploy-the-docs-site-to-github-enterprise-pages.md is pyforge-doctor's. The rename puts .github/workflows/pages.yml on this Spec's surface: now, declared ahead of the file (the scoped stamp follows Story 102.1, which creates it); the herald SPEC drop is the coordinator's; Story 102.5's how-to edit is a co-governor reconcile on spec-pyforge-doctor's memlog at implementation. (b) The sites the rename must move, each in a story's surface: scripts/pixi_version_registry.py registers 'dashboard.yml setup-pixi' and 'docsite-check.yml setup-pixi'; src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/meta/test_invariants.py reads dashboard.yml; tests/unit/test_preflight_selection.py uses docsite-check.yml as the oracle for the pages-check lane (Story 71.2); tests/unit/test_ci_status.py fixtures name dashboard.yml; docs/how-to/pause-ci.md, docs/reference/github-workflows.md and herald's how-tos name the file. (c) The no-op skip's state is the live site, not a repository variable or an Actions cache: a variable write is a second outward write path beside CAP-166's one and needs a token with variables scope on the deploy job; a cache is branch-scoped and evicted after seven days unused; the live <SITE_URL>/build.json is read-only, needs no permission, and is the same read steward pages status makes. The hash excludes build.json and the built-from footer (design-thinking Key Feature 6). (d) The release asset's home is a rolling pre-release pages-site on the deploying commit, replaced each deploy with --clobber: a release per deploy would mint about 130 releases a month at the replayed rate, and no CalVer tag exists yet (herald M3's /changes/ waits on one); the tag is outside the protected-refs rulesets (archive/**, preserve/**, rescue/**) and moving it forward along main orphans no commit; the deploy job alone gets contents: write. (e) FR placement: FR-41, the PRD's next free id (named by its 2026-10-13 currency section), in a new section of the PRD's currency reconciliation; FR-8..FR-11 (Epic 2's deploy dashboard) are superseded in part -- FR-8 (build) and FR-11 (status) move onto pages build|status, FR-9 (reconciled push) and FR-10 (dry-run) retire with the verbs -- recorded in the PRD's section, no FR renumbered. (f) AD: AD-4 is amended with a dated bullet, not a new AD: its invariant (diff before mutate, zero deploys on no diff) holds and only the mutation moves from a git push to a deploy-pages run; the spine's Deferred row 'Formal GitHub Actions deploy workflow for the dashboard' is taken up by Epic 102; steward's own ADs are cited bare, the satellite forms canopy:AD-n / pap:AD-n stay for the satellites. (g) Effort as the coordinator sized: 102.1 S, 102.2 M, 102.3 M, 102.4 S, 102.5 S; 102.2 carries the fold and the registry rows. (h) Deps: 102.2 on 102.1; 102.3 on 102.2; 102.4 on 102.2 and atlas Story 30.1 (the artifact producer and the deletion of the committed tree); 102.5 on 102.2 and herald CAP-60's flag-retire story (the coordinator fills the key). Every key is minted backlog; no blocked row (a blocked key is the operator's).

The seven operator rulings relayed 2026-10-14 are each placed (the decision by user entry; the renamed workflow supersedes the 2026-09-27 ruling; the six-hourly schedule with the no-op skip; configure-pages authoritative; the artifact handoff skipped never failed; the release asset; every job a PAUSE_* switch and a registry row by 99.2's convention). One sizing deviation from the Dream, which said 'one M2 story for the surface move and the duty': the coordinator's brief decomposes it into five stories (102.1-102.5) so the skip, the fold, the duty, the artifact mount and the host change each land and verify alone; recorded here, nothing dropped. The measured numbers stay in the Dream, the brainstorm and the design-thinking doc; the Spec cites them. Kinships kept on their own chains: doctor's liveness probe (its 2026-10-13 ruling), atlas's producer (Story 30.1), herald's content stories.

- (decision by user) 2026-10-14: operator ruling for steward Story 103.3, verbatim: "yes stdlib only". It answered the question "should pyforge-ci stay stdlib-only?", whose lean option was "move spec-kit out of the python feature" (DW-doctor-47-1-1, owner pyforge-steward). So pyforge-ci stays stdlib-only: spec-kit leaves what pyforge-ci composes, and every other environment that composes the python feature keeps it. - (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence and design for the CI status master switch (Story 103.1, CAP-166). Read at 846d8383f5: ci.py _build_rows (:565) emits rows only for registry surfaces, unregistered switch-like variables and unregistered disabled_manually workflows; _switch_names_in_registry (:301) counts conventions.master as registered, so PAUSE_ALL_CI never gets a row; each state verdict (_state_verdict, :463) reads only the row's own switch, while every push/pull_request/schedule job carries the rendered fragment with vars.PAUSE_ALL_CI != 'true' (_ci_surface_gates_render.build_fragment; completeness_findings reds a job without it; 13 workflow files read the master). Consumers read: doctor ci_pause_drift._parse_payload needs rows with a string id and an eight-value verdict and ignores other keys, and WARNs only on drift, stuck and review_days_left < 0; herald ci_surfaces_tile shows every non-live row, drift first, appends detail, and its contract test pins the verdict enum; marshal landing_ci_pauses.row_counts_as_paused_at_merge lists paused-manually rows and any non-opt-in-off, non-retired row whose live_switch_value is true, skips unregistered rows and never reads verdict. Design: one master:PAUSE_ALL_CI row (declared live, the variable's value, drift with a detail while true, since CAP-166 defines drift as declared differs from live and ci pause cannot declare a master pause); every row whose jobs read the master (derived from the workflow files with _ci_surface_gates_lib.collect_var_uses: a workflow row when every job reads it, a ci-job row when its job does, a ci-step row when its step or job does; value, retired and paused-manually rows never) reads paused with a detail while the master is on, below drift, stuck and overdue, and drift stays judged on the row's own switch; one additive boolean row property paused_by_master; no new verdict. With no consumer change: doctor WARNs once on the master row, herald lists the master and every gated row, marshal lists master:PAUSE_ALL_CI. Marshal does not list each gated surface (it reads live_switch_value, not verdict); that is marshal's own follow-up, named in its 100.1 Residual risk, and is not minted here. Rejected: a top-level master field (no consumer reads it), rewriting live_switch_value, a new verdict (doctor and herald would need changes), a paused master row (doctor stays silent), a declared reads_master registry field.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence and design for the reconcile's bmad-suite floors (Story 103.2, CAP-167). Read at 846d8383f5: estate_reconcile._build_generators (:68) adds generate-bmad-suite (:98-:105) when the batch moves bmad-method or a bmad-* package, and _run_generator (:210) journals only argv, exit and seconds; the conda-forge-expert generator bmad_suite_metapackage.py resolves each member's upstream latest (_resolve_upstream, :183), falls to a second registry or to the member recipe's context.version when a lookup fails, and never goes below the recipe (_prefer_floor, :170). Story 100.7: fe9679af8d wrote bmad-loop >=0.13.2, TEA >=2.0.3 and bmad-eval-quality >=8.0.0, none moved by the batch; the next reconcile wrote >=0.12.0, >=1.27.2 and >=4.3.0; the lock held 0.12.0 and 1.27.2; journal .steward/estate-refresh/2026-10-14.json records the moves bmad-method 6.12.0 -> 6.12.2 and bmad-module-skill-forge 2.2.0 -> 3.0.0 and, for the generator, exit 0 only. Main's suite floors bmad-method >=6.12.2 and bmad-module-skill-forge >=3.0.0 sit above their member recipes (6.12.1, 2.2.0), so a plain --offline regeneration would lower them. Design: the generator gains --journal <path> (batch mode: no upstream lookup at all; a moving member's floor is the highest to the batch records for it, never below its current floor; any other member keeps the higher of its current suite floor and its member recipe's version; a floor never falls) and --json (one report object: version_before, version, written, and per member name, registry, recipe_version, moved_to, floor_before, floor, source, fallback; on the hand path fallback is true when the floor did not come from the declared cfe-upstream-registry). Reconcile runs it with --journal <its journal> --json, writes the report to the journal's reconcile.bmad_suite, names each changed floor, and fails with exit 1 naming generate-bmad-suite on a non-zero exit or an unparseable report. The hand path without the new flags stays byte-for-byte. Reconcile asking for no lookup is how it refuses a fallback it did not ask for. Rejected: capping a looked-up floor (same floors, one more way to fail), --offline (lowers two floors below the lock), changing the generator's default (CFE's suite-advance path), steward computing floors itself (AD-1, CAP-167's own-generator rule), widening the bmad-* trigger (CAP-167's text). The effort invokes the conda-forge-expert skill first and closes with its retro: SKILL.md and CHANGELOG.md, a MINOR bump, in a retro(cfe): commit.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence and design for a stdlib-only pyforge-ci (Story 103.3, CAP-5). No steward CAP names pyforge-ci; CAP-5 is the CAP whose rules govern feature composition (features by name, a task in one feature, back-compat by composition). Measured on linux-64 from the lock at 846d8383f5: pyforge-ci holds 146 packages; 38 are reachable only through spec-kit (jsonschema via mcp, pyyaml, pydantic, httpx and the rest) and 2 only through the pyforge-ci feature's ruamel.yaml. tests/scripts in pyforge-ci: 1554 passed, 28 skipped. With those 38 import names blocked by a sitecustomize finder: 845 passed, 73 failed, 9 collection errors, 44 skipped, every failure and error No module named 'yaml'; 32 files fail, error or newly skip. Blocking also ruamel, requests, gitpython, truststore, rich, cryptography, twine, hatchling and the tools' other libraries changes nothing, and tests/packaging still passes 130. The 32 files pass in pyforge-guild serially (836 passed, 0 skipped, 56 s); in pyforge-doctor two error (requests, ruamel.yaml). Design: spec-kit moves verbatim to a new spec-kit feature composed by the 13 other environments that compose python; ruamel.yaml leaves the pyforge-ci feature (its one consumer, test_failure_catalog_check.py, also needs yaml); each of the 32 files gets a module-level pytest.importorskip, so pyforge-ci skips them cleanly; a new guild-tasks task scripts-lib-test runs 31 of them in pyforge-guild as a new scripts-suite step and pr-preflight-lanes leg, and the 32nd, test_detectors_doctor_sources.py, stays with the doctor aggregate step; a stdlib-only guard test in tests/scripts asserts the definition. Definition of stdlib-only: what pyforge-ci runs imports only the stdlib, pytest and the in-repo pyforge-core / pyforge-testing-kit sources; yaml, jsonschema, ruamel.yaml, mcp, pydantic, httpx and openfeature are not importable there; it still carries, as named decisions, the python feature's binaries, gitpython and truststore, and its own build/test/upload tools with their libraries. Found while measuring: a bare estate reconcile loads the latest tracked journal (.steward/estate-refresh/2026-10-14.json), re-runs that batch's generators (generate-bmad-suite included, network-resolved until 103.2 lands) and writes its reconcile block back into that file; recorded as DW-steward-103-3-1, not fixed here.

- (decision by user) 2026-10-14: operator approval for steward Story 103.4, relayed verbatim by the orchestrating session: the standing direction of 2026-10-14 "be autonomous, keep merging ... complete as much as you can autonomously" and "pre-existing findings are fixed now". Scope relayed with it: tests/scripts/test_ci_surface_gates.py builds every generated fixture under pytest tmp_path (or a copy of a checked-in input placed there) and never writes into the repo tree; a tracked fixture that a test only regenerates at runtime leaves git; a real checked-in input that no test overwrites stays; a guard test or meta assertion proves a run of the file leaves git status --porcelain -- tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates empty; pyforge-doctor-scripts-test (pyforge-ci) and the detectors scripts-suite stay green; no assertion weakens; measure first which tracked files tests write and which they only read. - (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence for the ci-surface-gates fixture writes (Story 103.4, CAP-166), measured at 6e1918fea2 in pyforge-ci. Twelve of the 20 tests in tests/scripts/test_ci_surface_gates.py write under FIXTURES = tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates/ in the tracked tree: the six rule cases (unregistered, row_drift, bad_name, wrong_cmp, missing_job, paused_no_review) each write <case>/.github/workflows/sample.yml and <case>/registry.yaml; the three live-mode tests write workflows_match.json, workflows_extra_disabled.json and workflows_active_mismatch.json; wrapped_if, multiline_if and missing_canary each write one sample.yml. That is all 18 tracked files, each written by exactly one test (one test run per test against an emptied directory), and no test reads a file there that it did not write first, so none is a checked-in input. With the directory deleted the file passes (20 passed, 9.6 s) and recreates every tracked path. On a clean checkout a run leaves exactly unregistered/registry.yaml (a yaml.safe_dump of the live docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml) and workflows_match.json (built from its rows) modified: Story 102.1 renamed .github/workflows/dashboard.yml to pages.yml in the registry, and the other 16 come back byte-identical. The 18 entered git only through the dispatch auto-checkpoints 2c6ff4dd4a (wip: 99.1) and aa8abbe32e (wip: 99.6, missing_canary). pyforge-doctor-scripts-test runs tests/scripts with -n auto --dist loadgroup, pr-preflight runs it, and the pre-commit pre-push-preflight hook fails a hook that modifies files whatever its exit code, so every push since 102.1 is refused after a green verdict; two pushes on 2026-10-14 went out with PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1. scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json names spec-pyforge-steward as the only governor of the test file; no fixture path is on any baseline. git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all over that one pathspec takes about 13 ms here.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, design for Story 103.4 (CAP-166). (1) Each of the twelve tests takes tmp_path and builds its case root, registry and workflow-state JSON there under the same relative names; workflow text, registry dicts, detector arguments and assertions are unchanged, and the FIXTURES constant goes. (2) git rm all 18 files under tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates/: none is an input, so none stays. (3) An autouse function-scoped fixture runs git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates after each test and fails the test when the output is non-empty or the status read fails (fail closed); function scope holds under xdist and any order and names the writer. (4) test_fixture_tree_guard_is_active_and_sees_untracked_and_modified_files asserts the guard is in request.fixturenames and runs the guard status read in a scratch repo under tmp_path (global and system git config off, --no-verify): empty when clean, a " M" line for a modified tracked fixture, a "??" line for an untracked one. Rejected: regenerating and keeping the two stale files (they drift again on the next registry edit); a .gitignore entry (hides the writes, blinds the guard); re-running the file in a subprocess (doubles the run, races under xdist); a module-scoped guard (per-worker teardown timing, names no test); touching imports or pixi.toml (Story 103.3 owns the env split and the import guards). Mutations to check: one case root moved back into the repo tree fails the guard; --untracked-files=no fails the named test.

- (event by claude) 2026-10-14, Story 103.4 independent review 1 (PR #2222 at 13b92761f7): FAIL, one blocker, four nits, one out of scope. (1) Blocker: the scratch-repo guard test ran git with the whole caller environment, though the spec Approach and the design entry said the global and system config were off; under a linked worktree pre-push hook git 2.43 exports GIT_DIR=<main>/.git/worktrees/<name>, and with it inherited the test set core.bare=true in the shared .git/config and committed seed onto the current branch (reviewer reproduced both in scratch repos; reproduced again here in a scratch main plus linked worktree with the 13b92761f7 file; the real primary .git/config reads core.bare=false and no seed commit exists, since scripts/pre_push_preflight.sh unsets GIT_DIR before pr-preflight). Fixed: _isolate_git(monkeypatch) deletes GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE, GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY, GIT_COMMON_DIR, GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES and GIT_PREFIX and sets GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null and GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1; the test exports a decoy GIT_DIR first and asserts git made the scratch repo own .git and left the decoy alone. (2) The guard status read adds --ignored=matching, so an ignored file reads !!, covered in the test. (3) The guard snapshots the status before each test and fails only on new lines, so only the writer fails; the named test also asserts the repo fixture tree is clean, so a leftover fails it once; the read runs git --no-optional-locks. (4) check=False on the seven older subprocess.run calls (ruff PLW1510 under the root config; ruff check now exits 0). (5) tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates/ leaves the story spec context. (6) Out of scope, deferred to a follow-up story the coordinator is chaining: test_six_fixture_rules_together_six_findings is vacuous (the len() of a six-item literal set), so Story 99.1 AC2 has no test. - (event by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence for the preflight lane identity defect (Story 103.5, CAP-159), read at 22e5c19c28. Atlas Story 31.1 (PR #2221, branch dispatch/pyforge-atlas/31.1) adds { task = "mypy", environment = "pyforge-foundry-full" } to pixi.toml pr-preflight-lanes beside the Guild's mypy, and a lint-types.yml step pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-foundry-full mypy in the same lint-types job; CI's steward-test fails test_preflight_selection.py::test_run_preflight_runs_selected_lanes_in_order_and_journals_the_selection with assert 20 == (37 - 16), because its helper selected_tasks() collects task names, so two selected mypy lanes count once. Reproduced locally with both 31.1 files checked out and restored (git status empty after): 1 failed, 72 passed; 37 lanes, 17 selected, 16 distinct task names. The runner has the same flaw. preflight.py lists one Lane per (task, environment) (list_preflight_lanes :172, LaneKey :47; pending and completed use it, :892-:893), but keys by lane.task: results_by_task (:891), written by _record_done (:900) and read for the ordered results and the journal (:990-:991, :1017-:1018); each lane's scratch directory and log, scratch_root / lane.task and <task>.log (:661-:662), and the red-lane log lines (:1026, :1036); _RunCoordinator.terminated_lane_tasks and proc_lane_task (:286-:287, register_proc :304), red_lanes (:284, :694), stop_trigger (:285, :697), suite_reduction_runtime_journal (:291, :481, _lane_journal_extra :528) and xdist_workers (:292, :664); and the CI-pause filter paused_tasks (:752-:771), which drops every lane whose task is paused. A two-lane fixture (a in inv exits 0, a in other exits 3, --keep-going, jobs 1) on main journaled [(a, other, red, 3), (a, other, red, 3)], printed "lane 'a' in environment 'inv' exited 3", and gave both lanes one a.log. preflight_ci.py: _step_matches (:455) matches a pixi run step by its task's depends-on closure (_closure :322) and ignores the step's -e, while the command-line match _segment_in_run (:416) already compares the environment (:428-:430). preflight_budget.py: _selection_selected_lanes (:101) returns names, so is_single_station_budget (:112) counts two lanes of one station test once, and _lane_timings (:121) labels lanes by task alone. preflight_xdist.lane_xdist_worker_map (:46) keys shares by task. Checked and sound: Selection.is_selected and the verdicts (preflight_ci :103) and the service mutex keys (:1469, :1499-:1552) already use (task, environment); estate_verify's --lane NAME (cli.py :1249, estate_verify.py :442) filters by task and appends one row per lane with its environment, so nothing is overwritten.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, design for Story 103.5 (CAP-159): a lane's identity is (task, environment) everywhere the preflight selects, runs, orders, journals, logs and budgets lanes. (1) results_by_task becomes results_by_key: dict[LaneKey, LaneResult]; ordered results and the journal read it by _lane_key. (2) A new lane_label(task, environment) returns <task>@<environment>; each lane's scratch directory is scratch_root/<label> and its log scratch_root/<label>.log, the red-lane lines print that path, and the root stays flat, one directory per lane (Story 71.8). Pixi environment names hold only [a-z0-9-], so the label is unique per lane. Rejected: <environment>/<task>.log (four scratch tests read the lane directory's parent as the scratch root and would pass while asserting less) and <task>.log unless the task repeats (two rules; a lane's name would depend on the others). (3) The coordinator keys terminated marks (terminated_lanes: set[LaneKey], proc_lane: dict[int, LaneKey]; register_proc takes the key), red_lanes, the suite-reduction runtime journal and the xdist share map by lane; the stop cause is one stop_cause tuple (trigger, environment or None) assigned once, with stop_trigger kept as a read-only property. (4) The journal, additively: LaneResult gains cancelled_by_environment and blocked_by_environment, written only when the cause is a lane, never for interrupt; no key is renamed and cancelled_by keeps its meaning. (5) The pause filter keys paused lanes by (task, environment); the pause banner still de-duplicates by task, because its lines are registry CI surfaces, which name no environment. (6) Selection: _closure returns (task, environment) pairs (a bare depends-on entry inherits its parent's environment, a { task, environment } entry takes its own), and _step_matches counts a pixi run step for a lane when the pair's environment is the lane's, absent (no -e) or dynamic, the rule _segment_in_run already applies. Measured: over every workflow step and all 36 lanes at 22e5c19c28 this changes no match; with 31.1's pixi.toml and lint-types.yml it changes two, each mypy lane matching only its own step, both in the lint-types job, so the selection is the same. With 31.1's pixi.toml alone the foundry-full lane has no CI counterpart and runs on every branch, which is CAP-159's rule; 31.1 lands both files together, so AC9 checks out both. (7) Budget: _selection_selected_lanes returns (lane, environment) pairs and the single-station rule counts station-test lanes by pair; _lane_timings labels <task>@<environment> when the entry has an environment, else the task. (8) lane_xdist_worker_map takes and returns lane keys. Kept by task, on purpose: the suite-reduction plan (build_suite_lane_overrides) derives from a pixi task definition, which has no environment; the CI surfaces registry's local_twin; estate verify --lane NAME. Tests: test_preflight_selection.py counts lanes by key (a selected_lanes helper; the journal skipped count reads entries); the concurrency, scratch, suite-reduction and workers tests move each internal check from a task name to the lane key or label, with no assertion weakened; a new test_preflight_lane_identity.py covers AC1-AC5 and AC8; four mutations (results by task, <task>.log, an environment-blind closure, the pause filter by task).

A fix under CAP-159, in Epic 103 because Epics 71, 88, 90 and 98 are done; the Epic 103 heading now names CAP-159. No CAP, and no SPEC.md, PRD or spine change. ACs: (1) both lanes of one task run; (2) each journals its own environment, status, exit code and seconds, and stderr names only the red one; (3) each writes its own <task>@<environment>.log; (4) a lane that stops or blocks another is journaled with cancelled_by_environment or blocked_by_environment; (5) a CI pause drops only its own lane; (6) _step_matches matches the step's environment, absent or dynamic counting for any; (7) test_preflight_selection.py counts lanes by (task, environment); (8) the budget check and the xdist map key lanes by (task, environment); (9) with atlas 31.1's pixi.toml and lint-types.yml checked out, never committed, pyforge-steward-test passes; (10) no existing assertion weakens and the journal changes only by the two optional fields; (11) four mutations; (12) pyforge-steward-test, pyforge-steward-coverage-gate and lint-types green, the surface reconciled and stamped scoped. - (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, Story 103.5 design amended in implementation (CAP-159): the environment-aware CI-counterpart match is dropped, and _step_matches keeps Story 71.2's task match ("a step whose run: invokes pixi run ... <task> for the lane's own task or for a task whose depends-on closure holds it"). Measured: the env-aware _closure changed no step match over the 36 lanes at 22e5c19c28, and with 31.1's pixi.toml and lint-types.yml it only moved each mypy lane onto its own step inside the one lint-types job, so no verdict changed. But it failed test_service_lanes_never_overlap (test_preflight_concurrency.py): that test's lanes run in the invoking environment (pyforge-steward under the test run) while its workflow runs them with -e pyforge-guild, so they lost their CI step and the services:postgres mutex that keeps them apart. The same would strike every bare depends-on lane of `pixi run -e local-recipes pr-preflight`, which pixi.toml documents as a supported entry: a bare lane's environment is wherever pr-preflight is invoked, not CI's. Selection's lane identity is already held where it decides: one verdict per (task, environment), Selection.is_selected on the pair, and the journal's selected and skipped entries carrying the environment; test_preflight_selection.py now counts lanes that way, and test_preflight_lane_identity.py::test_selection_keeps_each_lane_of_one_task proves a verdict decides its lane alone. _step_matches' docstring now states the rule. Changed with it: AC6 restated (selection decides each lane alone; every _step_matches case keeps its value), AC11's closure mutation replaced by dropping the red blocker's environment, and the Story 103.5 text in epics.md, the story spec (Approach, Rejected, I/O matrix, Spec Change Log) and the Dream's addition (its sentence on CI steps removed) amended in the implementation commit. Also found by the re-keying: test_preflight_ci.py's two pause tests built their PausedLane in pyforge-guild but ran run_preflight in the test process's environment, so their paused entry named a lane the run did not have; task keying hid it. Both now pass invoking_env=INVOKING_ENV and assert the paused lane by key (held back without --ignore-ci-pauses, run with it).

- (event by claude) 2026-10-14: independent review of steward Story 103.5 (PR #2225 at 8dd1fd00ad) returned PASS, with one should-fix and five nits; recorded in the story spec's Review section, status kept in-review. (1, should-fix) Two re-keyed coordinator paths were untested: a mutant comparing terminated marks by task at both key-in-terminated_lanes sites (m5) and one dropping cancelled_by_environment from the terminated-lane result (m6) each passed all 270 preflight tests, and neither _skip_lane_on_red_stop path in _run_lane_in_pool had an assertion. Resolved with test_a_stop_marks_only_the_twin_it_terminated (fail goes red, its stop terminates a@inv, a@other already exited on its own stays ok, terminated_lanes == {(a, inv)}), test_a_lane_the_stop_keeps_from_starting_names_its_twin (the first stop check) and test_a_lane_stopped_while_it_waits_for_its_service_names_its_twin (the second, after the service lock, made deterministic by a lock that signals its waiter), plus a cancelled_by_environment assertion in test_real_subprocess_red_lane_cancels_running_peer. Re-run against test_preflight_lane_identity.py and test_preflight_concurrency.py, each mutant now fails: m5 fails the twin test; m6 fails it and the real-subprocess test; m7 (no environment on the first stop check) fails test_a_lane_the_stop_keeps_from_starting_names_its_twin; m8 (no environment on the second) fails test_a_lane_stopped_while_it_waits_for_its_service_names_its_twin. (2, nit) test_sigint_cancels_real_subprocess_lanes now asserts no cancelled entry carries cancelled_by_environment. (3, nit) The stop cause is now checked and set under a dedicated reentrant stop_lock by _RunCoordinator.set_stop_cause, used by the red lane and the SIGINT handler; not proc_lock, because the handler runs on the main thread, may re-enter, and already takes the plain proc_lock through terminate_children. test_the_first_stop_cause_wins covers it. (4, nit) The suite_lane_plan comment says the reduction is applied by task across environments and names the case where it would matter: a station test in two environments while its coverage gate runs in one. (5, nit) Atlas 31.1's two mypy lanes share .mypy_cache when run concurrently: not changed here, tracked separately by the orchestrating session. No AC, CAP or SPEC.md change.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence and design for marp in the Guild env (Story 63.8, a fix under CAP-5 / Story 63.1, with CAP-152 / Story 63.6 for the task's move). The gap: Story 63.1 moved the Guild tasks into [feature.guild-tasks.tasks] so they run under -e pyforge-guild, and Story 63.6 moved deck-export, deck-facts and deck-trio there with their pyforge-herald import; deck-export then runs the marp binary, and marp-cli = ">=4.2.3" was declared only in [feature.local-recipes.dependencies] (pixi.toml :2314, with the nodejs-26 / libabseil note). At 846d8383f5 .pixi/envs/pyforge-guild/bin/marp does not exist and .pixi/envs/local-recipes/bin/marp does, so scripts/deck_export.py run_marp exits 1 with "'marp' not on PATH -- run via: pixi run -e local-recipes deck-export ...", the form session_denials refuses as a Guild-task / local-recipes mix-up; herald's deck pull, deck sync-all and the deck pipeline shell the task as pixi run -e pyforge-guild deck-export <slug> (herald 25.1). (3) Every usage line for a Guild task reads -e pyforge-guild: the three descriptions, the guild-tasks banner (now names the marp dependency), the script's docstring and hint, the how-to's note and its three marp commands. (4) Pins: steward tests/meta/test_marp_in_guild_env_story_63_8.py asserts marp-cli is a Guild-feature dependency at the local-recipes floor, that every guild-tasks task naming marp resolves it there, and that no guild-tasks description carries a pixi run -e local-recipes <guild task> usage line; tests/scripts/test_deck_export.py gains test_run_marp_hint_names_the_guild_env. Not changed, recorded for herald: deck_facts.py and deck_trio.py docstring and emitted usage lines (deck_facts.py writes one into every facts.yaml header) and the herald story specs / Spec companions that cite pixi run -e local-recipes deck-…. - (event by claude) 2026-10-15, evidence for the touched-scope preflight (Story 103.6, CAP-159), measured 2026-10-14/15 and read at fccf4f3ee7. Local pr-preflight lanes run in parallel and finish in about 10 min on a quiet host; under host load 29-114 they took 13-28 min and timed out: chain_currency_sweep_check passed its 300 s limit though it takes 75 s alone, and atlas e2e port binds passed their 60 s limit. The slowest local lanes were pyforge-steward-test and the steward coverage gate (9.7-17.9 min each), test-ci (13-28 min) and detectors-ci (6-11 min). GitHub runs the same lanes in about 10 min. The lane list at fccf4f3ee7 has 37 lanes (pr-preflight-lanes expanded from pyforge-guild). run_preflight (preflight.py:764) selects (:797), drops CI-paused lanes (:800-:834), prints the count (:835-:841) and journals one run record (:1103-:1132; interrupted :1053-:1069); main (:1166) parses --jobs, --keep-going and --ignore-ci-pauses and reads no scope. PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1 is checked in scripts/pre_push_preflight.sh before pixi is called, so it is all-or-nothing, and nothing lists or later verifies what skipped pushes left unchecked. The five cheap planning detectors are guild tasks of their own, each a cmd under [feature.guild-tasks.tasks]: spec-surface-check (python -m pyforge.doctor.sources spec-surface), flag-gate-check (python scripts/flag_gate_check.py), story-status-check, ledger-regression-check and chain-completeness-check (python -m pyforge.doctor.sources ...); at f7be77efad in pyforge-guild they took 2.9 s, 2.4 s, 0.6 s, 0.9 s and 0.7 s. detectors.yml has no paths filter on pull_request, so detectors-ci is selected for every diff. Station lanes are told apart by environment: every pyforge-<x>-coverage-gate runs the same cmd (python scripts/coverage_gates_ci.py --base origin/main --head HEAD --suites unit) in pyforge-<x>. Found at f7be77efad: flag-gate-check failed on spec-102-2 (flag-test-file-missing, bare test_preflight_selection.py and test_ci_status.py in its Verification); PR #2233 (0d99ddf693) fixed it before this mint, and flag-gate-check exits 0 at fccf4f3ee7.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-15, design for Story 103.6 (CAP-159). (1) Scope: PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SCOPE=full|touched chooses it; unset, the flag pyforge.steward.preflight_ci_arbitrates does (on = touched, off = full), read only when the variable is unset; any other value, the empty string included, refuses with exit 2 and names both values; a flag-tree error refuses with exit 2. It is resolved in main, so run_preflight keeps scope full as its default and its callers and tests keep their behaviour. (2) The touched cut starts from the lanes full would run (selection, then the CI pause filter) and keeps a lane, keyed by (task, environment) as Story 103.5 made it, when: its environment is pyforge-<x> and the diff changes src/shared/packages/pyforge-<x>/ or django-<x>/ (each only if it exists); or a token of its pixi cmd, read relative to the task cwd, names an existing repo file or directory (never the root) that a changed path equals or lies under; or its task is in the lint-types depends-on closure and a .py/.pyi file under src/ changed. A kept lane brings its dependencies. When detectors-ci is among the lanes and not kept, the five fast-core tasks (spec-surface-check, flag-gate-check, story-status-check, ledger-regression-check, chain-completeness-check) run in its environment and its place; a missing fast-core task is a config error. Every other lane is deferred, reason "CI arbitrates". Changed paths are the selection's own (diff against refs/remotes/origin/main plus the working tree); an unreadable diff keeps every lane and says so. (3) stdout names the scope and its source and one line per deferred lane task@environment; the stderr count line adds the deferred count. (4) The journal, additively: the run record and the interrupted record gain mode (full|touched) and deferred ([{lane, environment, reason}], empty in full); selection.mode (diff|all) is a different key and stays; a record without mode reads as full. (5) The hook is unchanged: it checks PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1 before calling pixi, so a skipped push reads no scope, and it passes the variable through. (6) A new duty preflight with one verb, verify-deferred [--since YYYY-MM-DD] (module preflight_verify.py): it prints every journaled run since the date (UTC, inclusive; all when absent) whose deferred is not empty and every skip-log line since then except branch deletes, then runs pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight with PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SCOPE=full and the git hook variables unset, by subprocess (AD-1), returning a DutyResult (AD-8): 0 green, 1 red, 2 bad --since, 3 could not run (preflight exit 2 or no pixi), 130 interrupted; it is not flag-gated. (7) The flag: flags.json (owner steward, story key, created 2026-10-15, defaultVariant off), flag-overlays.json (dev on, staging and production off), the core and platform flag contract tests, the regenerated flag inventory; read in preflight.py. Rejected: a --scope argument (the hook calls pr-preflight bare); reading the scope inside run_preflight (with dev on every caller and test would flip); running the fast core always (detectors.yml fires on every PR, but tying it to detectors-ci keeps touched a subset of CI); a hand-written lane-to-path table; counting a task cwd as a path it names (npm ci would run alone for every docs-site change); journaling deferrals in the skip log; gating verify-deferred behind the flag. Not edited: pixi.toml (its pr-preflight description is stale since Story 71.2, but a pixi.toml change fires every station suite in CI and needs an environment.yaml re-export), scripts/pre_push_preflight.sh.

- (decision by user) 2026-10-15: operator ruling for steward Story 103.7, verbatim: "fix this now - One more gap: marshal 100.1's landing line recorded the CI pauses as unknown. It reads steward ci status --json, which exits 1 whenever any row is stuck; the container-build pauses make three rows stuck. So every landing records unknown." The orchestrating session corrected the stated cause after reading the code, before the mint: a stuck verdict never fails the read (run_status sets ok = not partial, and partial is only a failed run-list fetch); the corrected evidence is the next entry. Scope relayed with the ruling: a retired row's workflow is not fetched (or its 404 is not an error), so it cannot make the read partial, and it keeps its row with last_green_run empty, not unknown; decide whether a genuine partial read still prints its --json envelope on stdout with partial and run_list_errors inside it (additive), and if so keep marshal's adapter, doctor's source and herald's tile working, marshal reading a valid envelope on exit 1 as a read carrying partial, each consumer edit kept to a few lines. Numbering ruled: Story 103.7 in Epic 103, a fix under CAP-166 with no CAP, no flag and no SPEC.md change.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-15, evidence and design for the ci status partial read (Story 103.7). Owner: this Spec, CAP-166 (Story 99.3's ci status; Stories 99.7, 103.1). Measured at f3cab009ec (main after doctor 48.1): pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge steward ci status --json exits 1 with stdout empty and the JSON envelope on stderr. The live snapshot's run_list_errors has two entries, both HTTP 404 "workflow ... not found on the default branch": .github/workflows/reusable-staged-recipes-linter.yml and .github/workflows/reusable-staged-recipes-linter-selftest.yml. Both are registry rows declared retired whose files Story 99.7 deleted (35344ca194). The first was on: workflow_call and the second on: workflow_dispatch only, so neither ran as a workflow of its own and GitHub keeps no record for its path. The four other retired rows fetch without error because GitHub keeps a deleted workflow's record once it has run: linter_issue_comment and staged-recipes-linter read their 2026-08 runs, and docsite-check and docsite-check.check (the file folded into pages.yml by Story 102.2) read a last green of 2026-10-15T08:03:18Z for a file that is no longer in the tree. Mechanism: _collect_snapshot (ci.py about :242) fetches gh run list for every surface row's workflow, retired rows included; a failed fetch appends to run_list_errors; run_status (about :876) sets partial and ok = not partial; cli.main prints a failed duty's summary on stderr, so the --json payload lands there and exit is 1. The 3 stuck rows (station-tests.guild-container, platform-ci.container, platform-ci.container-dbgpt: paused cost-high rows whose canary has no green job in 15 days) do not change ok. Consumers at f3cab009ec: marshal adapters/steward_cli.py records {state: unknown, reason: "steward ci status partial read (exit 1): <first stderr line>"} on any exit 1, and the doctor 48.1, herald 43.1 and atlas 30.1 landing journals of 2026-10-15 each hold "steward ci status partial read (exit 1): {"; doctor sources/ci_pause_drift.py parses stderr on exit 1 and adds a ci-pause-partial WARN; herald's tile reads only the committed snapshot docsite/content/ci-status.json, which its spec defines as the command's stdout, so a partial read leaves nothing to capture. Design: (1) _collect_snapshot reads no run list for a row whose state is retired, and _build_rows gives a retired row last_green_run null and no runs, so it can neither be partial nor stuck; drift on a retired row whose file still exists is judged locally as before. The unknown match becomes e.startswith("<workflow>: ") instead of a substring test. (2) Decision: on a genuine partial read --json still prints its envelope on stdout, carrying partial: true and run_list_errors (one "<workflow>: <gh error>" per failed fetch), and the exit stays 1. Reason: the variables and workflow states were read in full, so every row's declared state and switch value are true on a partial read; only last_green_run and the run-based stuck rules depend on the failed fetches. A reader therefore gets every row it can, and partial tells it the rest. The two keys are optional schema properties, present only on a partial read, so a complete envelope and the checked-in master-on fixture are byte-identical. The duty asks for stdout through details["summary_stream"] = "stdout", which cli.main honours beside the existing exit_code request; ok stays false, so nothing reads a partial read as complete. The table form keeps stderr. (3) Consumers: marshal's adapter parses stdout on exit 1 and, when it is a valid envelope, records a read; record_from_envelope adds partial: true when the envelope says so. The PR line is unchanged, because surfaces come from declared_state and live_switch_value, which a partial read still holds. Exit 1 with no valid envelope on stdout stays unknown, and stderr is still never parsed for rows (marshal 100.1's rule). Doctor parses stdout on exit 1 and falls back to stderr for an older steward, keeping its ci-pause-partial WARN. Herald's tile needs no change: its validator ignores top-level keys it does not know.
- (event by claude) 2026-10-15: Story 103.7 minted in Epic 103 (fix, S, no deps). Key 103-7-steward-ci-status-reads-no-runs-for-a-retired-row-so-a-landing-records-the-ci-pauses is at backlog; epic-103 keeps its status. It went in through a worktree-local Tier-3 feed and sprint-ledger-sync --project steward. Spec spec-103-7-steward-ci-status-reads-no-runs-for-a-retired-row-so-a-landing-records-the-ci-pauses.md: status ready-for-dev, no flag (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1). A fix under CAP-166; Epic 103 because Epic 99 is done and Epic 103 holds the 2026-10-14 ci status fixes. No CAP, and no SPEC.md, PRD or spine change. Epic 103's HARD boundary "No consumer station changes" gains this story's exception, on the operator's ruling: marshal's steward adapter and landing record, and doctor's ci_pause_drift source, a few lines each. ACs: (1) a retired row's runs are never read and it reads last_green_run null, so the read exits 0 with the envelope on stdout; (2) a live row whose fetch fails still makes the read partial, exit 1; (3) on a partial read --json prints the envelope on stdout with partial: true and run_list_errors, and the table keeps stderr; (4) the schema gains only the two optional top-level properties, and the complete envelope and the master-on fixture are unchanged; (5) marshal records a read on exit 0 and on exit 1 with a valid envelope, carrying partial, and unknown otherwise; (6) doctor reads the partial envelope from stdout, falling back to stderr; (7) herald's tile reads a partial envelope unchanged; (8) mutations: re-fetching retired rows, and dropping summary_stream, each fail a named test; (9) the live command exits 0 with JSON on stdout; (10) the suites, lint-types, flag-gate and the planning checks are green, and every governing Spec reconciles and stamps scoped. Story 103.5's block moves back under Epic 103 (it sat under Epic 104), and Story 103.4 regains its missing Status line. - (decision by claude) 2026-10-15, evidence and design for the docs-site preflight mutex (Story 105.1, a fix under CAP-159; no CAP, no SPEC.md change, no flag). CAP-159's intent already says concurrent lanes share no mutable state, and the docs-site/ tree is mutable state two lanes share, so the fix restores the intent as written and needs no CAP amendment (the Epic 88/90/103.5/104 form). Evidence at 38ec9a2d25: docs-site-install is npm ci in docs-site/ (deletes and reinstalls node_modules); herald's built-site tests call pages_astro.astro_build, which runs astro build with cwd docs-site/ and rewrites docs-site/public/, and pages_astro reads RENDER_READY when it is imported; pages-build/pages-check write and read docs-site/public/ and build/site/. Nothing orders pyforge-herald-test against docs-site-install (Story 88.1 orders only a task's own dependencies), and Story 71.3's mutex is one service key per lane. The orchestrating session measured two herald reds ([UNRESOLVED_ENTRY] astro/entrypoints/prerender) on a full-scope run, and the suite alone passed 2058. This story reproduced the race twice through run_preflight's own selection, with herald 2033 passed and 30 skipped against 2058 passed and 5 skipped alone; all 25 extra skips name the docs-site install, so the race reds or silently skips the built-site tests. Design: one declared frozenset preflight.DOCS_SITE_TASKS (docs-site-install, docs-site-build, pages-build, pages-check, docs-site-validate-sidebar, pyforge-herald-test, pyforge-herald-coverage-gate, pyforge-herald-test-coverage); lane_mutex_keys gives each lane a sorted tuple (its service key, tree:docs-site); _run_lane_in_pool takes them in sorted order and releases them in reverse; mutex_lock creates one lock per key under a guard (the old unguarded check-then-set could hand two threads two locks); the scheduler leaves pending a lane whose keys an in-flight lane holds, so no slot waits. A meta test derives the set from pixi.toml (cwd docs-site/, the docs-site-install closure), the docs-site scripts' import closures (an npm package) and herald's tests (astro_build), and fails on a missing or extra name. docs-site-validate-links and -sidebar-order import only node builtins and stay concurrent.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-15, Story 105.1's independent review (PASS, 3 should-fix, 4 nits; scratchpad review-105-1.md) resolved on the same branch, no AC removed, no CAP or key moved. (1, should-fix) The meta test gains a fifth rule: a task that runs a docs-site/ script whose import closure takes node:child_process and names npm, npx, pnpm or astro as a command belongs to the tree; pages-build and pages-check match it on their own, and a planted pages-preview (node docs-site/scripts/pages.mts build, no depends-on) is now derived. (2, should-fix) Ready lanes start in _run_order: first a lane that holds a mutex and that pending lanes depend on, then declaration order, so docs-site-install and pages-build run before pyforge-herald-test and herald's built-site tests read this run's install (a fresh worktree no longer skips 25 of them; a package-lock change no longer tests a stale install). The review's suggestion of needed-first for every lane was narrowed to mutex holders: applied to every lane it reorders lanes that share nothing (test_preflight_lane_identity's --jobs 1 stop test). One existing assertion is restated: test_preflight_selection.py::test_run_preflight_with_no_base_ref_runs_every_lane asserted the serial order equals the declared order; it now asserts every lane runs once, lanes outside the docs-site set keep their declared order, and docs-site-install is the first docs-site lane. (3, should-fix) Measured; see the event below. (nits) The lock-map test now bites (a dict that holds the check-to-store window open; the old unguarded check-then-set and an unguarded setdefault each store two locks); the unit tests run under faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(120, exit=True) with the stacks in <basetemp>/hang-guard-<test>.txt; docs-site/src/lib/status.ts leaves steward_paths (docs-site/src/lib covers it), and a new meta test checks steward_paths covers every docs-site file an import closure visits, which the AST trigger map cannot see; after a red stop without --keep-going no lane starts (Story 88.1's needed-by exception only started lanes whose dependents never run, and with the deferral spawned docs-site-install only for register_proc to kill it), so such a lane is a never-started lane (Story 71.10) and _run_lane_in_pool loses its pending argument.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-16, Story 105.1's re-review (NOT RESOLVED: one should-fix, four nits; scratchpad rereview-105-1.md) resolved as one unit; the story stays done, no AC removed, no CAP or key moved. (should-fix) _run_order put a needed mutex holder ahead of every ready lane, so at --jobs 1 docs-site-install and pages-build ran before ruff, mypy and detectors-ci and a red npm ci cancelled 35 lanes. It becomes _next_lane: every lane outside DOCS_SITE_TASKS keeps its declared turn (its index), and the docs-site lanes, which run one at a time, trade turns among themselves only; the next docs-site turn (the declared index of the next docs-site lane) goes to the ready docs-site lane pending lanes depend on, else the first declared. Measured with the re-review's order_probe.py, adapted to _next_lane (scratchpad rr2/order_probe2.py), on the shipped 36-lane list at --jobs 1: every lane outside the set runs at its declared position; only docs-site-install 30->16, pages-build 31->24, pyforge-herald-test 16->30 and pyforge-herald-coverage-gate 24->31 move, each into a docs-site turn; no dependency violation; --jobs 16 runs each lane once. A red install now cancels 19 lanes (those declared after the first docs-site turn), a subset of the 35 under 6b622f2b36's rule; 9ef2b206e4's 5 is not reachable while the install runs before herald, because herald, declared first, is then among what a red install cancels. A red herald cancels 5 (19 before), a red ruff the same 35. The restated selection assertion now checks declared positions for every lane outside the set. Rejected: deferring herald until the install's own turn (the outside lanes would move, and a red install would still cancel herald). (nits) The spawn pattern reads npm, npx, pnpm or astro as the first word of a quoted or backticked string (execSync('npm run build'), `npm`); the planted test uses .pop('depends-on', None) and the rule guard accepts needs_install or spawns_tool for pages-build and pages-check; the lock-map test sleeps 0.1 s after its check instead of a barrier, so a guarded check-then-set passes and both unguarded forms fail; the spec's residual risk quotes c340e80d (chain 43.9 s under peak load, herald 40.5 s after the install).

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-16, Story 106.1 (fix under CAP-159; no CAP, no flag; closes DW-atlas-retro-2026-10-15-2, steward 103.5's review nit 5): the two mypy lanes pr-preflight runs at once since atlas Story 31.1 (pyforge-guild through lint-types, and pyforge-foundry-full) both ran scripts/lint_types.py mypy, i.e. mypy -p pyforge.<station> from each package with no cache dir, so both read and wrote <package>/.mypy_cache/3.14/ (mypy 2.4.0's SQLite cache, 16 shards); CI's lint-types job runs both in one checkout. Measured at f44a99ba0c (16 cores, mypy 2.4.0, Python 3.14.7): 5 cold + 3 warm concurrent trials all exit 0 with no traceback, lock or error; with a planted error only foundry-full sees (dagster.AssetKey assigned to int in atlas orchestration/definitions.py) 6 sequential steps, 5 cold + 3 warm concurrent lane trials and 10 concurrent atlas-only trials all gave Guild 0 / foundry-full 1; mypy -v shows foundry-full re-checks the atlas modules the Guild cached (dependency sets differ). Could not reproduce a wrong verdict, corruption or crash; the defect is the shared mutable state CAP-159 rules out and its churn (two warm lanes at once 8-11 s vs 3 s each alone). Design: lint_types.py passes --cache-dir <package>/.mypy_cache/<environment>, keyed PIXI_ENVIRONMENT_NAME, else basename(CONDA_PREFIX), else basename(sys.prefix); nested pixi run -e sets PIXI_ENVIRONMENT_NAME over the inherited one (checked); the path stays under .mypy_cache/ (root .gitignore :214 and :539); --cache-dir outranks MYPY_CACHE_DIR (checked: the env dir is never created). CAP-159, not CAP-153: CAP-153's intent and success hold before and after; the rule broken is CAP-159's 'concurrent lanes share no mutable state'. Rejected: a preflight mutex (serializes, leaves CI shared, touches preflight.py, which Story 105.1 had just changed (#2242)), MYPY_CACHE_DIR per preflight lane (CI and hand runs still share), .mypy_cache-<env> (no ignore rule or walker knows it), a cache per lane run (always cold). Residual: CI's foundry-full step no longer reads the Guild step's cache (41-42 s shared vs 53-54 s alone, measured locally).

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-16, Story 107.1 (fix under CAP-159; no CAP, no flag, no SPEC.md change; closes DW-steward-103-6-1, DW-steward-103-6-2, DW-steward-45-1-1): (1) run_preflight never ends when a lane it runs depends on a lane outside the run: _deps_all_ok stays false, _mark_dependency_blocked skips a lane while any dependency has no result, and with no future in flight 'if not futures: continue' loops without a pause. Probed at 2ca62dbca0 (fixture lane a depends on b, the selection a alone): 8 s at 7.96 s user time until SIGINT; a touched run whose fast-core task depends on a deferred lane: the same, flag-gate-check never started. (2) pixi run --as-is --dry-run -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight-lanes exits 1, 'could not find the task pages-build': pixi 0.81.0 looks a bare depends-on up in the invoking environment, breadth-first, and the site feature's tasks exist only in site. Design (orchestrator's brief): a selected lane brings the lanes it depends on, transitively, as pixi run does, after the selection and the CI pause filter and before touched_cut (a paused dependency included: its dependent's CI job runs it through pixi run; it drops its paused not-run row), one stderr line per pulled-in lane and an optional selection.pulled_in journal key, declaration order kept, nothing changed when nothing is pulled in; after the cut and before any install, a lane that still depends on a lane outside the run refuses the run with EXIT_CONFIG (2), naming lane and dependency (the backstop; only the fast core reaches it, DW-steward-103-6-2); the scheduler loop is not restructured. Every depends-on entry of a feature.site task names environment = site, as pyforge-herald-test-built does; a steward meta test walks pr-preflight-lanes from pyforge-guild by pixi's rule (bare: the invoking environment; explicit: the named one; an environment's features from [environments], the default feature unless no-default-feature; breadth-first) and fails naming every missing (task, environment). Measured in a scratch copy at six states (as committed, then each site task fixed in turn): the walker's first missing pair is the task pixi's dry-run named every time, and it is empty exactly when pixi exits 0. Rejected: DW-103-6-1's option (b) alone (refuse every stranded selection: keeps CI's set but a selection pixi itself would run refuses); restructuring the scheduler; describing pr-preflight-lanes as runner-only (the task is the serial reference and should run).

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-16, Story 107.1's independent review (FAIL: two should-fix, four nits; scratchpad r107/review.md, read against the spec at 043eed5557) and the orchestrator's ruling, which replaces design item 1: drop the dependency closure, keep only the refusal (DW-steward-103-6-1's option (b)). Checked against preflight_ci.py: _step_matches matches a pixi run W step to every lane in _closure(pixi_data, W), which is transitive, so a dependent selected through a task match has its dependencies selected by the same step; discover_paused_lanes marks a lane paused only when it is unselected with the pause and selected without it, so a dependency its dependent's unpaused step matches is never paused. The selection therefore strands a dependency only where nothing in CI runs the lane with it: a lane matched by its command line (coverage-gates.yml:202 runs python scripts/coverage_gates_ci.py), a lane no CI step runs (_outcome's 'no CI counterpart ... (always runs)', which the builder added: pulling in there masks no CI red, but is not CI's behaviour either, so the refusal applies with its own message), and, after the touched cut, a fast-core detector's dependency. Pulling a dependency in there runs locally what CI does not and could turn a CI red into a local green (should-fix 2). On the shipped graph the three lanes with a dependency are matched by pixi run steps, and the command-line-only lanes (eight coverage gates) and the uncovered ones (detectors-ci, docs-map-render-test, docs-gen-test) have none. Result: close_over_dependencies, its wiring, selection.pulled_in and the pulled-in stderr lines removed; the refusal names each stranded lane, why it runs (verdict reason, or the fast-core reason), each missing dependency, what CI does, and the fixes (make the CI step run pixi run <task>, or drop the depends-on; for a fast-core detector, drop it or take the task out of FAST_CORE_TASKS). Should-fix 1: the pixi.toml comment and herald's test_the_coverage_gate_takes_no_install_dependency docstring restated. The four nits (interrupted-record pulled_in, the skipped count, breadth-first attribution, the ignore-ci-pauses row) went with the closure. The story is retitled 'A stranded preflight lane is refused, and pr-preflight-lanes resolves under pixi'; its key becomes 107-1-a-stranded-preflight-lane-is-refused-and-pr-preflight-lanes-resolves-under-pixi (the old backlog key, never on main, dropped through the Tier-3 feed and sprint-ledger-sync --project steward --allow-regression, which named it); the spec file and marshal-policy.toml's "107" entry follow.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-16, Story 107.1's re-review of 42c3ffff5d (FAIL: one should-fix, one nit; scratchpad r107/review.md 'Re-review', probe r107/tools/probe_ignore.py) resolved on the same branch. (should-fix) --ignore-ci-pauses is a fourth way to strand a lane: the selection is read with the pause applied, so a paused lane and the dependency its pixi run step runs with it are both left out, and the flag re-adds only the lanes discover_paused_lanes returns, which need a docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml local_twin row; a dependency with no row stays out, and the refusal said 'CI runs L's command without P ... make the CI step run pixi run L', false for that step. A lane the flag re-added now gets its own kind (STRANDED_PAUSE_IGNORED): its why is its pause ('paused in CI (<switch> since <date>, review_by <date>), re-added by --ignore-ci-pauses'), then '<P> is held back by the same pause, and --ignore-ci-pauses re-adds only the lanes docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml names as a local twin', then 'fix: name <P> as the local twin of its surface's docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml row, or run without --ignore-ci-pauses'. Which lanes the flag re-adds does not change. This is the first review's fourth nit, which the change log had closed as gone with the closure; it outlived it. (nit) A lane no CI step runs has no step to change: its fix line reads 'add a CI step that runs pixi run <task>, or drop <task>'s depends-on'. The four-case list replaces the three-case one in the spec (Intent, Approach, ACs, residual risk, I/O matrix, change log), the Dream entry, Epic 107 / Story 107.1 and the DW-steward-103-6-1 closed: line. Latent today: no local_twin row names a preflight lane.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-17, Story 108.1 (fixes under CAP-167 and CAP-166; no CAP, no flag, no SPEC.md change; closes DW-steward-103-3-2 and DW-steward-101-1-2): (1) estate reconcile's env-matrix generator runs [python, scripts/pixi_env_matrix.py, --update, docs/dreams/pyforge-unifying-strategy.md], but the script takes the Dream only as --dream, so argparse exits 2 (unrecognized arguments) and writes nothing; run_reconcile acts on a generator's exit only for generate-bmad-suite (the scribe branch is a pass), and GATING_CHECKS has no matrix check, so the run reads green. Measured at ab38be642f in a scratch copy with the lock moved: the reconcile argv exits 2 and pixi_env_matrix.py --check still exits 1 after it; --update --dream <path> exits 0 and the check then exits 0. (2) ci.py _row_gated_by_master leaves out kind in (value, retired), but the registry records retirement in state and its schema has no retired kind, so a retired row whose workflow still exists and reads the master carries paused_by_master true, against Story 103.1's contract; probed at ab38be642f with retired workflow, ci-job and ci-step rows and the master true: each drift with paused_by_master true. Design (orchestrating session's brief): the generator passes --update --dream <the Dream>, one constant shared with a new gating check pixi-env-matrix-check (pixi_env_matrix.py --check --dream <the Dream>, a Guild task like the others, after bmad-estate-check); every generator's journal row carries its label (and error: non-zero exit when it fails), and a generator that exits non-zero reds the run with EXIT_RECONCILE_CHECKS (1), its label, exit and command named in the summary, after the other generators, the checks and the spec-surface reconcile have run, as a failed gating check does today (Story 100.3's I/O matrix row for the scribe catalog, which the code never implemented); generate-bmad-suite keeps its early stop and names any other failed generator beside it. _row_gated_by_master returns false for a row _is_retired names (state retired), beside the value-kind and paused-manually tests; the dead kind == retired test goes. The script's usage docstring is corrected to --update --dream / --check --dream. Rejected: a positional Dream argument in pixi_env_matrix.py (the script's interface is --dream; the caller is wrong); stopping the run at the first failed generator (the 100.3 matrix keeps the checks running, and an operator sees every failure in one run); leaving the kind test beside the state test (no registry row or schema value carries kind retired).

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-17, Story 108.1's independent review of 5ed998b0e7 (FAIL: one should-fix, two nits; scratchpad r108/review.md) and the orchestrator's ruling (fix the should-fix inside 108.1, do not only record it), resolved on the same branch with no AC removed and no CAP or key moved. (1, should-fix) The bmad-estate-catalog generator ran [sys.executable, -m, pyforge.scribe.cli, catalog, bmad-estate, --write]; the duty runs as pixi run -e pyforge-steward pyforge steward estate reconcile, and that env has no pyforge.scribe, so it always exited 1 (the 2026-10-14 refresh journal records the row at exit 1 beside env-matrix at exit 2). With a failed generator now redding the run, no reconcile could exit 0 in its own env. Fixed: the generator runs _guild_python(-m, pyforge.scribe.cli, catalog, bmad-estate, --write), the env scribe's own REGENERATE_COMMAND names, as the checks do. Measured in a scratch clone of 5ed998b0e7 with the steward env's Python, the journal also moving pixi and bmad-method: before, bmad-estate-catalog exit 1 (No module named 'pyforge.scribe') and the other seven generators exit 0; after, all eight exit 0, the catalog changes only its Generated date, and bmad_estate_check.py exits 0 after it. Pinned: every generator's argv as a literal list, and a -m generator run with sys.executable must name a module that resolves in the duty's env. Corrected: the spec's residual risk (the env matrix was not the only known failure), the PRD's 2026-10-17 FR-40 sentence (two surfaces, both now met), the spine's AD-1 note, Epic 108 and Story 108.1's text, the DW-steward-103-3-2 closure and the Dream entry. Rejected: adding pyforge-scribe to the pyforge-steward env (a station env grows by a station package to run one command the Guild env already runs). (2, nit) AC2's test asserts the block's lock-sha256 equals the fixture lock's digest directly, beside the script's --check. (3, nit) AC6's test compares the matrix check's argv with a literal list, not the implementation's _guild_python.
